# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1988

## The Problem with the Vyāsa-pūjā Book by Śeṣa dāsa

Alas, today is Sunday, May 1, 1988, the deadline date for submission of the offerings for this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā book. It seems like every year I wait until the very last moment to write my offering and send it in. And, I’m not alone. I hear the BBT office in Los Angeles has been receiving express mail packets and telefaxes from all over the world for the last couple of days! You’d think that after all these years I could at least get my offering in a little ahead of time. After all, isn’t the meditation we record in our offering something we live with every day of our lives? Of course it is, so what’s the problem?!

One problem is that, although we may be deeply motivated by intense feelings for our beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it’s often very hard to express those emotions in words. I’m sure we have all had the experience of seeing a very senior disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda, who has proven his devotion to his spiritual master through years of sincere service, refuse to speak or say very little on Vyāsa-pūjā day. Although words may not be spoken, the emotions are clear and appreciated by everyone in the room. Actually, such occurrences are often the most memorable events of the Vyāsa-pūjā day, and they spring from realization of the tremendous mercy granted to us by Śrīla Prabhupāda. What can you say, what can you do to repay or even acknowledge the gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? Such mixtures of love and devotion are understandably difficult to articulate and are most often only gotten out of us by one thing-deadlines. That’s the problem with the Vyāsa-pūjā book: it not only forces us to crystallize our feelings into written text, but this difficult process must conform to a strict time schedule.

Just as the Vyāsa-pūjā book presents problems for the devotees offering homages to Śrīla Prabhupāda, this annual publication also presents some special book production problems for those of us at the BBT. May 1 is only the beginning for the BBT, and all the late offerings don’t help the situation. For a month and a half the deadlines come quickly. First the offerings, which are often hand-written, have to be deciphered and entered into the computer. Then there is English editing, which is specifically limited to slight grammatical adjustments. We don’t edit the content of Vyāsa-pūjā offerings, because we realize that devotees have the full right to express themselves in front of their spiritual master. But sometimes that takes a lot of patience. It would make it a lot easier for us if devotees steered clear of political or controversial subject matters in their offerings. (This is not a policy statement but a humble request. After all, what controversy is there in the glorification of Śrīla Prabhupāda?) Sanskrit editing, layout, photo editing, and proof-reading follow, but because of late offerings, our usual step-by-step production is often quite jumbled as we attempt to include as many of the late offerings as possible. The printing and shipping arrangements in the production of the Vyāsa-pūjā book are particularly intense because the books have to arrive at the temples by a final deadline, Vyāsa-pūjā day.

Of course, BBT book production has always involved the pressure of adhering to strict deadlines, and nothing exemplifies this more than the 1975 *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* production marathon. Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted seventeen volumes produced in two months! Deadlines! The editors and typesetters worked around the clock, fighting to keep their eyes open and their minds concentrating. The artist awoke from napping only to find their unfinished canvasses missing from their studio and already photographed for printing. Tight deadline scheduling was the only way the miracle Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee wanted was accomplished. The books might not have been perfectly done, but they were done, and that pleased Śrīla Prabhupāda. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s satisfaction is not something comes cheaply, but it’s worth all the endeavor. Just ask any of those press workers who sacrificed so much as Vyāsa-pūjā day approached in 1975. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s satisfaction is the essence of Vyāsa-pūjā day, but it does present us with some problems.

The problem with the Vyāsa-pūjā book that is forces us to be Kṛṣṇa conscious. As individuals, we are forced to be Kṛṣṇa conscious, at least while we are meditating on our offering; and a book publisher, we are responsible for producing a book that captures the Kṛṣṇa conscious moods and expressions that the unspoken essence of Vyāsa-pūjā day. When you think about it, that’s probably the reason Śrīla Prabhupāda put us in deadline situations. Therefore, we pray that, despite all the problems associated with producing a yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book, year after year we will be able to continue to be as burdened, because it is a means for our purification.

Śeṣa dāsa  
North American BBT

## Introduction by Yamunā devī

It has been sixteen years since I attempted to write an homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda on Vyāsa-pūjā day, although he requested his disciples to do so every year. Feeling very inadequate, it was a painful experience for me then, and remains so today.

In the last five thousand years, millions of great Vaiṣṇava have pondered the significance of Vyāsa-pūjā. Indeed, scholarly treatises and philosophical commentaries have been devoted to this profound subject matter. In its simplest context Vyāsa-pūjā is the occasion for honoring the *guru* on the day of his appearance. Reflecting on the unique attributes, potency and purity of the *guru*, a disciple attempts to pay homage to the *guru.* Further, it is a day for glorifying the original spiritual master, Śrīla Vyāsadeva; the disciplic succession; and the rich attributes of Vaiṣṇava thought, culture, and history.

My first Vyāsa-pūjā experience with Śrīla Prabhupāda was in 1968 at a Montreal stopover enroute to London. ISKCON was in its infant second year, with only a handful of initiated disciples and established temples. Though Śrīla Prabhupāda had informed us that there were Vaiṣṇava festivals to celebrate every day of the year, only four or six were observed on a grand scale, and the back-to-back celebrations of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-janmāṣṭamī and Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā were the high point of the year.

Four non-stop Vaiṣṇava activities-chanting , dancing, feasting, and scriptural discussions-became the stage for the Vyāsa-pūjā drama. Śrīla Prabhupāda was the director of the joyous event, personally supervising devotees in preliminary preparations-shopping, cleaning, banking, decorating, cooking and advertising. Teaching by example, Śrīla Prabhupāda joined us in all of these activities. Observing him engage in loving service to his spiritual master while teaching us how to serve him gave me my first taste of the essence of Vyāsa-pūjā.

Śrīla Prabhupāda requested each disciple and guest “to speak something about the spiritual master.” I remember speaking in Canto One, Chapter 2, text 6of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam: sa vai puṁsāṁ paro dharmo yato bhaktir adhoksaje,* telling Śrīla Prabhupāda that is seemed impossible to attain the qualities of being unmotivated by seeing such qualities personified in him, it gave me hope from which to aspire to them.

The tradition of glorifying the spiritual master on Vyāsa-pūjā is necessary because it reminds us of our eternal indebtedness at his lotus feet.

Yamunā devī

## Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the G.B.C.

## Agrāṇī Swami

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Once again, devotees from all parts of the world are observing the anniversary of your appearance within this world. You are the complete manifestation of the Lord Caitanya’s mercy, specifically empowered by Him to expand what He Himself initiated five hundred years ago: His glorious *saṅkīrtana* movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This day marks the most sacred and blissful event of the entire year and will be celebrated with much discussion of your fascinating pastimes and qualities. I am looking forward with great eagerness to participate in these ecstatic discussions.

You are the most perfect transcendental personality! From the very beginning of your life, you were serving Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Lord Jagannātha with pure devotion. Never once in your life did you deviate from religious principles, nor did you ever once forget Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You are the most perfect disciple of your spiritual master. Since first receiving the sacred order from Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura to deliver the teachings of Lord Caitanya to the English speaking world, you sought tirelessly to fulfill it. You sacrificed the comforts of home and family, and finally, at a time in life when other devotees would take to retirement in a holy place, you sacrificed the sublime peace in residing in Vṛndāvana-dhāma to bring fortune to the godforsaken West. You are the greatest scholar and author of transcendental literature. You gave the world an unsurpassed treasure of the most pertinent and elevated Vedic literatures such as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā, The Nectar of Devotion*, and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* These literatures provide all conditioned souls the opportunity to return to their constitutional position of eternal, blissful life. Already these potent books have left an indelible impression on the hearts of all who have been exposed to them, and they form the standard of spiritual practice to endure for the next ten thousand years. You are the most bold and uncompromising preacher of Śrī Caitanya’s philosophy, especially with regards to “kicking on the faces” of the high priests of today’s atheistic society, the scientists and impersonalist philosophers. Yet your manner is so pure and free from false ego and enmity that you win the affection of all. You are the most powerful, charismatic person. Wherever you go, being the most perfect *guru* and representative of Kṛṣṇa, you are the center of all admiring attention. You so ingeniously organized Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement that every living entity could find his place in it. Indeed, it is said that you “built a house in which the whole world can live” in blissful Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have made realizable the predictions of Lord Nityānanda and Bhaktivinoda Thākura—that the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa would liberate all conditioned souls of the world. Your profound love for Kṛṣṇa is such that during the final stage of your manifestation in this world, when your physical body was harassed by disease and discomfort, you remained undisturbed by your bodily condition. You could think only of pleasing Kṛṣṇa by surrendering to His plan, in life or death, happiness or distress. With complete dependence upon Kṛṣṇa, you faced death with the courage of a lion.

There is no end to your glorious characteristics and activities, and today, above everything else, grateful devotees everywhere will be absorbed in hearing and chanting them in ecstatic jubilation. From a more personal perspective, however, there is something very special about you which is above and beyond everything mentioned hereinbefore. The most wonderful thing about you, in my eyes, is that it is you who have made my life fortunate. You taught me about the soul and Kṛṣṇa and instructed me in principles of intelligent human behavior. You brought me into disciplic succession and gave to me the supreme boon of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. And you continue to bestow your mercy upon me by carefully protecting and maintaining me in spiritual life. Although I am undeserving and insignificant, you have kindly blessed me with an invaluable spiritual treasure. In my life, which was previously devoid of anything beneficial, I am now in possession of many wonderful things, and they have all been given to me by you! Remembrance of this is what inspires me most to become your loving, dedicated servant. In life I wish for only one thing - to give myself unconditionally to serving Lord Caitanya’s wonderful *saṅkīrtana* movement, for I know that is what pleases you best. Although I am unfit, I daily pray to Kṛṣṇa that I never forget what you have done for me, and that my love for you may grow forever stronger, for you are everything to me.

*Praying to remain always at your lotus feet,*  
**Agrāṇī Swami**

## Balavanta Das

Ninety-two years since you kindly appeared,  
This passage of time, a life fully divine,  
A munificent plan to elevate man,  
God’s servant, alone you set out from home.

Wandering the earth, the land of your birth  
Neither consoled nor extolled you, so  
With your mind firm, your blessed glance turned  
To the noise and the din, the reveling sin  
Of the new world.

You there planted the seed, a most benevolent deed  
Of unfettered devotion and holy emotion,  
And from where you stood, you saw it was good,  
You knew what some missed, this mission was blessed.

Then  
Amid our deepening fear that we’d soon disappear  
One Vṛndāvana day you had your own way  
Now  
Though matter transcended and freely ascended,  
You still easily reach us, continually teach us.

As clearly foretold, ISKCON soon will unfold  
Unpretentiously, born like Vāmana’s spiritual form,  
Relentless advancement, the drama’s enchantment,  
Each juncture a lesson, unfailing progression.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Balavanta Das

## Bhakti Cāru Swami

**The Final Instruction**

Surrounded by devotees  
In Vṛndāvana  
You were preparing to depart.  
You lay in your bed  
In silent meditation  
With Kṛṣṇa playing in your heart.  
The room was filled  
With the fragrant smoke  
Of frankincense  
And sandalwood pulp.  
Your body was fragrant  
With some divine perfume  
From the spiritual sky.  
Devotees sang softly the holy name  
Accompanied only by a small kartal.  
But the sound penetrated  
Even the stonelike hearts.  
Satsvarūpa Mahārāja entered with Madhudviṣa,  
Who left the movement with a girl.  
Your meditation broke, and you spoke  
Like a father concerned about his prodigal son,  
“You got married, breaking your vows. So what?  
Still you can serve.”  
Pointing at the householder devotees in the room  
You said, “Look at them.  
They are also married.  
So you also can come back and stay  
In the association of devotees.  
No matter whatever happens,  
Don’t ever leave ISKCON.”  
You repeated—  
“No matter what happens,  
don’t ever leave ISKCON.”  
I visualize the material nature  
An abysmal ocean,  
The bodies swept away  
In its dangerous currents.  
No one can ever swim those waves,  
What to speak of the deadly acquatics  
That impatiently wait for their prey.

On that ocean there is a ship  
Sailing smoothly with the strong wind  
Of Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra in its sail.  
The best captain, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Is steering that ship.  
Devotees blissfully chant and dance  
On the deck.  
A flag on the mast  
Proudly bears the insignia “ISKCON”  
In the whorl of a golden lotus.  
Who will ever want to leave that shelter?

\* \* \*

In the middle of the night  
The world is asleep.  
Only the sound of the holy name  
Glides through the air of Vṛndāvana  
From some distant place.  
You try to translate  
In spite of your weak health.  
You did not eat anything for weeks.  
Waiting to serve you, I watch.  
You can’t even lift the hand-set  
Of your Dictaphone.  
My heart was heavy.  
Not due to my love for you,  
But because someone had hurt my pride.  
I do not like to fight,  
But passionate encounters hurt me.  
I brood over some insignificant happenings.  
Exhausted, you lay down.  
I walked over to your bed  
To massage your feet,  
Not out of my unalloyed love for you,  
But out of some dry sense of duty.  
My stonelike heart is still heavy from the wound.  
Obsessed with my own feelings,  
I do not appreciate your compassion  
For all and your suffering  
Due to our sins.  
Mechanically I massage your feet.  
You can understand  
What goes on in our hearts.  
Breaking the silence, you say softly,  
“Just offer this life to Kṛṣṇa.”  
The veil of Māyā is lifted,  
And my heart breaks, and  
Tears come streaming down my eyes.  
“I love you, I love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!” I cry.  
“If you love me,”  
you replied,  
“then cooperate with them  
who also love me.”  
I resolve in my heart,  
“I will, I will, Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

Bhakti Cāru Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*Oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāyjana-śalakayā*  
*Cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

Most merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda:

By your divine blessings I spent a few days in New Dvārakā Dhāma. Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rukmini-Dwaṛakādhīśa, Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Jagannātha, Baladeva and Subhadrā, your beloved objects of worship, ever remain showering Their divine blessings like the rays of *śarat-pūrṇimā,* cool and blissful, upon every sincere seeker of the Absolute Truth. This is the wonderful gift of Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda. New Dvārakā Dhāma reminds me of the pastimes of Your Divine Grace and divine instructions fresh like the morning dew.

The trees and the footpath in Cheviot Hills are still remembering Your Divine Grace. I could hear silently the evolution dialogue, and the crying trees are still witnessing the powerful voice of Your Divine Grace: “Darwin’s forefathers were monkeys, but my forefathers were kings and saints.” The restless waves in Venice Beach and the sands on which you walked are missing greatly the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace. The little sandpipers seemed to have departed from Venice Beach because Your Divine Grace stopped coming. You spoke about the flock of pelicans flying together early in the morning at 6:30, reminding that in the *saṅkīrtana* movement each one of us must be strong so that we can fly together like the pelicans. Those fortunate souls seemed to have gone to associate with Your Divine Grace, since Your Divine Grace departed to Goloka-dhāma to serve Śrī Golokanātha.

The quartz crystal on your work table reminds me of that very special moment. Your room reminds me of your most dedicated and sincere servant, Śrī Rāmānanda dāsa. He is not known by many. However, he was most enthusiastic in scientific preaching and serving your divine lotus feet. Only by his mercy and friendship was this most lowly servant brought to Your Divine Grace. He has already gone to serve you in that world.

I could hear the silent voice of your beautiful songs that you sang in your studio. The devotional zeal and the intensity of your dedication to broadcast the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead will always remain unparalleled in the history of the Vaiṣṇava culture throughout the three worlds. The forceful sounds of your preaching to Professor Benford and Professor Tiwari on your garden and room can still be heard with reverberating echoes. The plan of your divine mission that you chalked out from New Dvārakā Dhāma is expanding in all the eight directions. Many wonderful devotees are helping me in finishing our book, *Synthesis of Science and Religion.* I am grateful to all of them. Your divine presence is always felt here.

The Vyāsāsana from which you transmitted the message of the love of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is still holding Your Divine Grace strong and firm. On this most important day kindly bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so that I can carry out your divine order.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhaktitīrtha Swami

*Tvaṁ naḥ saandarśito dhātrā*  
*Dustaraṁ nistitṛṣatām*  
*Kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*Karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of Providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

If I love you, I will love your disciples and your granddisciples.  
If I love you, I will not give in to sense gratification.  
If I love you, I will not give my *māyā* to others.  
If I love you, I will not let others stagnate me by their *māyā.*  
If I love you, I will have compassion for all conditioned souls.  
If I love you, I will see myself as the most conditioned.  
If I love you, I will be steady in happiness or distress.  
If I love you, I will always see myself as nothing more than a student, although I will teach on your behalf.  
If I love you, I will always remember that I am a neophyte disciple, although I will be a *guru* on your behalf.  
If I love you, I will always learn tremendously from those whom I agree with, and even more from those I disagree with.  
If I love you, I will not give others orders that I am not willing to follow myself.  
If I love you, other’s misfortune I will see as my own.  
If I love you, every time a devotee leaves the society or gives up his Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I will see it as my fault.  
If I love you, I will be a catalyst for helping to bring all members of our family back together.  
If I love you, I will help make secure arrangements materially and spiritually for all those devotees who have or had given their lives for this mission.  
If I really loved you, I would be so happy all of the time thinking about how fortunate I am that you picked me up from the lap of *māyā* that there would be no opportunity for depression.  
If I really loved you, associating with the Six Gosvāmīs would be a natural experience.  
If I really loved you, summoning any extraterrestrial, demigod, or great *ācārya* to help this mission and this planet would be a joyful duty.  
If I really loved you, contacting you would be easier than calling someone on the telephone.  
If I really loved you, conscious communication with the Supersoul would be a moment-to-moment affair.  
If I really loved you, by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, I would experience that the name Kṛṣṇa is nondifferent than Kṛṣṇa.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we wonder who out there really loves you. To love you is the most powerful thing, as you are Kṛṣṇa’s confidential servant. As we love you, we are loved. I can surely say that I do not have unconditional love. THE TIME IS NOW! Enough of this theoretical devotional service. Our devotees are becoming more disappointed and confused. Let us become genuine lovers of Śrīla Prabhupāda….But can we love Śrīla Prabhupāda without loving each other? I do not think so. Please forgive me, for I am the greatest offender, as lust, greed, and selfishness are my daily partners, and love, compassion, and selfless are foreign to me.

Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us catch up with you; allow us to fall madly in love with you.

*Aspiring to become a true lover,*  
Bhaktitīrtha Swami

## Dānavīr dāsa vanacārī

My dear beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glory to your divine appearance in this world, which has raised the dead and given them life anew. You have taught us to seek the blessings of the previous *ācāryas,* without caring for popular opinion. Yet because the Vaiṣṇava develop all wonderful qualities, they are adored by the ruffians as well as the gentle.

Some bewildered souls intimate that ISKCON needs something beyond your nectarean instructions in order to flourish. Some say that everything is ISKCON except ISKCON——sounding similar to the Māyāvādīs who claim God to be everywhere except in the temple. Some abandon ISKCON to begin their own world movements and even try to imitate your organizational policies.

It is your causeless mercy that you allow them to do these things while disobeying your orders. This shows how much you love your disciples in particular, as well as living entities in general. We pray that we may follow in your footsteps by acting kindly to them while knowing of their unfortunate position and their ultimate failure.

As far as we are concerned, we desire to strive for pleasing you by preaching vigorously as you requested. In this endeavor we are very confident that we will get the full mercy of the Pañca-tattva and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, because our longing is to glorify you. In Berkeley, you once told a group of temple presidents, “If you become glorious, that will be my glory.” I understand that to mean that we should become pure, enthusiastic, successful preachers of the Lord’s message as received through your lotus mouth. In this way you will be satisfied just as the spiritual father is always happy to see his loyal sons prosper.

My humble request of this most auspicious day is that you kindly bestow your faithful followers with added *bhakti-śakti* to dispel all the dark clouds of illusion, confusion, and delusion conjured by the magic of Māyā and her assistants. Then everyone, both devotees and others, will dance in ecstasy under your ISKCON banner.

We need not compromise to accommodate the “new age” ideas; rather, they shall be elevated to the transcendental ageless realm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by united ISKCON. We shall now, by your mercy, that sincere followers of Your Divine Grace outshine all others. The more I meditate on your maxims “Books are the basis, purity is the force, preaching is the essence, and utility is the principle,” the more I am convinced that we must return to this simple yet sublime formula for complete success. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ.*

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing this wretched rascal to continue in your blissful devotional service.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Dānavīr dāsa vanācarī

## Gaṇapati Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my worthless obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. You write in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (9.5.20*) vaiṣṇavera kriyā mudrā vijñeha nā bujhāya:* “Even the most learned person cannot understand the value of a Vaiṣṇava. There is always a possibility for so-called *jñānīs* and *yogīs* to be mistaken when studying the character of a Vaiṣṇava. A Vaiṣṇava can be understood by how much he is favored by the Supreme Personality of Godhead in terms of his inconceivable activities.”

It is becoming increasingly obvious to this fallen soul that I don’t understand you at all, Śrīla Prabhupāda. That, however, should be no surprise, since you are a “Vaikuṇṭha man,” an eternal resident of the spiritual abode, and I am a conditioned inmate of this material prison house, and have been since time immemorial. Just as Lord Brahmā or Indra may have, an occasion, overestimated their ability to ascertain the limitless potency of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, so I have been foolish enough to assume that I could penetrate the transcendental nature of your teachings and activities with my mundane mind. But the more I attempt this, the more I feel like Dr. Frog. How can my froglike brain ever hope to fathom Your Divine Grace’s oceanlike compassion, tolerance, and wisdom? My flimsy speculations are repeatedly foiled by your impenetrable divine innocence. You write your Bhaktivedanta purports such that anyone who reads them with a challenging attitude is deluded by a myriad of (apparent) contradictions. Just as Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kaviraja Gosvami presented the intimate *līlā* of Caitanya Mahāprabhu in consideration of both spiritual and mundane audiences, so your purports explicitly reveal the glories of the Lord for the humble and meek, while remaining indecipherable to the envious. My realization of your exalted instructions is very shallow and superficial. But just as Lord Kṛṣṇa inspired Arjuna to rise to a higher level of intelligence in order to satisfy conflicting considerations in the case of Aśvatthāmā’s crime and punishment, so you are training me to escape the bounds of my narrow conceptions in order to accommodate the variegated perspectives that you propound.

You describe the Lord as He who can adjust all opposing elements. For Him, “yes” can be “no”and “no”can be “yes”. Desirable qualities like mercy and even supposed negative qualities like anger originate and abide in their pure form in His absolute self. Thus, God is “all-good.” Similarly, you, Śrī *Guru*deva, are “always right” because you are the Lord’s pure devotee. Because of your unalloyed love for Kṛṣṇa, He had given you full freedom to act on His behalf, and thus you cannot in any way displease Him. A wonderful example of how you are always right even when giving opposite instructions was your dealing with the two *sannyāsīs* in Vṛndāvana, telling one to manage the project forever, while simultaneously forbidding the other to stay for more than three days.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please protect me from merely trying to imitate you, lest I become like the veterinarian’s apprentice who simply created havoc by imitating his teacher’s treatment of hammering on the swollen neck of a horse. Help me to abandon the impossible task of fully comprehending you, of categorizing you, of expecting you to confirm to my limited and perverted standards. Let me rather be content just to act for your sweet satisfaction. In a *Bhāgavatam* purport Your Divine Grace writes: “It is not within the power of one’s small brain to comprehend (how the Lord’s inconceivable potencies act in the action and reaction of this material world); unless one accepts the inconceivable power and energy of the Lord, one cannot make any progress.” (*Bhāg.* 4.11.18) Also, “No one can know You, because You are unlimited. It is best not to try to know You by unnecessary mental speculation. Rather, kindly bless us so that we can worship You with causeless devotional service.” (*Bhāg.* 3.13.45) Similarly, kindly allow me to have full faith in your divine directives and, with a pacified mind and energetic body, worship you with causeless devotional service. Help me to share your enlivening association, in the form of your books and literatures, with all human beings. Let me, furthermore, assist rather than inhibit them in their natural development of faith in and enthusiasm to assist you in your mission. As my spiritual father, you have given me a genuine, eternal life of devotional service. A son’s duty is not only to make the father the source of supply for all one’s needs, but also, when he is grown up, to render service unto him. Please help me to become a mature and useful son.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Girirāja Swami

“Even though one may be an incarnation of Kṛṣṇa, or especially empowered by Him, he should not advertise that he is an incarnation. People will automatically accept the real truth in due course of time. “(*Bhāg.* 4.22.5, purport)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my eternal prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, and boundless mercy upon the fallen souls.

When you were personally present, we could not understand your actual position. We thought of you as a spiritual master. But as you said of your own *Guru* Mahārāja, “He was no ordinary spiritual master. He saved me.” You have saved thousands of us—including me.

“There are many hundreds and thousands of devotees of Lord Caitanya among whom there are no special symptoms, but when a devotee of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu functions with specific prowess, he displays the feature called *āveśa.* Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu personally spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and He advised all the inhabitants of Bhārata-varṣa to take up His cult and preach it all over the world. The visible bodily symptoms of devotees who follow such instructions are called *āveśa…*The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* states that in this age of Kali the only spiritual function is to broadcast the holy name of the Lord, but this function can be performed only by one who is actually empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. The process by which a devotee is thus empowered is called *āveśa*, or sometimes it is called *śaktyāveśa.” (Cc. Ādi* 10.59, purport)

We pray that you will grant us the intelligence and strength to realize your actual position, to act in our proper relationship with you, and to broadcast your transcendental glories throughout the entire world, by our words and deeds.

*Your unworthy yet hopeful, servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Gour Govind Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Again, whenever I try to glorify you, I find myself wholly inadequate to express my debt of gratitude to you. It has been fourteen years since you picked me up out of the darkest region of material existence and put in my heart the seed of devotional service by your divine instruction and the divine message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With what can I repay you?

In 1977, out of your causeless mercy you came to Orissa, Bhubaneswar, and stayed here for sixteen days in a very humble mud hut I had erected on the land donated to you, without expressing any sort of discomfort. At that time this place was an out -of -the -way place, without any modern amenities. Still, very gladly you stayed here, saying, “What Kṛṣṇa has arranged for me is very good for me.” I was going to arrange the State Guesthouse for your stay, but you refused to stay there. You are so merciful. Very wonderfully you teach us by your actions. This is the real Vaiṣṇava *guru’s* way of teaching. By your actions you teach us to accept everything as the arrangement of Lord Kṛṣṇa. In spite of all opposition at that time, you were so kind to lay the foundation stone of this temple in the auspicious day of the appearance day of Lord Nityānanda. And during your stay here you had whispered in my ears, “Gour Govinda, this will be one of the best temples in the world. You should stay here and see this project is done. You stay here, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, translate my books in Oriya, and publish them and preach….” You have said many things to me. Still these words are resounding in my ears. Those words of yours are my most valuable asset. With this asset I am still living and staying here, tolerating many impediments, obstructions (to fulfill your desire), which are coming like thunderbolts to me. Please give me strength to withstand it. I am an unworthy fellow, unqualified person. Only your causeless mercy is required.

*Yogyatā vicāre kichu nahi mora, toṁāra karunā sāra*

It is some eleven years that have passed since you have laid the foundation stone of this temple. But still the project remains incomplete. This has been a great shock to me, because I have not been able to give you pleasure by fulfilling your desire here. I find myself very, very incapable and unqualified to do it. Again since your disappearance from this material world, I feel the pangs of your separation and feel myself very weak to withstand Kali’s obstructions. Please always shower your causeless mercy upon this unworthy servant of yours, because your mercy is my only strength. If I will be devoid of that strength, how can I overcome the obstacles put forth by material nature? This is my only prayer: Please accept me as your eternal servant and shower your causeless mercy on me. I am your eternal disciple. Please inflict discipline on me always, so I will be able to serve you; otherwise it is not possible.

If someone wants to be successful in rendering service to a Vaiṣṇava or *guru,* he should obtain the mercy of the Vaiṣṇava *guru.* Citing Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thākura’s words, I may say, *āmi ta durbhāgā ati Vaiṣṇava nā cina / more kṛpā karibena Vaiṣṇava āpani.* I am very, very unfortunate. I cannot recognize a Vaiṣṇava. Please shower your mercy on me, because you are a Vaiṣṇava.

Again, citing the words of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Thākura, I offer my concluding prayers:

*kī rūpe paiba sevā muni durācār*  
*śrīguru vaiṣṇave rati nā haila āmār.*  
*māyāre kariya jayachādān nā jāya,*  
*sādhu-guru kṛpā vinā nāhika upāya,*  
*adoṣa-daraśī prabhho patita uddhār,*  
*ei bāra narottame karaha nistār.*

I am a very, very sinful person, *durācāra.* I have no Vaiṣṇava *sadācāra.* So how can I get the opportunity to serve a Vaiṣṇava *guru?* I am so weak that I have no strength to overcome Māyā. Only the mercy of *sādhu-guru* is required. I have many faults; I have been committing many offenses. I am a very, very fallen soul. But you are *adoṣa-darśī* and *patita udhāre.* You are so merciful that you always excuse the offenses, and you have come here to deliver the fallen souls like me. This time, please shower your causeless mercy on me and deliver me, and thereby I will be able to render pleasing service to you; otherwise I have no other way.

*With this prayer at your lotus feet / remain your eternal unworthy servant,*  
Gour Govinda Swami

## Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa

*Oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāyjana-śalakayā*  
*Cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Harassed by uncontrolled senses and haunted by mental speculation, I have wandered abjectly from one body to another since time immemorial. Despite my complete lack of redeeming qualities, you have inexplicably bestowed upon me the rarest of opportunities, that of service to the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

You have adopted me as one of your sons and daughters and engaged me as a servant of the movement which you struggled so diligently to found, nurture, and protect. Your great legacy is your books and the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, which you predicated upon them. On several occasions you stated that even you like to study your own books. You took equal pleasure in declaring that you yourself are also a member of ISKCON.

As a devoted follower of yours, let me renew my pledge to live by your divine instructions and remain in the association of those who propagate your sacred mission through ISKCON. On this holy day of your appearance, as I contemplate anew what it means to be your follower, let me eschew the self-indulgence and superficiality which have been the bane of my conditional existence. Let me become fully mindful of the solemn purposes for which you wrote your transcendental literatures and conceived your unique society. Dear spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, mold me into the kind of disciple and member of ISKCON who may one day have reason to believe that he has satisfied you through his humble service.

*Your menial servant,*  
Guru-Gaurāṅga dāsa

## Harikeśa Swami

My dear eternal master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my most humble obeisances thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Lordship, the creator and source of sustenance of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to thank you for all that you have done for me. You have lifted me out of the darkest illusion of ignorance and made me your servant. How can I ever repay you? I can simply offer you my heart and soul, birth after birth.

I want to offer you praise just befitting your exalted position on this most special day of the year, but I do not feel especially qualified to do so. Despite this, I shall try to render my feelings into words for your satisfaction.

At last year’s Vyāsa-pūjā festival, during the reading of our offerings to you, it was discovered that very few preparations had been made for the feast, for hardly anyone was cooking for you. Somehow I felt inspired to try and save the situation and went into the kitchen to cook. I must admit that it felt very good. I was very happy about the thought of cooking for you again.

I also managed to bring down another five or seven of your disciples to cook as well, and somehow or other, somewhat against my will, I was appointed the manager of the cooking, and thus I was not cooking personally but organizing the facilities to allow the others to cook faster. Although I did not like that so much as cooking for you, still it was blissful in its own way. After all, we are working for your pleasure, not our own. If you are satisfied, then without a doubt we will automatically be satisfied. Our cooking marathon must have been what you wanted, for within ninety minutes your cooks prepared sixty-five preparations, and they were, by your mercy, actually good.

There is nothing nicer than working together with many devotees in a marathon for your pleasure, even if it is something simple. Just getting the opportunity to work hard and fast together, with a certain feeling of helpless desperation while trying to accomplish something that seemingly cannot be accomplished, somehow creates a bond between devotees that cannot be compared to anything within this world. But due to the influence of time, we forget…….

Personally, I best relate to your service in these terms. I like to be a part of a group of devotees working extremely hard to fulfill your desires and offer the fruits of our labor at your lotus feet. The results of the various book distribution marathons that take place during the year are an example of such ecstatic opportunities to serve you. Somehow I cannot relate to prolonged meetings, innumerable administrative resolutions, and parliamentary formalities. I am at fault, but I think I am not qualified for these things. I just want to work hard, inspiring, in whatever way I can, the creation of those basic things that you want and which please you.

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive me, for although I am personally worthless and unqualified, I want to ask of you a benediction. Generally on these Vyāsa-pūjā days we are meant to give you something, usually some heartfelt words of glorification, flowers, a feast, and a gift. I like to do that, too, but somehow, without my having that real taste which comes as a result of capturing your mercy, such offerings do not entirely reflect the mood that Your Lordship instilled in us while you were present. I am dried up and useless, lacking any understanding of spiritual life, and therefore I am daring to beg you for some mercy.

I fear that without your direct inspiration and guidance, our ISKCON movement may wander into areas of activity which somehow do not deliver the taste for which we are always hankering. Actually you already know this very well. Be that as it may, since we are personalists, and I have no one else to depend on than you, I would like to ask you for a benediction.

I have nothing to offer you in return but my service. You already have this, and therefore what more can I give you for your pleasure? However, being impoverished due to conditioned miserliness and impious selfish activities, I will ask you to increase the stock of your mercy upon us all so that we may exponentially increase our offerings to you throughout the years.

My request is as follows: Please enter our hearts, purify them of all our material contaminations, remain there as brilliant as the sun, remind us always of the real point of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness mission, and engage us, as only you know how, in those services which will bring us together in helpless desperation while working very hard to accomplish that which you want us to do.

I am specifically requesting you to grant us that service which can engage us in such an intense concentration of preaching activity that we will transform this miserable material world into Vaikuṇṭha.

Everywhere I look I see the material modes of nature fully in control of dull, materialistically -mined people who are totally blind to their own self-interest. The lower modes of passion and ignorance rule unchallenged from any quarter. Māyā is laughing in our faces as we stumble on the battlefield, but beyond our vision she is also crying, as she is a servant of the Lord who is waiting for us to get inspired to hurl the Kṛṣṇa conscious *śastras* at her illusory creations.

Seeing such a situation, and knowing our own weaknesses, I am requesting you to please personally intervene. We cannot change this world on our own. Either something is wrong and we are unable, or we were never able, and you are simply waiting for us to surrender fully unto your lotus feet. Only you were able to cut through the maze of material ignorance and make the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement powerful throughout the world. We acted simply as your messengers, carrying your books to every person who would hear us.

Please give us the power to again illuminate the darkness of this age of Kali, as it was done before, during your time with us. Please give us the strength to extinguish this blazing fire of material existence by showering the world with your literatures, which are filled with sublime words meant to cause a revolution in the hearts of the conditioned souls. These words are perfectly composed with all the Supreme Lord’s energy required to turn the material civilization from its course of self-destruction.

Please do something to take this mission take birth again as you made it appear years ago. As the embodied soul never factually dies or grows old, but just changes his body to reappear again in a new one under the direction of the law of *karma,* similarly the mission you installed never grows old and cannot die, and by your order it must appear for the sake of the world.

I can offer you nothing since I am nothing. I can only offer you my services, which are also worth only as much as you make them. I am your tool; use me as you like. We are all your tools, your humble slaves. Please do not neglect us. Make us a miracle. You did it before; you can do it again if you so please.

I am waiting your reply with eager anticipation. I see no other hope.

I hope that you are well and that your preaching mission in other regions has already started. We all are confident that wherever your preaching mission appears, morality, virtue, cleanliness, mercy, austerity, truthfulness, and all forms of spiritual potency will automatically appear as well, to make it a grand success.

*Your eternal dog who feels very happy to have you as his master,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Harivilāsa dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I depend entirely on your mercy and that of your devotees. As I flounder in my life. I feel more and more helpless and dependent on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Without your mercy, my spiritual life is finished. Therefore, I am very concerned about following your instructions and not deviating. Although I am weak personally, I have full faith in you and everything you have offered to the world, especially your books, the association of devotees in ISKCON, temple Deity worship, street *saṅkīrtana.* Just a as a newborn infant’s body is made from the body of its mother, your mercy had made me alive and somehow active in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your spiritual instruction is the only real asset in my life. If I love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then I will be abjectly bankrupted.

I pray you permit me to continue to the end of this life and all the future lives to remain your helpless disciple. I really “need” your expert guidance and Kṛṣṇa conscious vision. I feel the “need” more acutely than my need to breathe, because my breathing has no value unless I have your unique direction. Beside you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no one who can fulfill the desires of your *Guru* Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura, to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. If I simply follow you, without trying to imitate or jump over you, then there is hope for the future.

Please bless me to feel ashamed of my sinful desires and activities. Give me the conviction to never lose faith in chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in the association of your devotees. Enlighten me to properly respect all others so that I can chant in ecstasy and can have a faint hope that one day my offenses will cease. I am spiritually and materially destitute. Please keep me close to you out of pity. Teach me to love the association of devotees and to remain neutral to the urges of my senses and cheating mind. Keep me close to you and teach me always to serve you. I have nothing of value in my character. Your mercy is essential to me.

Without your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have no reason to maintain my life, not even for one second.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Harivilāsa dāsa

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvati Gosvami. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya-deva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda once emphasized that love begins with obedience. Love for Kṛṣṇa grows through obedience to the merciful command of the spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda, the towering founder-*ācārya* of Lord Caitanya’s own ISKCON, has given us a simple, sublime program. It is perfect for our own Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and it is perfect for preaching powerfully to the lost souls of this age. And we must simply obey him.

This is my understanding of all things, and I pray at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda to increase and strengthen this transcendental knowledge, and to thus fix me forever as a truly humble servant of His Divine Grace. There is nothing more or greater to desire. And to achieve such mercy is the greatest of all acquisitions.

*His eternal servant,*  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadish Goswami

**You Gave Us Everything**

Unless we work for your desire  
Our strength will be mundane,  
And in that case, before too long,  
We’re sure to see it strain.

Whate’er responsibility  
He wants us to accept  
Requires a simultaneous  
Abiding spiritual debt.

In spite of this you’re using me  
To carry out your plan,  
By this you’re proving certainly  
The pow’r of your command.

I see no other reason for  
Your hanging on to me,  
But that I have firm faith; you gave  
Us everything we need.

Jagadish Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All Glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda had brought the world what no one has given before. He has carries the transcendental mercy of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaura all over the world and fulfilled the predictions of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrīla Prabhupāda has fulfilled the predictions that Jagannātha temples would be established all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda is directly the external manifestation representing Their Lords Nityānanda-Gaurāṅga!

Lord Nityānanda Prabhu predicted that in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur a magnificent temple would be built which would broadcast the eternal devotional service of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu around the world:

*Eka adbhuta mandir hoibe prakāśa*  
*Gaurāṅga-nityasevā hoibe bikāśa*

Śrīla Prabhupāda has instructed his followers to construct the most attractive temple in the world at Śrīdhāma Māyāpur, and to establish a spiritual city there as well. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s every deed is a living representation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s desires. Following His Divine Grace’s instructions directly satisfies Lord Kṛṣṇa’s desires. The individual success of each devotee in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, as well as the collective progress of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, all depends on how effectively we implement Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions.

I personally express my offering of Vyāsa-pūjā to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda with all respect and gratitude. Śrīla Prabhupāda has picked this fallen soul out of the ocean of illusion and personally engaged me in devotional service out of his causeless mercy. Through having granted this most fallen soul His Divine Grace’s personal association, Śrīla Prabhupāda gave me the greatest blessing and opportunity to achieve Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this life. Although I don’t find any quality in me that qualifies me for the mercy of His Divine Grace, still he is such a magnanimous master that he has accepted me as his personal servant. Moreover, Śrīla Prabhupāda has personally engaged this insignificant *jīva* in his confidential service in developing the kingdom of God, at Śrīdham Māyāpur, ISKCON’s world headquarters. Actually, if I were to try to express all the mercy that Śrīla Prabhupāda has bestowed on me I could never complete the list. What to speak of all this mercy, even a drop of His Divine Grace’s mercy is more than enough to overflood the universe!

Yet in spite of receiving countless opportunities and incalculable mercy from His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I am the most unfortunate person due to my own faults. My heart is hard like a stone. I’m most inconsiderate, grossly ungrateful, possessed with false pride, and I have a materialistic mind. I am a source of anxiety to others, causing them incalculable problems, without solution. In short, far from being able to repay my great debt to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I remain a perpetual liability. I am not able to offer His Divine Grace anything suitable in return for his unlimited causeless mercy to me. Finding myself in this hopeless condition, I simply cry. I have nothing to hold on to, nothing to help me, except the causeless mercy of His Divine Grace. Therefore, for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s satisfaction, I cling to his lotus feet through devotional service, always hoping for his causeless mercy.

However, although I am unqualified and incapable, I have no alternative but to try to offer my devotional service for the satisfaction of His Divine Grace. My hope is that as Śrīla Prabhupāda is the most merciful, when he sees I am trying to serve him, even though I am not able to do so properly, he may grant a drop of his mercy unto me. I have no other hope except the causeless mercy of Śrīla Prabhupada.

As a disciple it is certainly my duty to take up the burden from my spiritual master and work to further the mission of His Divine Grace. Although by nature I am weak, I gain strength from Vaiṣṇava association. However it is my bad fortune that I am frequently bereft of association of Vaiṣṇava, I am not qualified to associate properly with them and gain their favor. Nevertheless, since Vaiṣṇava are desire trees and compassionate by nature, I humbly pray to all the Vaiṣṇava to bless me—that I may be able to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda in the association of devotees without interruption until the last breath of this body, and eternally thereafter.

Alone I am not able to fulfill the desires of Śrīla Prabhupāda properly. To fulfill Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire for establishing Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in every town and village, the cooperation of many devotees is certainly required. May I always be able to place personal material considerations aside, and follow the instructions of Śrīla Prabhupāda to cooperate with the devotees dedicated to serving His Divine Grace’s orders.

His Divine Grace wanted us to preserve the properties, buildings, and temples of ISKCON which were obtained directly by Śrīla Prabhupāda and due to his inspiration. He knew that after his manifested presence Māyā would try to destroy our determination to maintain the temples and worshipable Deities; therefore His Divine Grace stressed the importance of this sacrifice time and time again. It was the largest portion of text in his legal will. In India many spiritual empires and vast properties were squandered and gradually sold off by followers who degraded to the level of maintaining themselves off of liquidating the results of their *guru*’s hard labor. As disciples and followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can we but endure whatever sacrifice or hardship is required to not succumb to this sacrilege?

Śrīla Prabhupāda made a distinction between different kinds of expenses in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Funds spent for printing and publishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness literatures was given the most importance. Also, money spent for immovable properties and other permanent assets for dedicating in the service of the worshipable Deities was considered in a special category compared to funds spent for meeting the daily maintenance expenses of the temples. Śrīla Prabhupāda has given his blood to build permanent shrines to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Śrī Rādhā Kṛṣṇa. To dismantle or wreck the fruits of his preaching is a great offense. Certainly, it is the duty of all devotees to serve the spiritual master and to preserve what he and the previous *ācāryas* have created or dedicated to Lord Kṛṣṇa. May we be blessed to realize this on this sacred Vyāsa-pūjā day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda built a house in which the whole world can be sheltered. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is to expand and bring in a ten-thousand-year era of transcendental peace and love of Godhead. Now ISKCON is growing in experience, growing in maturity. Śrīla Prabhupāda has given the foundation and structure of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement; now his followers must finish the construction according to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s blueprint. The actions of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement today are an example and precedent for the generations to come. May we be empowered to provide an example worthy of being followed, and not be an example that future generations should avoid following.

Kṛṣṇa conscious truths, *saṅkīrtana* preaching, spiritual institutions and traditions, the faith of the devotees, and the good will of the public all must be preserved—balancing everything properly amidst the extreme influences thrust upon ISKCON by the torrents of ever-changing time, place, and circumstance. The direct disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda have this great responsibility of preserving and, where not present, establishing traditions—traditions of ISKCON which may last for thousands of years, traditions which must enshrine Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings and his transcendental mellow of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda reveled in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that ISKCON was a branch of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s transcendental tree. Simply by realizing this one achieve complete security under the shelter of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s lotus feet. However, if one leaves the shelter of Lord Caitanya’s tree, under the control of his false ego, and takes instead the so-called shelter of the illusory energy - one simply gets kicked by Māyā. May we water the root of Lord Śrī Caitanya’s tree profusely, without interruption, so that the ISKCON branch can spread its shade all over the universe.

Meditating on the magnitude of the service before us and then upon my own lack of qualifications, it is my fervent and humble prayer on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā celebration of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda that Śrīla Prabhupāda may ever draw us closer under the shelter of his lotus feet and that he may never allow us to be cast away from his shelter. We hope for this since His Divine Grace is our ever well-wisher and the most compassionate. We are dependent upon his causeless mercy. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your unqualified servant-*  
*Who is always anxious for your causeless mercy,*  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kīrtirāja

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupada,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your unlimited causeless mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as usual, I am late in writing to you. It has been a very long time since I have written you a letter. Actually, it has been too long. I apologize for not having written to you sooner. Please forgive me.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is only by your grace, by your causeless mercy, that I am able to be engaged in your service and this most scientific yet sublime process of *bhakti-yoga.*

Śrīla Prabhupada, you have given us the most valuable treasure, but still we are sometimes not satisfied. We think there is something else. We think there is something more. Dhruva Mahārāja, after having had the *darśana* of Lord Viṣṇu in the forest, admitted that he had come to the forest looking for the broken pieces of glass, but instead of finding the glass, he had acquired the most beautiful and precious gem. He realized that in the beginning his desires had not been cent percent pure.

But then, after having been purified, he realized what was the real treasure, what was really worth searching for. You have given us this same treasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with next to no effort on our part. Now we must make a serious and determined effort to please you. You have fulfilled your responsibility to your *Guru* Mahārāja, and now it is time that we fulfill our responsibility to you. We cannot repay you for what you have given to us, Śrīla Prabhupada, but we certainly must try. I hope and pray that someday I may be able to appreciate what you have given us and that I may be able to repay you by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and spreading this message to those fallen souls who are not as fortunate as we are.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray, *namo mahā vadanyaya Kṛṣṇa prema pradāyate krsnāya Kṛṣṇa caitanya nāmine gaura tviṣe namaḥ.* We call Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu the most munificent incarnation of the Supreme Lord because Mahāprabhu distributed freely that which even Kṛṣṇa Himself did not give, pure love of the Supreme Lord. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are even more munificent than Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu because you give the message and ecstasy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, pure love of Kṛṣṇa, to people such as myself who are far more unfortunate than even Jagāi and Madhāi. But, as I am saying this, I am beginning to think that actually I am much more fortunate than Jagāi and Madhāi because I have had the supreme benediction of the Lord—that of having had your association. You are distributing this message of love of Godhead to every nook and cranny of this horrible and painful material world. In all the cracks and crevices of this world, where the dirt and grime of material contamination have built up, lifetime after lifetime, seemingly never to be scrubbed or scraped out, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not discriminate against the residents of such hellish places. You make no distinction as to who is qualified and who is not. You distribute your mercy and the message of Kṛṣṇa without discrimination. If you did not distribute the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness so indiscriminately, then I would not be here today. You have not ceased to distribute your mercy; you continue to distribute this mercy to this very day. Your kindness is unlimited. And your potency is unlimited as you are able to empower not only your disciples, but your disciples’ disciples as well, to spread the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glories are unlimited. Even if all of us had hundreds or even thousands of mouths and tongues, we would not be able to describe your glories fully or sufficiently. If it were not for your mercy there would be no hope whatsoever for the residents of this material world. Their only hope is that they may get your mercy. This is their only hope. You told me once during a morning walk in Māyāpur that if the people of the world do not have Kṛṣṇa consciousness then their suffering will only continue. You did not want to see people suffer due to a lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We do not want to see them suffer either. It is our responsibility as your followers, as Prabhupāda-anugas, to relieve you of your anxiety by delivering the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village the world over. This was the prediction of Mahāprabhu, and it was that which you worked for. We want to carry on your work, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I pray that we may do it to please you. We should not become like the two sons who were “massaging” the father.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can understand as you often said that association with you through your instructions is far more important than association through your physical presence.

However, I want to have your personal association again. That association and the instructions that you gave me are my life and soul. I know that you have given me enough instructions that it would take me millions of lifetimes to accomplish. I pray that I may have millions of lifetimes to engage in your devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we also pray *yugāyitam nimeṣeṇa cakṣusā prāvṛṣāyitam śūnyāyitaṁ jagat sarvam govinda-viraheṇa me.* It has almost been eleven years and it seems like twelve lifetimes. Certainly there was a great vacuum created when you left this physical world. That vacuum can be filled up by the instructions which you have given to us in so many varieties of forms—lectures, books, personal instructions, stories, etc. You are still with us, guiding us along this “razor’s edge” path back to Godhead. I pray that I will have your association again and that I may do something for pleasing Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, seventeen years ago you traveled to Moscow with Śyāmasundara and Aravinda. You found the field for preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness there ripe. A little more than a year ago, the devotees were able to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and “Jaya Prabhupāda!” every day in the streets of Moscow and eight other Soviet cities for more than a month before they were eventually stopped by the police. I could hardly believe my ears and eyes while watching the video recording of this most historic of events. Unfortunately, I could not be there in person.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Soviet devotees have distributed on the streets of the USSR thousands of thousands of the more than 25,000 copies of your books which they printed there, including your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* in Russian. Now they are preparing to print your *Kṛṣṇa* book. And, just today I received a phone call from one of the Moscow devotees, informing me that our seven-year struggle to register your International Society for Krishna Consciousness in the USSR had finally borne some fruit— the Soviet authorities approved our application to be recognized as a bona fide religion in USSR.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you went to Moscow with just two devotees, and now you have more than a thousand devotees in more than sixty Soviet cities and towns, trying to please you by spreading this mission, despite the difficulties they encounter. You once said that by distributing your books in the Soviet Union the inevitable war between Russia and America could be put off. Now, to the utter amazement of people the world over, such good relations are developing between America and the Soviet Union. Could this be attributed to the fact that the Soviet Union is being influenced by Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the Soviet people are becoming more and more interested and anxious to read your books? Perhaps this is one very clear example of how, as you said, “It will be noted down in the history books how the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement had changed the course of the world history.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is all proof that you are still with us. You are still fighting like you used to, to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I beg that you will continue to shower your mercy on my head, and I pray that you will give me the strength and determination to forever stay engaged in your service and not leave the shelter of your lotus feet. Please keep me fully engaged. I beg to always remain,

*Your faithful servant,*  
Kīrtirāja dāsa

## Loka-sākṣī dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

After I fall flat at your lotus feet, please accept my most humble offering of love and loyalty on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

Because of my insignificant position and poor understanding, I do not even dare to consider the magnitude of your status in the spiritual realm, but what matters is that Lord Caitanya had sent you, a pure *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee, and you, by your causeless mercy, are maintaining such a fool and rascal like myself in this Vaikuṇṭha movement. You never give up trying to save your most weak son, because, Lord Caitanya is the most munificent of the incarnations of God, you are the most merciful of the devotees.

*Yasya deve para bhaktir yathā deve thatā gurau*  
*Tasyaite kathitā hy arthāh prakāśante mahātmanah*

**“Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spritiual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

Of course, I am not a great soul, but for sure my faith in your and Kṛṣṇa’s instructions will give me whatever knowledge is needed to become a humble servant of Kṛṣṇa and His devotees.

It is the duty of everyone who wants to be a pure devotee to faithfully see the bona fide spiritual master and the Lord within, provided they are purified in heart by following the directions of the spiritual master. Because that is my honest desire, please give me the power to be able to follow your instructions in the shelter of your ISKCON, in the association of your sincere devotees.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Lok-sākṣī dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who is very dear to Lord Krsna, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*Yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*Yathā deve thatā gurau*  
*Tasyaita kathitā hy arthaḥ*  
*Prakāśante mahātmanah*

**“To one who has staunch faith in the words of the spiritual master and the words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the secret of success in Vedic knowledge is revealed.” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.23)**

Many times, in letters, conversations, and in formal lectures you stressed how you wanted ISKCON’s members to carry the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement on in your absence. You did not want a “personality cult” of disciples who could work only under your direct personal supervision, but ones who, in your physical absence, would be competent to continue and expand the work you set in motion.

As early as December 25, 1969, you wrote in a letter to Gargamuni: “I am an old man, and I may shift away from the scene at any moment, but I am very much hopeful of my disciples who are so sincerely helping me. This movement is greatly needed for the human society so we are not working like any political party, but we are trying to execute the will of the Supreme Lord.”

Yet despite your repeated pleas over the next decade for your disciples to work cooperatively within ISKCON and to remain fixed in their vows of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, schismatic tendencies arose, and many drifted away from the schismatic standards they had vowed to maintain. After your departure both phenomena escalated.

You warned us about both pitfalls. Regarding the breakdown in unity, you cited an example in the recent history of the Gauḍīya Math (Cc. *Mādhya-līlā* 12.8, purport) Here you explain how at the time of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, he “requested all his disciples to form a governing body and conduct missionary activities cooperatively. He did not instruct a particular man to become the next *ācārya.* But just after his passing away, his leading secretaries made plans, without authority to occupy the post of *ācārya,* and they split into two factions over who the *ācārya* should be.” No governing body was formed, and litigation between these factions, you wrote, “is still going on after forty years with no decision.”

You also said that by not following our strict devotional standards we would compromise the movement’s purity and power to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness and thus “ spoil everything” from within. The fall in recent years of many leading figures in ISKCON has put the movement to a severe test. In some cases this created baffling organizational difficulties, and even caused doubt the very process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness itself.

The tendencies toward schismatic organizations and compromised devotional standards still remain and at times complement each other. Added to this, there has been a general drift (for multifarious reasons) from a greater commitment to ISKCON. At no time had the dual need for unity and purity been more urgently felt than now.

ISKCON is passing through the difficult transition from its “strictly *āśrama”* phase to a predominantly congregational membership of both initiated and non-initiated devotees. Thus the test is to retain and strengthen our principles and practices of purity while simultaneously maintaining a “house in which everyone can live.” You were simultaneously the strictest and the most liberal; you were uncompromising in your purity, yet you never alienated a sincere soul; you encouraged everyone, fanning the tiniest sparks into blazing devotional fires. It is the level of devotional depth and maturity we must strive to achieve if ISKCON is to successfully pass through the current period of its spiritual evolution.

Your word—which are all readily accessible in the form of your books, lectures, letters, and conversations—exactly follow in the footsteps of Lord Caitanya.

“If we simply follow Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instructions and follow in the footsteps of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement can advance, and even more difficult tasks \[ than those performed by Arjuna and Hānuman\]can be performed by the preachers remaining faithful to the service of the Lord.”(*Cc. Mādhya-līlā,* 16.65)

Let us all pray for your mercy, because the mercy of Lord Caitanya is available only through Your Divine Grace. If we develop implicit faith in your words and constantly study and meditated upon them, we shall, as confirmed in the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad,* realize the ultimate “secret of success.”

*At the dust of your lotus feet, I beg to remain your eternal servant,*  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! Please accept this humble offering in the most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world:

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* First Canto, Second Chapter, verse 16, it is stated:

*śuśrūṣoḥ śraddadhānasya*  
*vāsudeva-kathā-ruciḥ*  
*syān Mahāt-sevayā viprāḥ*  
*puṇya-tīrtha-niṣevanāt*

**“O twice-born sages, by serving those devotees who are completely freed from vice, great service is done. By such service, one gains, affinity for hearing the messages of Vāsudeva.”**

From this verse, I found the hope  
To gain the taste I need,  
To be an instrument for your plan  
To purify all hearts from greed.

Without attraction for constantly hearing  
The messages from your lips,  
What hope is there to influence others,  
And free them from Māyā’s grips?

No purer words have I heard  
Than those coming down through you.  
In time, through continued service,  
Even I become enthused

To hear the messages of Vasudeva;  
Nondifferent from the Lord,  
They cleanse the hearts from impediments  
And provide the best reward.

Association with His pure devotee,  
No more valuable gift than that.  
One moment in your presence  
Can start one on the path.

*sādhu-saṅga sādhu saṅga*  
*sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge*  
*sarva-siddhi haya*

That’s all it takes to plant the seed  
For service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.  
The desire to serve is already there;  
It’s you that makes the connection.

By serving those who are freed from vice  
The greatest service has been done.  
You’ve given each and every one of us  
The means by which we can become

A menial servant of yourself,  
Who incessantly would speak  
The glories of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa  
To everyone you’d meet.

No vices did you manifest;  
Your example was perfection.  
Every act performed by you  
Was full of good intention.

No hidden motives behind your acts,  
Only one way for that to be,  
Through unalloyed devotion  
So rare, such pure bhakti.

Such selfless compassion upon  
The fallen residents of Kali,  
Your determination was so fixed  
To stop them from their folly.

“Just hear the glories of the Lord—  
they’ll purify the heart.  
Don’t hear from those whose motives  
Are selfish from the start.”

These words of wisdom would come from you  
In your classes and your purports.  
Relentlessly you’d emphasize,  
“Don’t hear from these upstarts!”

“Hear from one who knows the truth.  
Everything is in my books!”  
All the sages who speak within them  
Know how Kṛṣṇa looks.

It’s obvious by reading your purports  
That you know Kṛṣṇa too.  
No speculations anywhere.  
You’re realized through and through.

Within these books you’ve freely given  
The chance to hear from you.  
If I can help in some small way,  
What’s left for me to do?

Just as in your presence  
We’d bring everyone to hear,  
Whenever you would come to speak  
We’d go out and bring them near.

Our enthusiasm for preaching  
Would come by bringing them to you.  
In this way that same enthusiasm  
Should still exist now, too.

Let’s preach to others and be fixed  
To see that your books continue.  
And those who’ll come to assist us  
Will be inspired by that example.

So inspired they will become,  
They’ll give their lives to you.  
For it’s you who’ve given us the chance  
To connect them all through you.

Please let me go on in this way  
Just remaining your assistant  
Eternally, I have no other hope;  
I’m just your menial servant.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṅgacit Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Transcendental knowledge reveals everything, just as the sun lights up everything in the morning. Thus a pure soul can see reality that is beyond the three modes of nature. This reality, manifested as the spiritual master and the Supreme Personality of Godhead, reveals itself to the surrendered soul. Dear *Ācārya*deva, O divine master, you reveal yourself in fullness according to the seriousness of the follower. O perfect example of the *saṅkīrtana* devotee, you paved the road for all the future souls for the next ten thousand years so that they can all go back home. And this you did as a perfect engineer, not making any mistake so that everyone can go safely. This book distribution is the life and soul, and this you had shown us even before coming to America.
