Everything you did, as well as your divine appearance, were to show us that all our attention should be towards book distribution; all purity, realization, bliss, advancement, etc., would come from surrendering to this sublime mission, without deviation or speculation. Therefore, on this divine appearance day of yours, I beg that you give me intelligence, because, if the disciple does not follow the order of the spiritual master, what is the use of his existence? Your Vyāsa-pūjā day is to reinforce even more our strong desire to distribute your books with purity and determination.

All Glories to Oṁ Viṣṇupada Paramahāṁsa Parivrājak*ācārya* 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the great preceptor of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and all the glories to this glorious book distribution.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Paraṅgacit Swami

## Prabhavishnu Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day, I humbly prostrate myself at your lotus feet and request your blessings to be able to continue to assist you in pushing forward this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Of course, you have always told us that your mercy comes in the form of your instructions, which are all contained in your transcendental books. This material world is very dangerous place for one who is attempting to advance in spiritual life, but by carefully studying your books we can get the determination and guidance necessary to cross over all obstacles. Your books are like a window to the spiritual world, but one must keep constantly looking through the window; otherwise one may become weak and attracted by the glitter of *māyā.* You are so much present in the form of your books that when we read them we feel that you are personally giving us protection and encouragement, and especially you are giving us the intelligence to discriminate between that which will help us to advance spiritually and that which will not. There is so much service to be done in spreading this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, but unless we have a strong foundation in hearing your instructions properly, we will not be able to accomplish anything effectively. Therefore you have stated that “Books are the basis.” By studying them carefully, we ourselves will have a strong basis, and by distributing them profusely, others will get a strong basis in spiritual life. Once you asked me if I was regularly reading your books. “Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda,” I answered sheepishly. Then you said, “Unless you read, how can you preach?” Since that time I must confess that I do not feel satisfied that I have adequately studied your books. I very much desire to spend more time scrutinizingly studying them. I therefore pray to you on this auspicious day that you help me to increase this regular study. By better hearing, then I am confident that the quality of my chanting will also improve, and consequently I will be able to better accomplish all the other service which I have been requested to perform. Kṛṣṇa is the powerhouse of transcendental energy, and you are our only connection to Him. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am a very weak and feeble person, with hardly any attraction to Kṛṣṇa. But for your mercy, I would still be floundering in the material world. Please give me the strength to continue in your service.

*I beg to remain your eternal servant,*  
Prabhavishnu Swami

## Ravindra-Svarūpa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. *Mahāt-sevam dvaram ahur vimuktes:* the service of your lotus feet is the gateway to freedom for the whole world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am asking only one thing of you. Please let me understand with full and irrevocable realization that your order is my very life and that outside of your order I have no life at all.

Please eradicate in m this illusion; my life belongs to me. Help me to remember that on that radiant day I took *japa* beads from your outstretched hand and had the *kaṇthi-mālā,* your livery, bound about my neck to show the world you were my master—on that day I renounced forever all false claim to what I called “my own life,” “my own time,” to “private life” and “time-off.”

My life is not mine. It is your possession to do with as you will. You own me—body, mind and soul.

I beg you, then, make me over according to your will.

I know that I am a tiny spark, a marginal particle, and that by my essential nature I am bound to be controlled by higher powers. Let that power be you. I am a servant always, I must obey; let it be only you and only you I obey.

For I confess there is another power—subtle, wily, cunning—who struggles to possess me, who strives to wrest me from your service and enslave me to his. You know well about this. I have realized at last that if every thought and act, down to the slightest velleity of my will, is not dictated by you, it is dictated by this other. I have been taught by painful experience that your service is secured not by sentiments and good intentions but only by exacting obedience; without punctilious submission we are seduced into the contradictory, nonsensical endeavor of attempting to serve you while disobeying your order.

Whenever and wherever we are not controlled by you, we are controlled by this sinister power. This power works against you, and we becoming his agents, work against you even as we are supposed by ourselves and others to be your representatives. Save me from the most evil of fates.

It is impossible for me to wear these clothes, to preach what you preached without representing you to others. This is inescapably part of being your servant, and it is the most heavy responsibility. I protest that I am unworthy and unfit; I say that I am a candle to the sun. Nevertheless, people judge you by me. They say: “This person is *his* handiwork; this ISKCON, his creation. He claimed he was doing something wonderful. Let’s see how well he’s done.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is my duty to protect your honor, to let the world know how wonderful you are. Surely if I am convinced you are wonderful, then I will execute your order exactingly, and I will be made over by that order. Work your wonder on me and make me your representative. Let me honor and love for you be shown in my perfect conformity to your desire. Being then your servant, let me really represent you.

Becoming wholly yours, I will serve you and not the sinister power that comes cloaked in self-will and selfishness, in every impulse that does not rise from your order. Let me name that power plainly. It is Kali, and he goes recruiting among your disciples. I am afraid of him, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I have seen this horrible handiwork. I recognize his approach, his probes in the form of lust, greed, pride, and anger, searching out the weak places. But I want to be your disciple, Śrīla Prabhupāda, not his. I don’t want to be a *kali-chela* who, with *tilāka* and neck-beads—your livery—serves him and dishonors you. If I am not your servant, I am his; for me these are the only alternatives. Your sublime confidence in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness inspires me and heartens me. I can never forget the time you revealed that confidence to me. We sat in your office in the temple on Henry Street in Brooklyn, and you were telling us of the future, when devotees, commanding the wealth and power of nations, would confront and conquer demoniac hosts. And I was sick with misgiving. So I put the matter before you.

“You foresee a time when devotees will have the wealth of whole countries under their control and will deploy whole armies,” I said.

And you nodded, “Yes.”

Then my doubt: “In those circumstances, how can we be sure that we won’t become demons.”

You didn’t hesitate. “Chant sixteen rounds a day and follow the four regulative principles.”

“That’s enough?” I asked.

“That’s enough,” you said.

It’s a simple thing, isn’t it, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

But this question torments me: “Why have so many—so very many— gone?” I have lived through this terrible mortality; I have seen the brightest angels fall. What am I to do? How will I be protected?

I need your complete shelter; no partial measures will protect me. The simple truth is that “the simple thing”—four regulative principles and sixteen rounds a day— cannot be long maintained in isolation from all your other orders. Therefore I am earnestly seeking for perfect submission to your every order, to have my very pulse beat under your ordinance.

I am weak, Śrīla Prabhupāda; I have done terrible things. I cannot afford to give Kali even the slightest foothold in my life. Let me never forget this, and give me the intelligence to take shelter you give. This is what I am asking of you, in this, my seventeenth wondrous year of life at your lotus feet.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Rūpānuga dāsa

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances.

You said, “At least maintain what I have given.” Yet you have given so much! Your books, the temples, the farms, the schools, all the instructions, the orders in your letters and books. You gave so many orders, it will require many generations of your servants to carry them out. You are supporting us by your orders. Sometimes you called them “hints” or “opinions” or “suggestions”; still we accepted them as equal to Vedic injunctions, ruling our existence and giving the purpose of our lives.

Your orders are different than those of a mundane master because they are imbued with the potency to carry them out. They are backed up by the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa Himself. Mundane masters give money, men, or other resources; but because of you, His pure representative, Kṛṣṇa gives Himself.

If we are chanting sixteen rounds daily and following your orders, we can accept whatever happens to us as Kṛṣṇa’s merciful lessons, free from the government of the modes of nature and the starry skies. That is our certainty while we commit ourselves to you and your orders, avoiding inventions or replacements.

As the seconds fly by relentlessly, I ponder my list of orders: “teach by ideal character”; “contribute this son to Kṛṣṇa “; guard the spiritual standards; see that our men are adored as honest; carefully study your books; develop New Hastinapur ; write a book called entitled *Intellectual Animalism;* “develop the farm communities as far as possible”; “every center must have a farm”; arrange for dioramas; “begin a higher school of theology”; “never use this artificial fertilizer”; preach to the students; “keep a Sunday school for the Indian children”; establish *varṇāśrama* and *varṇāśrama* colleges; open restaurants with reading rooms; see that our books are distributed; and so on….

I wonder how I can possibly complete them in what remains of my allotted time, or which to emphasize, or how to carry them out. All of them are so glorious that to successfully complete even one fraction would be enough for the insignificant spiritual spark I am. Even for that I am totally dependent upon your blessings.

So, please bless all of us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we may carry out your orders now and in the eternal future; that we may deliver those orders to anyone who will take them; and, most importantly, that we may never follow the orders of anyone who contradicts you or attempts to create new orders for the world to follow.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

He reasons ill who says that Vaiṣṇava die  
When thou are living still in sound.  
Vaiṣṇava die to live and living  
Spread the holy name around.

It was the spring of 1978, the first Gaura-Pūrṇimā festival after your disappearance. I had gone early to Vṛndāvana, somehow expecting that you would still be there. How could you not be there? In the warm winter afternoon we walked to the little hut in the temple courtyard which they said was your Samadhi. Offering obeisances, I was overwhelmed with a mixture of emotions, inappropriate to describe here. I felt in turmoil, your absence a conclusive reality. My last hope was gone.

That evening you came to me in a dream. We were walking together around Kṛṣṇa-Balarama Mandira in the early morning hours. No words were exchanged; the only sound was the constant tapping of your cane as you walked, head held high. I was in anxiety, thinking that you were going somewhere. When you sat by the generator house I sat at your feet, and you emanated a wonderful security of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Then you smiled in a reassuring and all-knowing way, your eyes moist. I felt relieved. But then the thought of your separation overwhelmed me and I began to cry. With your lotus hand you rubbed my head repeatedly in a kindly way saying, “Do not worry, everything will be all right.” And as I sat there sobbing, you continued to repeat, “Everything will be all right.”

I felt assured by your instructions. Your association gave me strength. Time has passed. Now ten years later I am amazed how you have become the focal point of my existence. In the dedication to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* you state, “To Śrīla Prabhupāda Bhaktisiddhānta Gosvāmi Mahārāja. He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.”

I do not claim to be a follower: I am trying to be one. But even in my neophyte condition I have become increasingly aware of your presence. In numerous ways I am experiencing “living with you,” and even as I write this offering I am amazed.

When you were here it took little endeavor. A letter, at most an airplane ticket, and the reassurance that in any issue you were there, easily accessible, the ultimate guide and authority. Yet in your physical absence your association is no less sublime, no less real.

Being forced to depend on your instructions and your unmanifest features, am I learning to become more aware of you? It appears that my relationship with you is becoming deeper and more mature, for I am forced to see and hear you in places I did not notice before.

While chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* I remember you, seated on your *vyāsāsana,* your deep and resonant voice. Sometimes I feel very blissful. But this is only by your causeless mercy. Kṛṣṇa is nondifferent from His form as sound. And you have delivered that sound to me, enduring countless hardships leaving Vṛndāvana. Every name I vibrate is by your grace, so you are nondifferent from the name.

Every morning I pray to you. Please let me chant this holy name purely. My *anārthas* appear insurmountable. By the knowledge and service you have given us, we may be released from their clutches and be able to really say “Kṛṣṇa.” When will that day come?

In my waking hours how many times do I offer your *praṇāmas: namo oṁ Viṣṇupādāya Kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale…..?*Occasionally I hear what I am saying. You are so dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, delivering the world from impersonalism and voidism. And if I offer my obeisances with heartfelt devotion, are you not there to accept my prayers? *Śrīmate bhaktivedanta-svāmin iti nāmine.*

Sitting in a solitary room, reading *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*——every word is your meditation, and if I meditate on every word then certainly I am with you. You said, “If you want to know me, read my books.” So I am reading, studying, memorizing, trying to serve your ecstasies through the transcendental process. What greater association, what greater bliss is there than becoming absorbed in your sound? I know that you are there in your instructions for lost humanity, guiding them home, back to Godhead. Floundering, I am groping through the maze of my material desires, trying to achieve your lotus feet. Everything is there—*Bhagavat-gīā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Nectar of Devotion,* such wonderful books!

In 1980 you allowed me to begin assisting you in your worship of Gaura-Nitāi. How could I realize such good fortune? Every day you awake Their Lordships, dress Them, feed Them,, and put Them to sleep. I am simply your menial servant. As I meditate on Their service, it is only as your humble assistant. You have delivered the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda to such a fallen wretch as me. By your grace now I have the opportunity to travel with “the Lord in the box.” No doubt this has saved me from great tribulation. Twenty-four hours a day. They are my constant companions, as are you.

Although I am a recluse and irresponsible, through your transcendental arrangement you are forcing me to accept more responsibility. I came to England to travel and preach from college to college. I had my plan, but what was yours?

I organized *saṅkīrtana,* manages temples, counseled orphans, made mistakes, swept up shards of shattered *gurus,* perspired profusely, amazed all the while. How was all this going on? National Secretary? Not me! GBC! Why not someone else? Akhaṇḍadhī Prabhu was murmuring in the background, and Kṛṣṇa dāsa Swami would not give in. When I opened my eyes to see if they had gone, I found myself sitting through long, hot, arduous hours at Śrīdhāma Māyāpur trying to follow in your footsteps and “do as you did” with my other GBC Godbrothers. How did I ever get there? Only by your transcendental tricks.

I certainly did not want to accept disciples. Why take the risk? Why accept the *karma?* Do I need the extra burden? Let me wait a few more years. Back and forth, yes, no, maybe. Somehow you have forced me to accept the responsibility. Once again, through the directive of my Godbrothers, I feel your transcendental association. Bhāgavata Āśraya Prabhu yelled at me and told me I was giving him a headache being on the mental platform. I felt that you were yelling at me. “Why are you so selfish? You are not the doer. It is on my behalf.” Sitting on the other side of the fire, handing over tulāsi beads, I say, “Your name is Rūpa Raghunātha dāsa. “ I can only depend on you. What is my qualification? I know what I am. Unless I connect them with you, what is my value? And now in this service, always thinking of you, how to deliver you to those disciples, this is my service to them. Forcing me to depend on you, they have also become my *guru.*

Earlier, when we sat around the table in Brussels, Giriraja Mahārāja chastised everyone for all the money wasted on this international structure. I felt like you were chastising and reprimanding me. I had a sinking feeling in my stomach, very hopeless and penitent, because I knew you were displeased. There was no doubting that.

And what I did not hear from my Godbrothers I heard from your other followers. Although they were junior to me in years, I heard you speaking through them. “We want managerial accountability.” “Enough of heavy-handed dealings.” “No absolute promises or infallible daydreams.” “Inspiration, purity, kindness.” The voices echoed in my head day and night. Kṛṣṇatīrtha told me, “You ostracize those who don’t agree to work with you.”

The pressure was almost unbearable. It was like when you said to double it, and I could not understand how to do it. I knew, however, that there was no way to avoid it. I know that I have to change and there is no backing down, no alternative, not within Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I feel your association forcing and guiding me through other devotees.

And you are still guiding and directing things. What are we doing? We are trying to understand your plan, as you are unfolding it day by day. There is the manor campaign. Who would have understood the developments? The opportunities which appear to be manifesting are certainly not our doing. If you are not organizing things, who is? As you said, “I have created these big projects just to engage my leaders.” Are you not continuing to engage us by creating more big projects so that we will become more absorbed in Kṛṣṇa’s service and not get distracted by sense gratification, using our temples for eating, sleeping and opulent living? In the burden of that service is your loving glance. “Accept headaches for Kṛṣṇa’s service.” But such headaches are bearable only if you give us your constant reassurance.

When I came before Rādhā-Londonīśvara I think of you and how you kidnapped Them. Like Kṛṣṇa kidnapped Rukminī. You installed Them, your personal Deities, the first Deities of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa to come and accept your service. Rādhā-Londonīśvara and Rādhā-Gokulānanda, the bliss of New Gokula. Bhaktivedānta Manor, the place where you walked, talked and lived. The atmosphere in your rooms is so overpowering that even the most stone-hearted atheists are impressed when they enter. We live in your houses, serve your Deities, listen to your footsteps on the stairs and for your voice: “Who has left the lights on?” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the challenge in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and you are the opportunity. You are the enthusiasm that reaches its pinnacle at our Christmas marathon, and you are that satisfied feeling when we hear that we have distributed almost a million books this year. As the Supersoul is always there to give us direction and guidance, so I always hear your voice in the back of my mind, “Fifty percent to the BBT and build a great temple in London. A set of books in everyone’s home and everyone chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. At least we can work together cooperatively.”

What I have recalled here are but a few realizations, some thoughts of how I am increasingly aware of your presence. Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for your reassurance. Although it is not proper to dwell on it, thank you for reassuring me these dreams, when you left, when I took *sannyāsa,* when I was despondent and when I accepted disciples. Thank you for guiding me through my Godbrothers and your other followers. Thank you for your books, through which I can hear your transcendental knowledge. Thank you for your tapes, your morning walks and lectures, your voice ringing clear in the air, each and every word saturated with love for Kṛṣṇa, everything perfect.. Thank you for allowing me to chant the holy name. Thank you for the service you have given me of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness in England and serving Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda.

Sometimes in my mind’s eye, I dream of Bhagavat Āśraya, Akhaṇḍadhī, Keśava Bhāratī, Balabhadra and all the others falling at your feet. You are resplendent at always, sitting, casually leaning back on a cushion. We place before you a few morsels of service for your satisfaction. For you we have constructed these temples; for you we have distributed so many books; here these devotees are chanting; here is Kṛṣṇa consciousness being fulfilled in the way that you desired. Then with that reassurance that only comes when you are pleased, we feel overwhelmed when you smile at us and say, “Thank you very much. This is very nice.” We are trying to cooperate together, because you are the adhesive bond of love which keeps us struggling on against all odds.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are always accessible. In my pitiful groping I am trying to find you like a blind man grasping for a wall. You are my ultimate friend, my shelter, my worshipful Deity and my source of inspiration. You are the ultimate goal of all my services. I have no hope that I can please Kṛṣṇa or ever love Him, but I am constantly obsessed by the thought of trying to please you.

I often feel alone and frightened. The odds seem so overwhelming. Can I make it? Can I lead others to you? Will we win the campaign? Will we build a temple? You are the doubtless and fearless way of success. As my eyes open I am starting to understand that, if I stay at your lotus feet, everything will be all right, because with you everything is always all right. In 1975 we sat at the airport in Dallas. The devotees were sitting at your lotus feet and you were chanting *japa.* No one said anything; we just chanted. I felt a material energy recede, and there was an inconceivable atmosphere of peace-no anxiety. “Now I am in Vaikuṇṭha.” Yes, in your association there is no need to worry. Everything is always all right.”

So I pray on this day that I may become increasingly aware of you, both in waking and sleeping; and that I may at all times, in all places and always, remain a tiny servant at your lotus feet.

*Your servant,*  
Śivarāma Swami

## Tamal Krishna Goswami

**A Puppy’s Pūjā**

Of all the days Vyāsa-pūjā is the heaviest affair;  
To venture forth from gravity it’s doubtful few would dare.  
Yet who will mind when witty words relieve the tension and the pace,  
And tickle one’s intelligence, and force a smile across one’s face?  
There is no better cure for those who suffer from depression:  
A malady unique to man, proud pinnacle of progression;  
Though he prides himself far better than all other living creatures,  
Each one surpasses man with one or more outstanding features.  
And so we’ve come a long way ‘round in order to compare  
Śrīla Prabhupāda with these creatures by the qualities they share.  
In case you think us frivolous, we’ll start comparing those most bless’d  
And then proceed to name some others who, in fact, are more regressed.

Always serving, dear to Kṛṣṇa, all the Vedas to avow:  
Prabhupāda’s a pure devotee! He’s a wish-fulfilling cow!  
Though more tolerant than a tree and more humble than a straw,  
He prowles the planet like a lion, purifying by his roar.  
While Garuḍa feasts on elephants, timiṅgila swallows whales.  
This giant Śrīla Prabhupāda with his sharpened words impales  
All atheists and voidists, whom he hungrily devours,  
As tidbits for a giant with such superhuman powers.

His singing charms the songbirds, lulls the owls to sleep at night.  
His vision shames the eagles with his futuristic sight.  
The swan of men, he takes the best and leaves aside the worst,  
Drinking Kṛṣṇa conscious nectar with discriminating thirst.  
As a fish becomes caught by allurement of the tongue,  
As a deer lies fallen due to deadly tunes a hunter’s sung,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda sits motionless, tears gliding down his face.  
His senses victimized by Kṛṣṇa’s all-compelling grace.

His birth has lifted Mother Earth from the ocean of depravity  
And made all planets orbit her despite the laws of gravity.  
The snakes have vowed to mend their ways or else forfeit their skins.  
Charmed and swaying to his mantras, smiling their toothless grins.  
Giraffes stretch their speckled necks to nibble tops of trees,  
While children grasp expectantly to catch the moon with ease.  
But only Śrīla Prabhupāda can reach that hidden space  
To rouse the soul that lies unconscious by his merciful embrace.

More cunning that a clever fox when it comes to business deals;  
More playful with small children than a pool of buoyant seals;  
As steadfast as a stubborn mule (he argues with such candor);  
He’s equally evasive as a darting salamander!  
He bristles like a porcupine, guards Kṛṣṇa from offenses,  
But like a tortoise when attacked, he withdraws all his senses.  
No dog has ever been more loyal to his master’s mission,  
Nor followed faithfully behind, so closely to tradition.

And we’re his puppies, barking and howling, trying to catch our tails—  
As much as we cuss, and kick up a fuss, our barks never sound like hails!  
It was our hope, that within this scope, the quirks of Mother Nature  
Would magnify the radii of Prabhupāda’s outstanding stature.  
Alas, it seems we’ll have to wait awhile before we hit the mark;  
Next Vyāsa-pūjā day we’ll start to pray where we left off-Bark, bark! Bark, bark!

Tamal Krishna Goswami

## Toshan Krishna Das

All Glories to Śrī *Guru* and Gaurāṅga!

Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

My obeisances to you again and again. I want to associate with you forever, making my residence your lotus feet.

You ever beckon us to your shelter—your house—where everyone can live. That house has walls and floors built of your instructions preserved with your books. The foundation of this mansion is constructed of your firm adherence to the Paramparā, the statements of the previous *ācāryas* and *sadhu, śāstra,*and *guru.*

The roof of this expensive edifice(protecting all within) is the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which you attract (by your purity) and then bestow to all who will take it.

This house is eternal and can accommodate all the peoples of the world. It is located in transcendental knowledge, far from the precincts of illusion. This abode of yours resounds with intoxicating *kirtana,* describing the glories of the Lord and His devotees. The residents are dancing, and their ecstasy only increases.

The doors of this most sacred place, through which I long to pass, are always open—it’s an open secret; for you forever live in your divine instructions, and your followers live with you.

*Your fallen and lowly servant,*  
Toshan Krishna Das

## Vasudeva dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Some time ago, while I was reading the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* I came across the following purport written by you—

“…It is clearly stated in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Anty-līlā, 7.11):*

*kali-kālera dharma—Kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*Kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

In this age of Kali, real religious propaganda should induce people to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* This is possible for someone who is especially empowered by Kṛṣṇa. No one can do this without being especially favored by Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura comments in this regard in his *Anubhāṣya,* wherein he quotes a verse from *Nārāyaṇa-saṁhitā:*

*Dvāparīyair janair Viṣṇuh*  
*Pañcarātrais tu kevalaiḥ*  
*Kalau tu nāma-mātreṇa*  
*pūjyate bhagavān hariḥ*

‘In Dvāpara-yuga, devotees of Lord Viṣṇu and Kṛṣṇa rendered devotional service according to the principles of *pañcarātrika.* In this age of Kali, the Supreme Personality of Godhead is worshiped simply by the chanting of His holy names.’ Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura then comments: Without being empowered by the direct potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa to fulfill His desire, and without being especially favored by the Lord, no human being can become the spiritual master of the whole world….Only an empowered personality can distribute the holy name of the Lord and enjoin all fallen souls to worship Kṛṣṇa. By distributing the holy names of the Lord, he cleanses the hearts of the most fallen people; therefore he extinguishes the blazing fire of the material world. Not only that, he broadcasts the shining brightness of Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence throughout the world. Such an *ācārya,* or spiritual master, should be considered nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa—that is , he should be considered the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency. Such a personality is *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha*— that is, he is always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa.” (Cc. *Madhya* 25.9, purport)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this purport clearly reveals your eternal identity. You are, without doubt, that empowered incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s potency who came directly from the spiritual world, specifically to establish the *yuga-dharma* throughout this material world.

I offer my obeisances unto you again and again on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

*Your servant,*  
Vasudeva dāsa, G.B.C.

## Yaśoṁatīnandana dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet. Your divine appearance in this material world on the auspicious day of Nandotsava was certainly not an accident. From time immemorial the world has been celebrating the appearance of Lord Kṛṣṇa on Janmashtami day. Your appearance on the following day means the world will now celebrate both these days for thousands of years to come. It also means you are inseparable from Kṛṣṇa. Whenever one remembers Kṛṣṇa, one must remember you. The celebration of Janmashtami form now on is incomplete without celebrating your appearance also. Kṛṣṇa is more happy when His devotee is glorified than Himself. Unfortunately, the ignorant world does not know your glories, but as soon as the holy name penetrates their stonelike hearts, they will assemble together in millions and go up on the rooftops chanting, “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!” Just as Śrīmati Devaki and Vasudeva, while imprisoned by Kaṁsa, could not celebrate Kṛṣṇa’s appearance but celebrated in their minds and gave millions of cows in charity to the *brāhmaṇas,* in the same way, surrounded by the demoniac population of Kali-yuga, we, your helpless and weak disciples, are not able to celebrate your appearance day in the manner you, the greatest *mahā-bhāgavata* spiritual master of the universe, deserve. We can only pray to Kṛṣṇa that someday He may allow your divine appearance day to become more gloriously celebrated than Christmas.

Why do we give so much significance to your birthday? Is this just a sentimental attachment like the materialist’s attachment for the bodily birthday of his near and dear ones? No. This loving expression of an aspiring candidate for a pure devotee, representative of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, is the real understanding of the highest principle of spiritual life. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatīh kuto ’ pi.* Without your mercy, it is not possible for one to understand life’s ultimate goal. One will only get lost in the maze of sense gratification, materialistic religiosity, dry mental speculation, *ugra-karma,* blind following the blind, and non-Vedic *pāṣaṇḍa-dharmas.* Your birthday is not like the materialistic man’s reminder that one more year had been taken from his life. Your appearance day is to awaken the world to ever-blissful eternal life with Kṛṣṇa. Your appearance day signifies Lord Kṛṣṇa’s continuous concern to deliver the fallen souls by giving them *divya-jnana* in the chain of disciplic succession, consisting of great personalities such as Brahmā, Nārada, and Vyāsadeva. Even the sages and demigods are bewildered by various process of spiritual practices and therefore remain bereft of the *pañcama-puruṣārtha,* the fifth dimension, *Kṛṣṇa-prema.* Even the Vaiṣṇava sometimes become attracted to the majestic realm of Vaikuṇṭha and accept the four kinds of liberation. Lord Kṛṣṇa is not satisfied with this mixed devotion, full of awe and reverence. He is only satisfied with pure love, free from any tinge of awareness that He is God—the love exhibited only by the residents of Vṛndāvana. Just to propagate this pure love, Kṛṣṇa comes Himself as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Indeed, He is the topmost of all incarnations (*saba-avatāra sār śiroṁaṇi kevala ānanda-kāṇda).* Along with His Pañca-tattva associates, He distributes the highest to one and all, without any discrimination. What is most amazing is that Lord Caitanya has given away this most confidential power of distributing love of Kṛṣṇa to Your Divine Grace. The Lord is so pleased upon you that He is allowing you to surpass even Himself in distributing this love. You are so expert that you have made provision to distribute this love of God even after your disappearance from this world. Your Divine Grace has left two most significant contributions in this world that no previous *ācārya* ever emphasized so much: A transcendental spiritual organization, ISKCON; and your books.

As a visionary *ācārya,* you established a united front to fight with the force of Māyā and then flooded the world with the transcendental weapons of books—all in eleven short years. You are a trendsetter. Any sincere man can follow in your footsteps and go on developing by following your simple instructions. Unfortunately, we have not been able to steer your mission so well in your absence, but we have gained invaluable experience. We hope to learn from our mistakes and become your worthy sons. Due to material calculations, we try to take credit for some results that are arranged by Kṛṣṇa. Please, on this auspicious day, give us the strength to see the unseen hand of Kṛṣṇa everywhere and become humble servants of the disciplic succession. You always explained to us that Vyāsa-pūjā is meant for the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. You have so kindly delivered to us the divine knowledge, but due to stubbornness we keep our eyes closed. We beg your mercy to become fixed in the *bhakti-siddhanta* and weed out the *apasiddhāntas* from our hearts. Even though the material ocean is insurmountable, we have accepted you as captain of our ship, and therefore we are hopeful. Please do not forsake us. Keep us fixed as a dust particle at your lotus feet. Begging to remain the servant of your servant eternally…

*Your worthless servant,*  
Yaśoṁatīnandana dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

*Oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāyjana-śalakayā*  
*Cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Your appearance day is the day on which the knots of my heart become soft and loose, and the sun of hope illuminates my entire being. You are my secure and only shelter. No one will be able to comprehend the magnitude of your love until he has surrendered completely to you…..

Your movement of pure devotees keeps pushing forward strong. Your forecast that nobody would stop is confirmed. Your movement grows, transforms itself, expands, and shrinks—but does not decay. Your transcendental purity is still the ideal mirror in which we can see ourselves and our defects. You have been able to satisfy Kṛṣṇa. Who amongst us could say the same thing?

ISKCON is your own child. When you left it was still a small child, small and inexpert. Like children that imitate their father, so we tried to imitate you, due to our lack of experience. Now we are at the stage of adolescence in that critical stage of life in which one thinks he knows everything and cannot bear being instructed by others. We still have a way to go to become mature.

But your special mercy is present in the hearts of all your sons, and no matter in what situation they are in at this moment, none of them can forget Kṛṣṇa completely. You, who are the embodiment of mercy, have drilled our hard hearts to inject the nectar of your own love of Kṛṣṇa. Now we are your children and for Kṛṣṇa forever. You have marked us with the fire of divine love, and even when *māyā* tries to put obstacles in our spiritual advancement, she will not be able to stop us.

Being born into materialism, we have been educated to be hypocrites in a society of hypocrites. You pulled us out and forever from the world of falsity. But material motivations are still present in our activities, due to the influence of bad habits. The luster of that which is illusory still captivates our attention, but because of your wise teachings and your great example, we know it is only a farce.

By your causeless mercy we have the chance of not being confused in front of so many currents of imitators.

From you I have received an ideal and a goal to fight for, and its flag is your own quality: devotional service. You have the quality of serving Kṛṣṇa in the right way as much as the quality to correspond to others with your love of a father and an honest devotee.

From whom, if not from you, will I learn to have faith and compassion?  
From whom, if not from you, will I be able to have the necessary humility to help others?  
From whom, if not from you, will I obtain good lessons?  
From whom, if not from you, will I learn to be better?  
From whom, if not from you, will I receive the truth and the power to assimilate it in order to teach it?  
From whom, if not from you, will I get the intelligence to understand your mission?  
From whom, if not from you, could I have the answer for all the questions others present to me?  
From whom, if not from you, would I have the inspiration to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa with love and devotion?  
From whom, if not from you, could I have the power and strength to preach the glories of the holy name in the material world?  
You are the powerful angel that protects against all dangers.  
You are the incarnation of purity specially sent by Kṛṣṇa in order to counteract the poison of materialism in the wretched reign of Kali.  
You are the one who can allow this miserable worm also to be saved.

*The worst of all your sons,*  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

I have a dream….  
To sing of your glories,  
To see you being honored.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Most glorious, most learned.

I have a dream…  
To spread more of your fame  
In every town and village.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
One day all will acclaim.

I have a dream….  
To one day be purified,  
Absorbed in Caitanya’s līlā.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
The most perfect ācārya.

I have a dream….  
To learn how to cry,  
To learn how to laugh.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Teacher of all rasa.

I have a dream…..  
To learn how to write,  
To learn how to speak.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Perfect orator, perfect writer.

I have a dream….  
To join you forever,  
All Godbrothers united.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Perfect master, perfect guide.

I have a dream….  
To offer you surrendered souls  
From this land of Nimai.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Our very life, our very soul.

I have a dream….  
Young men in white dhotīs,  
Shining tilaka, bright-faced.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Sweet nectar from your lips.

I have a dream….  
Not to waste any time,  
To be eager to preach.  
You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Pure chanting you thus teach.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda…  
The great Bhagavata illuminator,  
Master of all leaders,  
At whose feet all surrender.  
Our constant meditation,  
Your Divine Grace.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
In love unsurpassed,  
In devotion unmatched,  
Effulgent as many suns,  
At whose feet all find solace,  
Your Divine Grace.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda……  
Kind and loving to all souls,  
True friend of the poor,  
Destroyer of darkness,  
Bearing torchlight of knowledge,  
Your Divine Grace.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
O most venerable Vaiṣṇava,  
Ever faithful and fresh,  
Our ever well-wisher,  
Only hope for the distressed,  
Your Divine Grace.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda….  
Be merciful unto me.  
I am most insignificant and fallen.  
All things to you I owe.  
Indebted forever I will be.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda….  
Never allow me to leave you;  
In happiness or distress,  
In East of West,  
Please teach me how to serve you.

Forever surrendered  
At your divine lotus feet,  
Let me follow you eternally,  
Serving Kṛṣṇa and His devotees.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
For coming to save me.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Daily we recite, *Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma kevala bhakati-sadma:* “The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way by which we can attain to pure devotional service.”

Keeping this in my mind, I wanted to bend low and circumambulate your lotus feet and appreciate the bearer of those divine feet.

**By Your Shoes**

By your shoes…..  
The kīrtanas resound.  
Love of God is abound.  
Then life’s all replete,  
Wholesome, perfect, complete.

They are the refuge of all  
Who fell from the fall,  
A relief to God’s kinderen  
From high to low brethren.

They defeat the proud lotus  
And give us the notice  
That nature is pretty  
Though active and steady.

They are cushions for divine feet,  
The base of the elite.  
They distribute their dust  
That wipe out our lust.

They have traveled abroad,  
Embracing earth’s sod,  
And blessed all those creatures  
Of miniature features.

Lord Vāmana had hurled  
Two footsteps— and the world.  
Three footsteps—and King Bali  
Equals your one step on Kali.

When King Rāma was banished  
His shoes reigned (not vanished).  
So to maintain your school  
Your shoes remain the rule.

They are holy and sacred  
And most consecrated.  
Their glories are interlude  
Invoking beatitude.

I tremble and cower  
At great Māyā’s power.  
Without grasping those shoes  
There is nothing to lose.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bīr Krishna das Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this occasion of your glorious appearance day, I pray that I will never develop a sense of independence from you. As you have instructed us to chant the holy names of God in the dependent mood of a child crying out for his mother, I want to be constantly crying out to you for guidance in my spiritual life.

Your books, letters, tapes, etc., must continue to be my shelter from the onslaught of the material energy. Let me ever remain chaste to you and your instructions.

I must have the faith that everything that I will ever need for my spiritual guidance has been given to me by you. There is no need of looking elsewhere, like an unchaste wife looking for satisfaction with a paramour.

My pleasure must be in pleasing you. My unhappiness must be in displeasing you. This simple consciousness I must maintain if I am going to obtain the lotus feet of the Lord.

Please give me the determination to serve you eternally.

*Your servant,*  
Bīr Krishna das Goswami

## Dyutidhara Swami

*samsara-davanala-lidha-loka-*  
*tranaya Karunya-ghanaghanatvam*  
*praptasya kalyana-gunarnavasya*  
*vande guroh Śrī-caranaravindam*

**The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Your inestimable trouble to deliver this wretched soul from this earthly fixation should make this hardened heart cry. However, we are still dauntless in pursuing sense gratification like a tiny insect rushing fearlessly into a fire. Śrīla Prabhupāda, who but your own self would work so hard to drag a suicidal candidate away from the brink of destruction? Only Your Divine Grace would have cared to enter the hearts and hiding places we inhabited, confidently assuring our cringing hearts of Kṛṣṇa’s infinite love. If it were not for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, mirrored in the form of your dear disciple’s determination, our stay in material existence would be interminable.

I pray daily to remember your unassuming sensitivity, keenly attuned to releasing us all from our own misconceptions and profound ignorance of spiritual life. To us you represent everything good and true, a genuine father, protector and all merciful spiritual master. The name **KṚṢṆA** didn’t exist in our lowly vocabulary but a short time ago, but flowing like nectar from your lotus mouth came unlimited elaborations on the Lord’s transcendental name, form, qualities, and pastimes. It is as if you are a great gardener planting innumerable seeds, carefully nourishing them seeing the tender creepers blossom into devotional fruits.

In India we see pilgrims offering their obeisances step by step around the *parikrama* of Vṛndāvana. In a similar way we pray to be able to offer unlimited obeisances at your lotus feet all around the universal manifestation, following your untiring preaching campaign of *Kṛṣṇa-bhakti.* As your divine self has written , “An offering of fruits and flowers does not constitute real worship of the spiritual master.” Therefore please engage us in your devotional service. Kṛṣṇa is yours, and if you like, you can certainly give Him. The bona fide spiritual master has understood all the purports of the Vedic literatures, bathed in all the sacred rivers, and purified all the places of pilgrimage. He is fixed in absolute surrender to the Supreme Lord and constantly makes dynamic arrangements to freely distribute the mercy of the holy name.

In a similar way I pray to distribute the nectar of your holy name, my eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Your glories are like unending waves within an unlimited ocean of devotion.

*Aspiring to associate with the disciples of your disciples’ disciples,*  
Dyutidhara Swami

## Giridhāri Swami

Timely you came, greatest of all masters,  
To rescue us all from spiritual disaster.  
Like sticks we fall in the dust of your lotus feet.

You picked us up from the darkness of ignorance  
And revealed the secret of freeing ourselves from lust, greed and belligerence.  
Taking your words into our hearts, māyā’s advances we will defeat.

Unlike so many others, who present their own speculative thoughts,  
You carried the timeless message that Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvaṭi taught.  
Following this disciplic line, absolute success is guaranteed.

You gave us Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, books, prasadam, temples, and so much more,  
In fact you even built a house for all mankind to live in and adore  
Miraculous feats performed by you, free of envy, anger, pride, and greed.

Your preaching manner was hard as a thunderbolt, soft as a rose;  
All materialists, impersonalists, and voidists you did expose,  
Though all humility, O master of this “chopping technique,” was at your command.

Your transcendental personality was always joyful, warm, and ecstatic;  
Thousands were drawn to you—attraction was automatic.  
Sacred this remembrance, by which the spiritual spark is eternally fanned.

Perfect teacher of the entire universe, you taught by example, not merely by words.  
So convincing this method, that hearing form others was simply absurd,  
The highest knowledge, in the simplest form, you made available for all without restriction.

Mahāprabhu’s mission you spread all over the world;  
In every town and village the nama-saṅkīrtana banner began to unfurl.  
Only you had the power to fulfill Śrīla Bhaktivinoda’s prediction.

Now is the time to strengthen our vow,  
To offer body, mind, word, and at your lotus feet bow.  
Our only prayer is that you grant us this benediction:

Śrīla Prabhupāda—the perfection o life is to constantly glorify and remember you;  
We cannot imagine living even a moment without you.  
Let us unite together and faithfully serve your most glorious ISKCON mission.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Giridhāri Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, please allow me to offer this humble prayer at your lotus feet:

You are my eternal, divine master. May I never forget that anything I know of any value, anything I have of any value, anything I do of any value, it is all coming from you. You have perfectly demonstrated how to plunder the storehouse of love of God and eat and distribute its contents. I pray to become an instrument for delivering to others this voluminous amount of mercy that you, Lord Caitanya’s exemplary follower, have bestowed upon me. And I pray that you please help me kick out the miserly mentality within my heart that is preventing me from doing so.

*yāre dekha, tare kaha “Kṛṣṇa”-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra ei deśa*

**Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*** **In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.” (*****Cc Madhya-līlā,*** **7.128)**

You have installed within us this mandate of Lord Caitanya’s imploring us to distribute your books to everyone and to help them become devotees. I pray to remember your tireless, unmotivated dedication to this mandate and to the order of your spiritual master and, in this way, not hide behind the poor example of those who somehow manage to excuse themselves from being in the forefront of your mission.

You are the maser of masters, “Śrīla Prabhupāda,” and by some stroke of mercy upon me you have entered my life. I pray to become free from my lethargy and feel you pushing me as you did when you were personally present. You made it very difficult for me to be complacent the. I therefore beg you to again please force me to be a useful soldier in your transcendental army, for I would much rather feel the pain of surrender than the pain of being a worthless disciple.

So many of my Godbrothers have valiantly taken up the struggle to carry out your mission in your absence. By their selfless surrender to your will they are clearly earning a place at your lotus feet. Whereas many others have fallen back into the material abyss, these stalwarts are passing the test of time. They are the true devotees, unlike my pretending self, who am always contemplating the vast material ocean before me and thinking how nice it would be to jump in. My only hope is that I can render some humble service at the feet of these surrendered devotees and take shelter of you through their staunch association, as they march steadfastly under your protection.

Praying that you continue to maintain me, I remain…

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This year, in the wake of a disasterthat claimed a friend  
And brought the walls crumbling down,  
I took up my staff and looked to the road,  
Hoping to find you again.

To gain your favor I gave up all that I owned  
And tried to renounce my pride,  
And, ever more cautious of women and fame,  
I traveled fat and wide.

Moving once in the north and twice in the south  
And east and west in turn,  
I learned firsthand of God’s creation.  
And your kind mercy as well.

Soon austerity came, claiming all of my wealth,  
But remained a welcome friend,  
And strength I gained, and detachment too,  
While traveling through foreign lands.

With no place to live, or a home of my own,  
I learned to take shelter in you,  
And the constant vision of birth and death  
Kept me learned and true to my vows.

In forests and cities, villages and towns,  
Repeating your words I roamed,  
Witnessing your mercy, as kind as you are,  
In delivering the fallen and poor.

Disease came in summer, and near death in the fall,  
In a jungle far to the south.  
But your saving grace and Nrsimha’s mace  
Kept me safe and protected through all.

Oh, how happy I am to serve you like this,  
Always preaching and traveling on!  
As a flowing river remains always clean,  
I pray to remain always pure.

In happiness and distress, in heat and cold,  
I want to keep fighting on,  
Delivering your message, as you asked me to,  
While I’m enthusiastic, youthful, and strong.

But Prabhupāda, the road is long,  
And there are many dangers in between  
Where I pause now….  
And you rest safely on the other shore.

So in this day, so full of grace,  
Please hear my fervent prayer:  
Within my heart, beyond what’s dark,  
There’s a shining love for you.

So guide me right, keep me in the fight,  
And away from Māyā’s glare,  
And when all is done, and the battle’s won,  
Take me home to be with you again.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this day of your appearance, we offer you what you offered us—the chanting of the *Mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare hare / Hare Rāmā , Hare Rāmā, Rāmā Rāmā, Hare Hare.

You offered it to us pure; our offering to you is tainted, covered by offenses. Please help us get free from offenses so we can serve you purely, as we saw you serve you r *Guru* Mahārāja.

You chanted purely, the perfectly determined servant of your spiritual master. You loved the holy name -Hare Kṛṣṇa-and had full faith in this transcendental sound. You knew that Kṛṣṇa was everything, and that everything else was nothing.

You gave us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not merely as sentiment but as science. You taught us precisely how to understand Kṛṣṇa, how to serve Kṛṣṇa, and how to go back to Kṛṣṇa.

May Śrī Rādhā-Govinda grant us the privilege to go on forever, trusting in you, following in your lotus footsteps, honoring your words, and chanting your glories.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna das Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

My earliest memories stem from our first, but probably the most momentous, meeting in 1973. I was entangled in householder life at that time, but trying to preach *sanātan-dharma* in Southeast London. In that meeting, I asked you many personal questions about your birth, parentage, education, initiation, marriage, children, and about your spiritual master. You also asked me the same questions. We discussed my being initiated into the Brahma-sampradāya in my youth. After that, you started to preach to me very strongly and went on preaching for almost two hours, convincing me to join your movement. At first I was very much surprised to hear your suggestion. But you answered all my questions and allayed all my doubts. At last, I had to surrender, and I requested you to become my *sannyāsa-guru.* Being merciful, you immediately accepted my request. Since the, I have been trying to serve Your Divine Grace in ISKCON under your guidance and blessings.

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvanmanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.” (*****Bhāg.*** **11.17.27)**

Śrīla Jiva Gosvami comments that a pure devotee’s observation of the spiritual master as one with the Personality of Godhead exists in terms of his being very dear to the Lord, not identical to Him in all respects. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Thākura confirms that al the revealed scriptures accept the spiritual master to be identical with the Personality of Godhead because he is a very dear and confidential servant of the Lord.

The *ācārya* is the representative of all the demigods. He is described as follows:

*Gurur brahmā gurur Viṣṇur gurur devo maheśvaraḥ*  
*gūruḥ sākṣād paraṁ brahman tasmai śrī-gurave namah*

**“The spiritual master is respected as being as good as Lord Brahmā, Lord Śiva and Lord Vishnu, for he is considered to be directly representing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

Considering the miraculous changes that I witnessed within myself from my fortuitous meeting with Your Divine Grace, and seeing the transformations you have inspired in so many other devotees the world over, I would also like to describe you in terms of the powers of the three *guṇāvatāras.*

Just as Lord Śiva accepts all fallen souls and delivers them by making them Vaiṣṇava, similarly, you very mercifully accepted those born in Western countries and addicted to all types of abominable activities as your disciples. Through your preaching you destroyed their demoniac mentality.

As Lord Brahma is responsible for creation within the material universe, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, create divine qualities in your sincere followers.

You not only destroy the demoniac mentality and create divine qualities, but through your books, instructions, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, you maintain our spiritual lives just as Lord Viṣṇu maintains the cosmic manifestation.

So by your marvelous achievements, you have proved yourself as good as Lord Śiva, as good as Lord Brahmā, and as good as Lord Śrī Hari. But of all your achievements, the fact that you have picked me up from rotting in material life remains the most miraculous to me.

Please continue to curb my demoniac mentality, to create the qualities of a devotee in me, and to maintain me in my humble efforts to serve you as you requested me.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Krishna das Swami

## Lokanātha Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances on this auspicious day of your appearance. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In October 1977, I was traveling in North India with our Śrīla Nārada Muni Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party, preaching, distributing books, and holding festivals. We received news that your health was getting worse, and that many devotees from around the world had gone to Vṛndāvana, so we also returned to Vṛndāvana. Soon after arriving, we had the opportunity to visit your quarters and to have your association. You were very delighted to hear of our *saṅkīrtana* activities: how much we were collecting daily, which book we were distributing the most, how the party was being maintained, etc. After quite some time we had spent with Your Divine Grace, it was time to leave, so we began to exit slowly. Everyone else had gone, and I was just about to walk out the door when you called me back and asked me to come close to your bedside. You said to me, “I want to talk to you. What time is suitable for you?” I replied that there was no question of my convenience, and you asked me to come at 4:00 P.M. that afternoon.

All day I was wondering what you wanted to see me about. I tried to think of various topics that seemed relevant and came up with many possibilities, but actually I was quite bewildered and couldn’t come to any definite conclusion. You had spoken to me in a very grave and serious way, and your humility was beyond my imagination. I waited anxiously for the 4:00 P.M. meeting, and when the time came I approached your quarters, only to be redirected to the guesthouse reception by your servant.

There, upon entering, I found many senior Vaiṣṇava in conference. Among them were some of your personal staff and secretaries, and it was obvious to me that whatever you had wanted to discus with me, you had already disclosed the same to them. They told me that you wanted to go traveling by bullock cart—to begin with, just around Vṛndāvana—and that you wanted me to make the arrangements. So I went to Mathura, accompanied by Pancadravida Swami and Trivikrāmā Swami, and, after talking with several bullock cart owners, we finally arranged for two carts to be used by you and your party.

These two carts had come that evening and were parked in front of the Kṛṣṇa-Balaram Mandira. They were all ready to get on the road next morning. That night I tried to take rest, but I was so excited and anxious to sleep. All I could think of was you. That same night, however, some of your dear disciples had begged at your lotus feet that you not jeopardize your health by going on the bullock cart, and after much discussion, you had finally agreed not to go. Some days passed, and then again you brought up the subject of traveling and preaching. You disliked lying on your bed; you had been there for months, confined to your quarters. Now you wanted to go out again and physically lead the *saṅkīrtana* mission. You had always said that you wanted to die on the battlefield.

This time you spoke not of using bullock carts, but of motor vehicles. You talked about having a main group of vehicles, with an advance party going ahead and arranging accommodation, reception, advertising, etc. After lengthy discussion on the topic, devotees started to plead with you that they too be allowed to go along with you. I remember Giriraja Swami saying to you that while traveling to these big towns and cities, he could do nice membership preaching, cultivate wealthy people, get donations, etc. You nodded in approval. Bhaktisvarūpa Damodara told you that in all the big cities there would be scientists, and that he could arrange conferences with them along the way. You also blessed him to go with you. So, like this, a lot of devotees took turns to appeal to you, and in turn you agreed to accommodate them all with you on your *saṅkīrtana* party. I also had asked you if I could join this party, and you replied “Yes, you will be our leader.” We had concluded that this traveling program would begin in about two weeks’ time after further recovery in your health. At this point everyone was happy for two reasons: firstly, the fact that you were making plans to travel and preach clearly indicated that you were going to stay longer on the planet with us, and secondly, everyone was looking forward to the nectar of having your divine association as we traveled with you.

In preparation, we purchased some maps of India and began working out the route, as well as arranging for vehicles and other necessities. Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami, Bhāvānanda Prabhu, myself and others were busy making the arrangements. However, in the morning of November 14th, the Kavirāja spread the prediction of your leaving us once and for all. And, that same evening, Your Divine Grace passed into s*amadhi.* Some days after your departure, in order to fulfill your desire, we arranged bullock carts and performed *parikrama* of Govardhana Hill, carrying a portrait of Your Divine Grace.

After you left us, in 1978 I made some attempts to organize a type of program you had in mind; but myself being a small-timer, it never came to pass. Then years came and went by, making it more and more difficult to undertake such a program. However, then Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s centennial celebration was coming up. ISKCON leaders were contemplating some grand scheme to glorify Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Ultimately, leaders unanimously settled for a *padayatra* (walking festival) to travel on the same route that Lord Caitanya had taken five hundred years ago. It was a case of “Better late than never.” After seven long years, we were ready to execute your unfulfilled desire of taking you, along with Their Lordship Śrī Śrī Gaura Nitāi and Their followers, chanting the holy names and distributing your books all over India. And I was surprised to find myself as the leader of the program once again. Everything was happening just the way you desired, so we started. Originally the plan was to go only up to Māyāpur, but with your blessings we were able to go beyond Māyāpur, and after passing through several more states and visiting many, many holy places, including Vraja-mandala, Badarikāśrama, Kurukṣetra, Puṣkara, Nāthadvāra, we were able to return after three and a half years to Dvārakā. Ordinarily, people these days are not even prepared to walk only fourteen kilometers, what to speak of walking fourteen thousand kilometers. Just the other day I read an advertisement in the newspaper. It read “Chār (four) Dhām air yātrā.” Several of your followers have successfully completed Chār Dhām Padayātrā. This is simply your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are naturally reminded of the verse,

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*paramānanda-mādhavam*  
*Madhya 17.80*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains. Such is the mercy of the spiritual master.”**

We just finished Padayātrā’s return to the Dvārakā festival. We have built an ISKCON Padayātrā gate. This monument will further glorify your followers’ attempt of the historical fourteen-thousand-kilometer Padayātrā. Padayātrīs seem not to be tired at all. They are back on the road again. They are determined to carry on walking and broadcasting your glories in every town and village in India, as well as all over the world, at least up until your one hundredth birth anniversary day.

On this auspicious occasion of your divine appearance, I simply beg unto you to keep giving us the blessing so that we never become falsely proud for whatever little something we may have done for you.

*I beg to remain your servant,*  
Lokanātha Swami

## Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Somewhere a madman  
Was looking for a jewel  
In a filthy, forgotten place,  
In untouched beds of lonely streams,  
Hoping blindly to find the rarest gem.

But you, O spiritual master divine,  
Your kindness knew no bound,  
Brought precious cargo across the sea,  
Sent your couriers to every land  
With a gift divine to every madman’s door.  
(O wonderous tale to tell,  
And tell we must, again)  
How you brought within your heart  
The precious holy name.

The gift was done, the contract made,  
And then you did depart.  
What was that you said? Do we forget,  
By Māyā being torn apart?

Age upon age, down through time,  
In parampara’s mighty voice,  
The order came to chant the name,  
“Chant the name! Chant the name! Chant the name!”

ĪśvaraPurī told Lord Caitanya  
In Bihar’s Gaya town,  
“Mūrkha tumi , You are a fool!  
Just let the names resound!  
Dance and sing with devotees,  
Tell them of Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
Let this formula be Your way  
To deliver men in every clime!”

Often you would mutter  
In gravely lowered breath,  
Harer nāma eva kevalam,  
But still we tend to doubt,  
“There is some other way?” we ask  
And vainly look about.

In every book, on every page,  
In every speech, your same refrain,  
“Just chant and be happy!”  
(O simple faith!)  
For that (so heavy), you came.

We children talk, and wonder how  
“Whole world in just one House?”  
The answer you give,  
nāma-cintāmaṇi-Kṛṣṇa.  
“Kṛṣṇa’s name is the blissful giver  
Of all spiritual benedictions.”

Without a doubt, we know not how,  
But by the grace of Kṛṣṇa,  
By telling our beads and telling the name,  
Spreading Lord Kṛṣṇa’s fame,  
With offenseless chanting  
Success is assured.  
Kṛṣṇa is ajita, who can doubt?  
Victory is assured.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our master divine,  
Please bless us with a little faith  
To take your gift and try and try and try, again,  
To chant without offense the holy name.  
If you are pleased, then all is well,  
And Kṛṣṇa will dissolve this Māyā’s spell.

We are still so wretched,  
A helpless brood,  
But please make us, now,  
Your madman of the holy name.

Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

## Mahābhāgavata dāsa Goswami

*nārāyaṇa-parā viprā*  
*dharmaṁ guhyaṁ paraṁ viduḥ*  
*karuṇāḥ sādhavaḥ śāntās*  
*tvad-vidhā na thatāpare*

**“No one is superior to you in peaceful life and mercy, and no one knows better than you how to execute devotional service or how to become the best of the** ***brahmanas.*** **Therefore you know all the principles of confidential religious life, and no one knows them better than you.” (*****Bhāg.*** **7.11.4)**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. I would like to have the inspiration of Yudhiṣṭḥira Mahārāja even for a moment to be able to mold with words what my poor heart would like to express when I am about to offer my prayers.

At the time of writing this offering of Vyāsa-pūjā, I realized how much we are lacking good epithets, knowing all your innumerable qualities that we find in your divine person. I do not possess these qualities at all, due to my low birth, and therefore you cannot expect much from me, just as you have written, “How can the limited describe the unlimited?” However, inspired by Your Divine Grace, we are trying hard to maintain our Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Dear *Guru*deva, you state clearly in your Bhaktivedanta purport that Yudhisthira Mahārāja knew that Narada Muni was the spiritual master for the whole world, for you have fulfilled the most difficult task of saving so many fallen souls worse than thousands and thousands of Mṛgāris, turning them into Vaiṣṇava. And it is not even necessary that a great soul like Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja declare it, because at this moment, in a practical way, we see how Narada Muni’s greatest aspirations have been fulfilled. The greatest longing of the disciplic succession is already being fulfilled by your divine person through this *saṅkīrtana* movement, and we, in spite of being so fallen and degraded, are trying to continue spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

*yāṅra citte kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*

**Even the most learned man cannot understand the word, activities and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead. (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **23.39)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this small appreciation as an homage on the auspicious day of your appearance in this world, and even though we may never be able to pay off your debt to you for your coming into our lives, we ask you one more time to please maintain us. As you see, every day and every moment we need your causeless mercy.

*Your most useless servant,*  
Mahābhāgavata dāsa Goswami

## Mahānidhi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are everything to me. Your presence in my life makes life worth living. You are my eternal guide, forever instructing me how to live for pleasing Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By acting for Kṛṣṇa’s happiness one avoids rebirth in this temporary world of continued sadness.

You are my daily shelter, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for I have given up family members, relatives, and all mundane friends. I turn to you often and pray, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, please protect me from all illusions and material attachments. Without your mercy I am lost.”

You have created a network of shelter in the form of the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. This global-sized fortress is complete with fences, moats, walls, barricades, and sentinels in the form of your transcendental books, *murtis,* lecture tapes, and *bhajanas;* Kṛṣṇa’s temples, deities, holy names, *prasadam,* farms, devotees; and full engagement in the service of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa. In all these ways I always feel completely protected by you.

You are the best friend because you never leave me alone and are always eager to help me with comforting words and divine instructions. I can reveal my mind to you in full faith….and trust that our relationship is confidential and completely personal.

Your pleasure is the goal of my daily activities. I will know if my attempts are successful, because of that special feeling a disciple perceives within his heart when he pleases his spiritual master. As I have experienced this world, I conclude that there is no feeling that compares with the transcendental happiness obtained by satisfying you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the controller of my heart. By your desire I move in surrender. Your mission to save suffering humanity is my mission too. Although I severely lack the requisite Vaiṣṇava compassion and devotional purity, feebly, by your grace, I am trying.

As my eternal spiritual master and teacher, you have pulled me from darkness to light. From the most basic activities of bodily maintenance to the most sublime meditations of remembering Kṛṣṇa’s loving pastimes, you have taught me everything. From a life of educated zeros, you have given my life value by the wealth of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Whatever materialistic education I had simply deepened my absorption in sinful activities and reactions of suffering. I didn’t even know how to eat properly, what to speak of worshiping Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. You expertly taught me how to honor Kṛṣṇa’s *prasadam* with my fingers, and with the same hand you showed me how to anoint the beautiful faces of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Syamasundara with sandalwood paste.

By seeing pictures of you engaged in various spiritual activities. I feel great happiness. Each picture awakens different responses, like love, respect, humor, awe, appreciation, and faith—leaning back against a tree, white-sheeted cushion underneath, your Himalayan knee propped up, and your pointed index finger powerfully preaching from its silken shelter within your bead bag.

The hundreds of pictures of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are wonderful to see and meditate upon. The camera’s eye never caught you forgetting Kṛṣṇa. These captured moments of your pure life give graphic proof that you never forgot Kṛṣṇa even for one-thousandth of a second. Although eyes may blink, within, you are constantly seeing your beloved Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Our solidly frozen hearts begin to drop a few tears when we watch video films of you. Seeing you in this medium overwhelms our hearts with deep impressions. We witness your awesome preaching powers, graceful gait, regal yet unassuming countenance, and firm adherence to the absolute truth in all circumstances. We never tire of seeing you as the *ācārya* and find complete shelter in “Your ever well-wisher.”

We sing out our hearts every morning, as you benevolently sit upon your crimson *vyāsāsana*. In surprise the *karmīs* say, “Oh, is he alive? Is *Guru*jī going to speak today?” Of course, we know you are alive and are speaking to us every day. Humbly we supplicate you, begging forgiveness for our offenses. Please allow us to serve you for another day. Please fulfill our one and only desire to unconditionally love you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Giving our hearts to you, we meditate upon your divine mercy and pray sincerely, “Prabhupāda, I am a completely undeserving disciple, but please protect me. I am poor, wretchedly fallen, and foolish, but please give me your association.” As the *kirtana* increases, our minds race in remembrance through the files of five thousand *guru-pujas.* Sometimes stopping briefly and relishing, we enjoy memorable *Guru-puja kirtanas.*

Los Angeles 1975: Jumping as high as possible we try to catch a second’s glance from Your Divine Grace. You smile, sweetly reciprocating, and gradually move your right index finger toward the sky. That is all the signal we need. Immediately, each disciple, empowered by love for you, exhibits Hanumān-like leaping power. Bounding off the walls and ceiling we cry out your name, “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!”

The *gopis* cursed Lord Brahma for creating imperfect eyes that blinked and momentarily removed Kṛṣṇa from their sight. In a similar way, I curse my ears, for sometimes they drink from pots of poison-sound instead of the sweet vibrations of your lotus voice. For the most part I never tire of hearing your speaking and singing on tape. Upon waking, through the day and before sleeping, I hear your speaking to me via your cassette-tape expansions.

So much knowledge, perfect guidance, and sheer spiritual satisfaction emanate from your lecture and *bhajana* tapes. Sometimes I think to sail away to a secluded island and listen nonstop to your pure devotional singing and philosophical preaching. But being very kind to me, you continually implore less selfish thoughts. By your aural encouragement, I try again and again to help others by telling them what I have just heard from you.

In these ways I attempt in my own small way to stay connected with you. It is not difficult to feel your benign association as long as we really desire it. You are very much here with us in many different forms, all available to the sincere in heart. Gradually, I am beginning to realize that the essence of all instructions is to always remember Śrīla Prabhupāda and never forget him.

*Your servant,*  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Majāśṛṅga Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Without having a proper education, undergone conscientious austerity, and lacking spiritual purity, it is not possible for me to glorify you properly. But since you have so kindly accepted me as your disciple, it is my duty to try….
