Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are mercifully representing the Supreme Personality of Godhead and have actually instilled fearlessness into our hearts by explaining how the surrendered devotees are given complete protection by the Lord. Fearfulness is the result of material involvement and being in a bodily concept of life. And because we were completely absorbed in thinking that we were these material bodies, we were anxious and scared. But you have taught us *bhagavad-bhakti-yoga,* so that we can become fearless of this material world. You have stopped our “monkey” business and shown us our identity as spirit soul. By engaging us in devotional service, you have made us joyful and happy for the first time in our lives, which is a debt impossible to repay.

Because you are not influenced by the material modes of nature and are eternally beyond the material manifestation, your descent from the spiritual world was only to reclaim we fallen souls who are captivated by the illusory energy. You broke the chains of our lusty fallacies with transcendental knowledge, bringing sanity into our lives again.

Thank you so much for your unlimited rain of compassion upon our steel framed suffering hearts, and for injecting spiritual information into our dull brains, extinguishing our fire of trying to savor this world. We were actually being attacked by the material energy, trying to enjoy her under false pretenses, until your all-merciful nature showed us the absurdity of running after an illusion.

You have arisen like the sun of Lord Caitanya to dissipate the darkness of *māyā* and release us from the clutches of nescience. By informing us of our real, constitutional position, you have changed our desire for material happiness in the form of society, friendship, and love to a yearning for association with a pure devotee. And not just any pure devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It’s you we want to associate with forever, because if somehow or other we can remain at your soothing lotus feet, we will always be hearing about the pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world.

All glories, all glories, all glories to your eternal transcendental pastimes. Śrīla Prabhupāda! May we always have the intelligence to listen with attention. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

*Your second-rate disciple,*  
Mahāśṛṅga Swami

## Prahlādananda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

One day, as I was fanning Your Divine Grace, I experienced that you were submerged in a mood of compassion when you said, “We simply want to see that people are happy.” Since people can be happy only in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the method of fulfilling your pure desire is to give the innocent people in general knowledge of how to revive their awareness of and appreciation for Lord Kṛṣṇa. Charity, as you also confirmed, begins at home, and so I must also give up envy born of ignorance and base my relationship with Kṛṣṇa not on the business of fulfilling my own personal desires, but out of genuine love for Him and His pure devotee.

A serious offense in devotional service is blaspheming devotees who are engaged in devotional service. What is constructive criticism and what is offensive, we must judge by *guru, sadhu, and śāstra,* and ultimately by its results—my aim should be to serve Kṛṣṇa and His devotees, not to unnecessarily disturb them. I can advance rapidly in devotional service by becoming the servants of His servants, and fall quickly by offending them. Giving up all unnecessary criticism, I can begin to develop a hearty appreciation for the big and seemingly not-so-big services that Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotees render to Him. I cannot taste the amount of love Kṛṣṇa devotees place in their offering, but you and Kṛṣṇa can. If I serve you and Kṛṣṇa’s devotees, I can make advancement in devotional service and give up my entanglement with Māyā. Let me follow your example and instructions and become a true friend to anyone who even renders the smallest service to Kṛṣṇa through your movement.

You have explained to your disciples that to expand their love of Kṛṣṇa further and further, they must preach by creating a world revolution to convince people that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. How do you do this? By art, by music, by theatre, and especially by distributing your books, and by any and every means. Let people know that Kṛṣṇa is God and if we serve Him and His instructions everyone can live peacefully and happily, make progress in life, and at the end go back to the spiritual world. Let us, your followers, compete amongst ourselves to serve you and Kṛṣṇa, who is the source of everything we hanker for. The more we become expert, the more we can engage our mind, senses, and intelligence fully in this work; indeed, we can engage all the Vaiṣṇava in this cultural revolution, then perhaps all religious groups. Let us conquer our atheistic minds and whimsical senses by conquering the world for you and Kṛṣṇa. “Work now, s*amādhi* later.”

*Begging to become your servant,*  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Raghunātha Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which are more cooling than millions of moons.

Where would we be without your mercy? We shudder to think of that fate worse than death—to be cut off from the pure devotee of the Lord. We are not great scholars, neither are we powerful *yogis.* We are simply the denizens of the age of Kali, who have, somehow or other, received your grace. Without you we are lost in the modes of passion and ignorance.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (7.5.32), Prahlāda Mahārāja states:

*Naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*Sprśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*Mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-‘bhiṣekaṁ*  
*Niṣkiñcanānām na vṛṇīta yāvat*

**“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from the material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for his uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa consciousness and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination.”**

In the purport, you explain that one must surrender to the pure devotee, thus taking shelter of a realized soul whose only interest is to engage fully in the service of the Lord. This is your prescription for spiritual health in this darkened age of spontaneous insanity. Without following in the footsteps of the great souls, we are bewildered.

In *The Nectar of Devotion* you so expertly describe the process of *sādhana-bhakti* and how such rigid execution can lead one to the platform of love. Interestingly enough, you use words like MUST, ORDER, WITHOUT ARGUMENT, etc. This is because you know, and you know because you have seen. You have full realization on all these points, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and are patiently guiding us towards becoming human beings. Yet we meet so many persons engulfed in the web of stringent *māyā* without the slightest inkling of actual reality. Therefore, what is the solution? How can we reach them? By giving them your association. Nothing else can save them except your divine instructions present within your books. You yourself are residing within your books, and it is up to us to make you available by profusely distributing them. Please give us the determination to face all odds and give our lives as you gave your for the pleasure of the Supreme Lord.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Raghunātha Swami

## Romapāda Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*  
*(Madhya 19.151)*

The seed of devotional service, or rather, the jewel more valuable than the *vaidūrya-maṇi* by the mine-load, given so freely by Your Divine Grace, is our most cherished possession. No. It is our only possession! What else can we claim to be ours? What else would we *care* to claim as ours, in comparison *or* in fact?

Please grant the power of remembrance that our *only* possession is not only the most valuable property in the soul’s existence but is exclusively, singularly a gift from you. How much we are indebted!

As the transcendental gardener, through your instructions you are cultivating this tiny seed of *bhakti.* Neglect of your instructions is fatal, as we can vividly see. But the potency of your instructions becomes gloriously revealed when followed. As the Lord’s glories are realized and appreciated by His devotees exclusively, so also are yours, as His representative. As the giver of the seed of *bhakti* with the greatest compassion, you have so liberally bestowed devotional service unto Lord Kṛṣṇa throughout the world in such an unprecedented scale that millions upon millions are your admirers, your worshipers, your devotees.

This seed of *bhakti* is not static. It continues to grow and grow, by your mercy. Again, this is further proof of your transcendental potency: as the years pass, even in your “absence” your presence becomes stronger. See how, within your ISKCON society, as your presence grows in the lives of *all* the institution’s members with you in the foreground as the founder-*ācārya,* life returns to the body. The continued bearing of fruit of the seed of devotion shows that it cannot be compared to any mundane entity, subject to material causes, nor stopped by any temporary display of the material energy.

As Dhruva Mahārāja was approaching the Lord for some material objective, we also initially came before you. Having no *real* sense of value of devotional service, your appearance was like that of Lord Viṣṇu’s before Dhruva. Lord Viṣṇu’s mere presence was so purifying that Dhruva forgot his former dreams of “broken glass” and felt he had instead achieved the rarest gem. And you have also given us the same gem. Yet your appearance and your gemlike gift seem to me to be even more glorious; by your grace this great gift of *bhakti-lata-bija* that you have given have made us into devotees; and you continue, through the same transcendental power, to drag us further along the path back to Godhead—as long as we follow your instructions.

You have taught us, “This principle is so simple, even a child can take part.” We are your children, Śrīla Prabhupāda, trying to become mature sons and daughters carrying on your mission, the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Let us stick to the “simple,” as per your instructions; in this way we can all “take part” with you (or wherever you may have us go) in the only joyful position of your divine service.

*Your most unworthy servant,*  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

**Calling Out to Śrīla Prabhupāda**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, who stands bravely between his devotees and death, may we not desert our places in the phalanx of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army, where you have placed us;

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, the waves of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* were spread by you much further than the waves made directly by Gaura-Nitāi, although you are simply extending Their mercy, and your mercy is also the personified grace of Bhaktivinoda Thākura and Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī;

O true teacher, who never invented theories to surpass the original truth, but who handed the delicate and delicious mango-truth of Gaudiya Vaiṣṇavism and passed it into the hands of thousands of hungry followers.

O transplant of the *tulasī* of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your personal work continues now and will grow in importance into the twenty-first century and beyond that, as willed by Lord Caitanya;

O Prabhupāda, you are the representative of all *bhaktivedāntas,* but you are an individual, rare soul. You kindly came to the Lower East Side of New York and acted humbly, as if it were something not impossible, and yet you performed *kirtana* and cooked and spoke as no one before or since has done.

O Prabhupāda, where is there purity such as yours? Where is the dedication to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa such as yours? Where is there an example of such a benefactor as you? O Prabhupāda, in your absence what can we do? Please enlighten us again, take us by the hand, and lead us through the dark of Kali-yuga.

O Prabhupāda, whose followers never tire of praising him because he is their *prāṇa* (life-breath);

O Prabhupāda, who is like the cow that protects the devotee-calves, and who is like a policeman to catch the Māyāvādī thieves by the throats—all glories to your actions in this world;

O master, whose instructions bind us to the regulative principles of devotional service, whose timely presence in our lives has saved us from descending to painful lower species of life, who reinforces the teachings of the previous *ācāryas,* and who says “No!” to illicit desires;

O Prabhupāda, who invites us to the Sunday *prasadam* feast of many excellent preparations such as spiced *sabjīs,* tender *puris,* and sweet-rice and *halavā,* all prepared personally by him and his servants, and who induces us to “Take more!” until we forget all our wayward desires, being satisfied with Kṛṣṇa *prasadam;*

O Prabhupāda, who leads us from event to event, like the gracious host of a festival, who points the way as the leading explorer on the expedition and who waits for us at the destination in Kṛṣṇa-loka—may we never lose connection to you.

O Prabhupāda, who appeared as the saintly, elderly *guru* of the younger generation, who wore simple *sannyāsī* clothes but accepted gifts like wool sweaters, inexpensive shoes, a gold ring, a watch, but who soon gave away the these few possessions to his followers, who treasure the remnants as *mahā-prasādam;*

O Prabhupāda, who went to England to teach what they had forgotten—which is God—who taught the world the prime importance of God consciousness, and the knowledge of *how* God is great, the knowledge of *Bhagavad-gītā—*and who revealed that God is the all-attractive Supreme Person, Kṛṣṇa.

O Prabhupāda, who reveled the hidden *avatāra,* Lord Caitanya, and His covered Vṛndāvana, Śrīdhāma Māyāpur, and who continues to reveal to us the way to fulfill the innermost desires of our hearts—our intimate loving exchange with Śrī Kṛṣṇa;

O master of the esoteric arts of *bhakti,* O practical provider of our daily needs, O *guru,* who is always the master and whose followers know that they are always the foolish disciples, please don’t let us drift away from you, your books, and your Kṛṣṇa conscious society of devotees.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, whom we think of day and night;

O Prabhupāda, who came to America with *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* as his only means and who sold volumes to bookstores in order to pay for groceries, and who thought in the beginning, “They will never accept this Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* but let me try”;

O Prabhupāda, who happily endured the austerities of New York winters on behalf of Lord Kṛṣṇa; O master, who years later made thousands of disciples and had many houses to reside in but who said, “I was happier in the beginning in New York because I had no one to depend on but Kṛṣṇa”;

O Prabhupāda, who favored New York City by opening his first ISKCON center there and by singing in Tompkins Square Park, who beat the one-headed drum hours at a time and sang strongly, who braved all the rudeness and strangeness just to deliver us from birth and death by giving us the holy names of Kṛṣṇa;

O Prabhupāda, whose preaching was guided by Lord Kṛṣṇa, whose preaching was “to go in like a needle and come out like a plow,” whose preaching was pure, and who stayed to do it; who fulfilled all the qualities of a saint such as tolerance, mercy, friend to all, and fixed in the absolute truth;

O Prabhupāda, who loved his disciples and nurtured them like a mother cares for her children, and who like a father imparted to his sons and daughters the gift of courage to stand and fight; O Prabhupāda, please live vibrantly in our thoughts and actions.

Satsvarūpa das Goswami

## Somaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Already over ten years have passed in your absence. So many years gone, so many things happened, but actually you have not left us. You are here in your *vāṇi* form; the *śāstras* indicate that we can associate by *vapuḥ,* physical presence, or by *vāṇī,* the instruction. You r instructions are there in your books, your letters, and so many tapes, so we just have to read and learn, and we will feel your presence at every moment.

Some say that now you are not here we have to go to another personality to take encouragement, but I can’t accept that, because that will be like saying that you are not present in your books and I don’t read anything else. I just try to serve and cooperate with my Godbrothers and with the body you have created and left as authoritative; nothing else! Let them call me brainwashed—I do not care, and actually I wish my brain to be washed, because it is filled with trash after so many lives in the material world.

You are my father. How can one leave his father? I pray to Kṛṣṇa to keep this illusion away from me. They say I need to associate with advanced Vaiṣṇava, and I approve. But who has opened hundreds of temples all over the world? Who has made thousands of devotees? Who has dedicated his whole life to save the world? You! You! And only you! So you are by far the greatest Vaiṣṇava, so where is the need to look for others when we have the greatest father? And you are not distant from us; I just need to raise my hand and pick one of your books from the shelf and read, and there you are.

They say: We need more pastimes, more nectar. But the sweetest nectar is the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya, so we must distribute more books, do more *harer nāma,* preach vigorously, as you preached, and then we will drown in the ocean of nectar.

We are your sons, and so we should follow your mood. And your mood was “Preach! Preach! Preach! Hard work now; *samādhi* later.”

Only you had the strong desire to save all the world. I can’t see a greater Vaiṣṇava than you in all of history, what to speak of nowadays!

You are my only father. I can never accept another. I can’t share my heart with anybody else—it belongs to you exclusively.

“Your love for me will be shown by how you work and cooperate together.” This was your last message to us. You left thousands of us, but now many are scattered here and there, and we are all looking for faults. Certainly everybody has shortcomings, but if we have love for you, we will find a way to stay together, to forgive, to admit our faults, and to keep you as the only father!

On this auspicious day of your appearance, I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa to give me the mercy of always keeping me as your faithful servant, and to make sure that my heart does not deviate from your lotus feet.

Please accept, millions of times, my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

*Your most foolish servant,*  
Somaka Swami

## Śrīdhar Swami

Anxiety again,  
No place to hide.  
Can this be spiritual life?  
Is ISKCON bona fide?

Who wants to manage?  
Set it all aside;  
My life’s now half over.  
Got to renovate my pride.

Establish my identity,  
Let all devotees know  
I’m marching to Pretoria—  
Just call me Major Domo.

But just before I embark  
On such a glorious campaign,  
Reflect, O conditioned soul;  
For whom is the refrain?

Who gave you life?  
Who gave you rhyme?  
Who gave you all the reason  
To pass away your time

It is for him that now you live,  
And for him you’ll die,  
And in the time that’s in between,  
To spread his mission you must try.

Whatever is the needful,  
Just see the time and place,  
Measure carefully your cadence  
Lest your father be disgraced.

Anxiety is piety  
When his pleasure is the goal.  
Be steady now; it’s not the time  
To hoist your flag upon a pole.

You always hated duty,  
But now you’re not your own.  
You’re a slave upon the master’s ship,  
Headed back to home.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is all you have,  
And he is using you  
In his master plan to save the world,  
So surrender all you do.

It may involve a little trouble,  
You may have to get through pain,  
But when you think of what he’s given,  
You surely can’t complain.

Just follow the example  
Of all your learned brothers.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda will guide you  
To transcend your meager druthers.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this insignificant beggar as your servant**.** Although I am practically *āsara,* or useless, due to my deep-rooted lust and envy, somehow I have recognized one thing: Your mission will be successful. Some foolish persons have claimed that there is a difference between you, your mission, and ISKCON as you set it up. Using this as an excuse, they find fault and then leave to create another institution, supposedly free from faults. For my part I could never be able to tolerate having your prestige diminished by the failure of the society you so painstakingly endeavored to establish.

While foolish persons may juggle words, trying to camouflage their own ambitions as your mission, I am bound to work by all means, however inconsequential, for ISKCON as it was, is, and will be. You would sometimes quote the British: “England with all my faults, I love thee.” There may have been faults in ISKCON, and I am sure there always will be, but I will not abandon ship so easily.

Although day by day ISKCON is regaining its health, some are still reluctant to take up responsibility (such as being temple president or whatever). This is another cop-out on Śrīla Prabhupāda. Now is the time, more than ever, for devotees to come forward and help rebuild the house in which we want all men to live.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am trying to use my intelligence to serve you in the best capacity I can, but only you know perfectly well my material desires and my weaknesses. Therefore please neglect my whimsical and irresponsible plans, and use me as you desire. Your mission will be successful, and those who surrender to do the needful will surely get your blessings. I pray to be in that number.

*Your menial servant,*  
Śrīdhar Swami

## Suhotra Swami

**A Petition for Surrender**

I am always cheated by my flighty mind. Though devotional service to Kṛṣṇa is surely the only way for me to cross over the raging *bhāva-sindhu,* I always approach devotional service most whimsically. I totally lack the fixed concentration of a surrendered soul seeking eternal shelter at the Lord’s lotus feet.

Even when at last I am to catch a glimpse of all-purifying power of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, I immediately attempt to engage that power for personal sense gratification. For instance, I might chant more than sixteen rounds daily, but think that gives me an excuse to be slack in other areas of *sādhana-bhakti.* Or, after just one day’s successful completion of devotional service, I become vain and think I have earned the right to criticize Vaiṣṇava.

How can I please Kṛṣṇa with this offensive, cheating attitude? And if Govinda is not pleased, how will I be able to control my senses?

There’s no doubt about it: in mentality I remain what I was when I was born—a sinful demon. Without Śrīla Prabhupāda’s continued mercy, I would not have the slightest inclination to give pleasure through service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By following my selfish ways, I will be put into hell. Somehow I must take shelter of Śrīla Prabhupāda to the fullest extent to be purified of my lust, anger, greed, illusion, madness, and envy.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, my heart is as black as a lump of coal. *Guru* means “heavy,” so only under the mighty pressure of your lotus feet can my heart be squeezed into diamond of unbreakable determination to serve the Lord of the senses, Hṛṣīkeśa. Otherwise, how will this fallen Suhotra attain any success in Kṛṣṇa consciousness? My life is simply hopeless without you.

Though I am so lost, so incapable, so ungrateful for all you’ve given me, you have not rejected me. With a mixture of astonishment at the oceanic kindness of Your Divine Grace, and bitter self-reproach, I prostrate myself at your lotus feet, the refuge of the three worlds. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me surrender to you now.

*Your servant,*  
Suhotra Swami

## Trivikram Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the savior of the most fallen.

Once in the summer of 1977 you asked, “So, will ISKCON continue after I’ve left?” When a disciple answered strongly in the affirmative, you said, “Thank you very much.” Certainly this was your desire. Can any honest disciple doubt this in the slightest? I think we needn’t belabor this point; it stands self-evident.

From our side, the disciple’s side, we also thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you from the bottom of our hearts for giving us this noble challenge. What a wonderful opportunity to put the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness into practical application—to show our love for you and for Kṛṣṇa by actually working cooperatively, by creating a Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere, where dedication to your goals and vision are the cornerstone; where self sacrifice and humility are the motor; where tolerance and honesty are the building blocks. This is the real ISKCON, and we are the most fortunate to have, by your mercy, such a society to work and give our lives for. Again and again we thank you for this opportunity. Please bless us to have the courage to live up to this vision of yours.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Trivikram Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**Śrīla Prabhupāda Has Come Back!**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I was born in the darkest ignorance, suffering as a result of countless offenses at the feet of the Supreme Personlity of Godhead. But you came, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and lifted me out of that dense darkness of illusion and forced open my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I offer my humble obeisances unto you.

When I was in England, in 1977, I was sick in hospital with measles. You were also sick in India, and I thought it was my misfortune that I would never again have your *darśana.* But when a devotee came to the hospital and told me, “Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming to England!”

I said, “How is that possible?” We had been hearing so many stories of how you were sick, how you might leave your body at any moment. How could it be that you were coming to England, and I might be able to see you again?

“It’s true!” The devotee told me. “ Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming tomorrow, and he is going to America to preach. He must have recovered or something , but he is arriving at the airport tomorrow!” I will never forget the exhilaration! Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming! Just when I thought that I would never again see my spiritual master, he was coming!

And you did come, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You came and gave us your mercy for three wonderful weeks, spending your last Vyāsa-pūjā with your disciples during your manifest presence.

Perhaps it was a result of that experience that since then, I have had a recurring dream. The various circumstances and surroundings of the dream change, but the basic theme seems always the same. I come walking through the halls of some temple, and everyone is rushing around. “What’s going on?” I ask. “Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming!” “What? How is it possible?” I ask. “We don’t know,” comes the reply, “but he has returned from the spiritual world, and they have gone to the airport to pick him up. He will be there any minute!” Once again, my heart fills up with exhilaration, and my eyes start brimming with tears. Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming! All our sorrows, all our confusion, all our pain and separation will now come to an end! What joy! What mercy! How incredible! And then I hear conches blowing, and as I look out of the window I see a car pull up. Devotees surround the car, chanting and leaping into the air. Garlands and flower petals fly, devotees bow to the ground, the door of the car opens, and out of the car comes Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Then I see you walk up the steps of the temple, and I am standing by the door, tears in my eyes as I chant: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāmā, Hare Rāmā, Rāmā Rāmā, Hare Hare. From there, the dream proceeds in different ways, which are soothing to our hearts, or sometimes you go to the temple room and sit on the *vyāsāsana* and hand out cookies.

All our problems, all our doubts, all our unhappiness, everything would be gone if you were to come back, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Those who are in other movements, those who are dissatisfied, many of those who are in Māyā, those who are bewildered in various ways, all would come back, if only you would reappear. We would all drive to the airport. Thousands and thousands of devotees from all over the world would come, and we would have an earth-shaking *kirtana* that has not been seen for the last five hundred years, if only we knew what flight you would arrive on, what airline, what airport, what time of day….

But then again, have you really left? Did you not tell us how you never felt separation from your spiritual master? Did you not tell us how the follower of the *guru* resides eternally with the *guru?* Did you not say that you were present in your books? Yes, you are present in your instructions, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but in spite of all of that, wouldn’t it be nice if suddenly someone came in and said, “I don’t know how it is possible, but Śrīla Prabhupāda has just come back from the spiritual world, and he is arriving from the airport any minute.” Wouldn’t it be so sweet…..

*Your beggar disciple,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Always when writing your Vyāsa-pūjā homage I feel very lost for words, and when I see all the expertly written homages of your other disciples, I feel even more inadequate. Still, it is the duty of every disciple to glorify his spiritual master, especially on the day of the spiritual master’s appearance.

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās*  
*tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ vibho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni*  
*svāntaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*

**“The saints of your category are in themselves places of pilgrimage. Because of their purity, they are constant companions of the Lord and, therefore, can purify the placed of pilgrimage.” (*****Bhāg.*** **1.13.10)**

You are not an ordinary person, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but the spotless representative of the Supreme Lord. You came to this world to deliver foolish persons caught up in the clutches of Māyā. Somehow or other, I stumbled across you as you were energetically preaching the glories of the Lord. I recognized you then; only a fool could not have recognized you. You were so wonderful. I knew that you were not of this world, and I reached up and took hold of your lotus feet, your divine instructions. I would never be so presumptuous as to touch your lotus feet without your permission. I was suffering so much in the material world, and if you hadn’t have come along, I don’t know what I would have done. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving me. I was the most wretched person, and yet you gave me your shelter. You are the greatest personality because you gave shelter not just to me but so many suffering persons within this world. Since I have taken shelter of you, you must never kick me away. I know I am a rascal and most unworthy of being in your association, but I have taken shelter of you!

Māyā-dānava was fleeing the burning Khāṇḍava forest. Kṛṣṇa was going to kill this demon, but Māyā-dānava rushed up to Arjuna and begged for shelter. Arjuna gave him shelter at his lotus feet. Māyā-dānava then offered to do some service for Arjuna, something special so that he could repay the debt. In this way, the Pāṇḍavas received their very special assembly house which rivaled the *sabhā* of King Indra in the heavenly planet. In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa would have killed me were it not for you; so therefore I must repay you. I will establish, by your mercy, a beautiful temple in Adelaide, and then everyone will see what is your glory and what is the glory of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara.

Then one day, when I die, I will come to you, my dear spiritual master, and touch your lotus feet. And then also I will get the association eternally of all of your followers from our ISKCON movement, and I will tough their lotus feet also.

This life is so short. Please forgive anyone else who has offended you and Kṛṣṇa. You once said that your disciples in Australia were like Hanuman. The inference was that we were a little “gross,” but we were very, very strong and determined to serve you. In Australia we are still working hard to please you, and we are definitely making headway. If we are making any mistakes, please excuse us, for we only mean to please you. We make money, but we print your books, millions of them, and we have beautiful temples, but they are for your glory, not for our own pleasures. We still go out on harinama, and we still shave up, and we read your books daily, and we still love every day of our devotional service.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! And all glories to all your wonderful followers all over the world who are working faithfully within ISKCON to satisfy you.

*We beg you to remain your servants at ISKCON, Adelaide, Australia.*

Kṛpākara dāsa / Jaleyu-devī dāsī / Ādi-puruṣa dāsa / Satyabhāmā-devī dāsī / Sarvadamana dāsa / Siddharūpā-devī dāsī / Dhaumya dāsa / Jīvānanda-devī dāsī / Bhīma dāsa / Subujī-devī dāsī / Bhakta Gordon / Bhakta Sandy / Ṛṣi-kumāra dāsa / Bhaktin Audrey / Mahābuddhi dāsa / Rāmanuja dāsa / Bhakta Ian / Bhakta Joe / Bhaktin Jane / Bhaktin Danielle / Kathleen / Don / Beryl and our whole congregation.

## Ahmedabad

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The spiritual master is a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa’s compassion to the conditioned souls. As stated in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya* 19.151),

*Brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*Guru-Kṛṣṇa prasāde pāya bhakti-latā bīja.*

**“In the course of traversing the universal creation of Brahma, one by the grace of Kṛṣṇa gets a** ***guru,*** **and by the grace of** ***guru*** **one receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”**

What qualifies a conditioned living entity to become free from material qualities and desires and be transformed to the spiritual atmosphere, where he can associate with and render service to Kṛṣṇa, is simply a change of consciousness. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have started this worldwide society for the purpose of becoming conscious of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and to teach us how to act in such a way that pleases Him.

The *jīva,* soul, while encaged within a material body and thinking it to be himself, is absorbed in trying to make different so-called tasteful arrangements for the satisfaction of the body, both subtle and gross. By your mercy, we have learned that for the soul to be satisfied one had to transfer this tendency of trying to give pleasure to the body to trying to give pleasure to the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. The bona fide spiritual master who comes in the disciplic line from Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the most merciful incarnation of God, is mainly concerned with giving out to everyone—young, old, white, black, poor, and rich—the message of Kṛṣṇa. As stated in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya* 7.128),

*Yare dekha tāre kahe ‘Kṛṣṇa’ -upade’ce*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru haṇā tāra ei deśa*

**“Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Kṛṣṇa as they are given in** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*** **In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.”**

Your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura, ordered you to preach Kṛṣṇa’s message in the English language. You did this through lectures, conversations, letters, and most powerfully through your writings, which have been printed in books, not only in English, but in approximately seventy languages. They have been and are still being distributed to the masses worldwide. You have not only preached Kṛṣṇa’s message yourself, but have inspired others, many of whom never had the fortune of meeting you personally, to take up this most merciful occupation of distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. Your purports are interlaced with quotes from previous *ācāryas* and Kṛṣṇa Himself and are placed in between verse after verse of *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and other Vedic literatures, which give instructions and inspiration to each spiritual perfection in this day and age.

By your mercy we are able to take advantage of the association of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, through His message given in *Bhagavad-gītā* and by the recitation and hearing of His holy name. We also get to read about such great stalwart personalities as Narada Muni, Prahlada Mahārāja, Śukadeva Gosvami and many more from the pages of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Each narration has unique and practical lessons to teach us all how to get free from our painful situation in this material world.

The books that you have given us are undoubtedly prefect, complete, and expertly designed with all the instructions and inspiration to guide conditioned souls like ourselves back to Godhead. It is up to not only us, your disciples and granddisciples, but as time passes by, many generations to come of devotees in this disciplic line, to practically apply the instructions given in your books in each of our individual lives. Our sincerity is and will be tested by how we are able to follow this principle.

Please keep in touch with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for by your association we can receive the desire to be conscious of Lord Kṛṣṇa always and have the potency to preach His message to whomever we meet. Please forgive us for all the offenses we have committed knowingly and unknowingly unto your lotus feet while endeavoring to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this immature, neophyte stage of spiritual advancement that we are in.

*Your fallen servants in Ahmedabad,*

## Almviks Gard, Sweden

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! Kindly listen to a few words attempting to assess your unfathomable greatness. You are undoubtedly glorious in your own right, but my words are hollow and dry without your validation of transcendental pleasure.

By your causeless mercy, the fallen souls, in this dark world are saved from the crushing wheel of the material nature. To contact your lotus feet is the rare opportunity of many lifetimes. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and your *Guru* Mahārāja entrusted you with the mission to spread *Kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* to every town and village in the whole world. To drink the ambrosial nectar of your chanting through the ear is to reestablish our long-lost relationship with the Lord of the heart. Your instructions are such unalloyed purity. All chanting processes are immediately exposed as self-motivated coverings. In fact, your personal example is so singular that simply your holy name alone is sufficient to shame all pride from the minds and hearts of your sincere followers. What to speak of chanting your name, the *darśana* of your transcendental form or even the remembrance of your lotus feet drives away the inauspiciousness of conditioned life. When will I reject once and for all the miserly weakness that prevents me from totally surrendering to you alone?

On this auspicious occasion of your yearly Vyāsa-pūjā observation, I reflect on the passage of one year’s service and hold myself accountable to you. More than ever before, the movement in Sweden has been attacked in the press by the demons as brainwashing. Reading your instructions to Ādi-keśava Prabhu in 1977, you called on the devotees to fight back by going door to door with your books. I confess that I have been hiding in the temple, simply eating and sleeping like a woman allowing debts to accumulate. On this holy day I beg to correct myself as follows:

—I will rise before 4a.m. daily and be clean.  
—I will chant sixteen rounds carefully in not more than two hours.  
—I will follow the four regulative principles strictly.  
—I will study the *śāstra* daily and hear from your advanced disciples almost as if form your directly.  
—I will go out the door and distribute your books, go on *harināma* SKP, and distribute *prasādam* as a regular daily function.  
—I will be happy to support myself and family with whatever *lakṣmī* that comes by your service.  
—I will remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him, even at the moment of death.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were my age your spiritual master’s mission collapsed, leaving you alone to carry on the preaching. Neophyte as I am, by your grace, I have the association of thousands of advanced Godbrothers and Godsisters in ISKCON, and I appreciate their service without envy. But please protect me from the *māyā* of thinking my service to you is insignificant, because it is factually the only important thing to me.

Now I have spoken such big promises. Although I refuse to accept service from you, my spiritual master, still I am helpless to pass the test of coming year without your assistance, O lord, father, savior. Please accept this prayer from your disgraceful ś*iṣya,*

Yogīndra dāsa

## Amsterdam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

So many things have happened since you left this world. You told us things would be difficult and that this world is always full of disappointments. For most of us, that is no longer theoretical knowledge. The disappointments have given us some realizations, and now many of us are seeing this world with justifiable pessimism. The scars left by unfulfilled hopes can always remind us to trust no future, however bright.

This Kali-yuga era is packed with one lamentable tragedy after another. The widespread hypocrisy is simply insulting to a conscious, feeling person. Unrelenting, Kali’s agents hammer on. Absurdity parades proudly, so that everyone feels “What is the use?” Despondency takes its toll and points the way towards forgetfulness and ignorance. So many follow.

You touched so many of us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in one way or another. Who can ever forget you?

When you left, that catastrophe was in some ways and for some time ameliorated by our caring brothers who sought to somehow make your absence less painful. And yet we floundered from self-deluding dreams of utopias. Your pure visions eluded us. We forgot to help each other, and so the Vaikuṇṭha of your person held only the corner of the eye. We became fixed on grand schemes and even grander methods, only to find our hopes for a perfect world dashed by our insistence upon success and position. In stretching for the moon we lost our balance and stumbled.

Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the only true revolutionary, for you are the ambassador of freedom, the bearer of good tidings, of succor, and of hope. You have brought the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which is the only liberating force within this world. Gaurāṅga has brought the only movement solely intended for the mass of people, regardless of any destinations, and you have so graciously exemplified that wellspring of compassion for humanity.

How can we imitate such a large heart? Our selfish means do not afford such caring for others. Lamentations are for those who, because of small and miserly hearts, would deny the grace of *saṅkīrtana* to our suffering human brothers. May we mark well the razor’s edge and tend well the riches of the holy name. That name is meant for all; we pray that we not be denied a place as a purveyor. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us not fail from so weak a heart. By your mercy you have given His name, and by your grace may we take it feelingly. Then will the bounds of our hearts enlarge, and then will we know a drop of your compassion for others.

In Amsterdam there are many devotees who have seriously taken up your cause. Most of them have not witnessed your regal gait, your knowing glance, or the shine of your wonderful form. Yet they are sincere in working in your service. You continue to be an inspiration even after your departure, and so your transcendental position is attested by the faith of these devotees in your words.

May you always be the cynosure of our vision, held there fast by the medium of sound. May we always feel the presence of Your Divine Grace, and may we unreservedly tender our hearts at your lotus feet. If you bless us, we may still correct our course towards simple lives of high thinking. Thus we may find our way to your shelter.

You are our one and only hope. You can heal the wounds of our disappointments. You have proven you will not let us down. May we hope to please you in the same way.

The cosmic clock winds to winter  
Sweet refrains becoming bitter  
Darkest deeds yet unknown  
Lamentations to be intoned

Fine qualities are quickly lost  
In the cold of Kali’s frost  
The pure and right are on the wane  
Human path becomes insane

No mercy found in common deeds  
Innocent animals profusely bleed  
Austerity, forgotten goal  
Intoxications take their toll

Sex termed love of any partner  
Fidelity moves from one to another  
Cleanliness suffers this abuse  
Disease unchecked, long life reduced

Unwanted children roaming streets  
Parents careless, abominable feats  
Destroy the chance of human birth  
And blame it on financial dearth

Heavy taxes the common rule  
To manufacture destructive tools  
Leaders point a hellish path  
Demoniac kings unleashing wrath

Rogues and thieves flourish unchecked  
Honest men become suspect  
The simple suffer profuse disruptions  
Political excuse for vile corruptions

External traits create affluence  
Image gives a man influence  
Word-jugglers appear as learned teachers  
Miscreant charlatans as respected preachers

Wealth alone buys dignity  
Class distinctions through prosperity  
Justice purchased by the rich  
Abandoned honor in the ditch

Slogans brandished as solutions  
“isms” offered in profusions  
while profit-mongers create pollutions  
and market problems as solutions

Kali’s work is plainly clear  
To spread confusion, dread and fear  
Troubles proclaimed audaciously  
Perpetrators promote hypocrisy

A downward spiral long to come  
Misery’s toll, enormous sum  
The lowest age upon us all  
A curse of illusion, whole nations enthralled

An ocean of misery is Kali’s curse  
The people hanker for a reverse  
From the trouble and strife of confusion  
Desire for freedom from the pain of illusion

Compassionate sages foretold a cure  
In ancient ages they saw the pure  
Solution from Kali’s strife  
“Take God’s name within your life!”

They prophesied Caitanya’s coming  
And spoke in awe of His loving  
Indiscriminate compassionate charity  
In spreading His name to all humanity

The yuga avatāra is Caitanyadeva  
With His associates He came to save  
Millions of desperate, suffering men  
From wasted life, ignoble end

You, dear master, are His dear  
In old age and free from fear  
As His servant, as our friend  
You journeyed far, our hearts to mend

You gave prasadam abundantly  
His name, your gift “incessantly”  
And from the confusing Vedic vault  
Purports expounded, free of fault

The bhakti path, your humble instruction  
Will save us form dark Kali’s corruption  
Your enlightened actions show the way  
Example pure, to serve you we pray

Your gentle manner soothes our hearts  
Your caring guidance does impart  
The bhakti secrets to advance  
With strict adherence our lives enhanced

Your clear expression of unassailable topics  
Defeats the popular convoluted logic  
On man as beast, descendant of ape  
Darwin confounded, speculative mistake

While scientists dare to propagate void  
By black holes, they say the cosmos destroyed  
You call into question their tiny gray matter  
Revealing their cheating as feverish chatter

You challenge them all, “Show the proof!”  
In laboratories, some life to produce  
Their endeavors, you show, are darkly fated  
Their claims blank checks, all poorly post-dated

Your countless achievements, all exceedingly rare  
From low-born men you made bhaktas, so fair  
Service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, your lesson sublime  
“Share it with millions,” your mission defined

O great unparalleled philosopher-saint  
O teacher sublime, no selfish taint  
Who can compare to one so complete?  
Lords and gods repose at your lotus feet

“Revolution!” the task at hand!  
To spread pure knowledge across the land  
Saṅkīrtana yajna your command  
Kali’s influence, to reprimand

To this task there is no impediment  
This purpose has no prerequisite  
No conditions for dissemination  
Your books the basis, holy propagation

Religion, economics, they have no play  
Sensory delights obstruct the way  
Liberation the product inconsequential  
Divine revolution the act substantial

You, dear master, are bright as the sun  
From you Kali’s darkness runs  
Regal, potent, loving father  
We pray, in bhakti take us farther.

(Written by Bhūdhara dāsa)

## Atlanta

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your effulgent lotus feet, and please allow us to always remain under their protection.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are feeling very intensely your separation, and we pray that this feeling will act as an impetus for our further dedication to your mission—to enlighten the people of this Kali-yuga.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please infuse us with the necessary potency to always work enthusiastically and intelligently for your great mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have arranged everything so nicely for your followers. We have so many wonderful activities to perform on your behalf, such as book distribution and *harinama-saṅkīrtana.* We pray that these sacred activities will never be neglected by us, your humble servants.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you visited us here at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, you remarked that what pleases you the most is that we love Kṛṣṇa. And we are trying our humble best to fulfill this innermost desire of yours, but because of our neophyte condition we have continually fallen short of that wonderful goal. So therefore, please continue to bless us with ever-increasing enthusiasm to reach this most desirable aim of life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that your greatest anxiety was that the mass of people were misdirected, so we have decided, on this most blessed day, Vyāsa-pūjā, to rededicate ourselves to relieve you and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu from this distress. And with your unfailing and endless mercy, I am sure that we will meet with ultimate success.

(Written by Balabhadra Bhaṭṭācāryadāsa)

*Your humble servants at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, Atlanta ISKCON,*

## Auckland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Is it possible that you could accept an offering from your most ignorant and most bewildered servant—one whose mind has been closed to truth and knowledge for so many long, wasted years?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books and your teachings are revolutionary! They are completely changing the lives of everyone on this earth. Revolution means “turning or reversing; right way up.” For countless years we have all ambled or staggered or dreamed through our lifetimes with no understanding of any purpose. Now you have brought us to the clear light of Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, and at last we are beginning to wake up.

Your instruction to us comes directly from the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and so how can we neglect it? We will always have difficulties in fighting the battles of Kali-yuga, but if we can remain with the weapons of knowledge and devotion (*bhakti-vedānta)* which you have given us, we must succeed. Our gratitude for that opportunity knows no bounds.

All glories to you, our most merciful spiritual master.

(Written by Mādrī-devī dāsī \[granddisciple\])

## Australia Traveling Temple

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We thank you very much for so kindly coming to the West with the message of Lord Caitanya.

Now the years are going on, and despite all our trials and tribulations, still your movement is strongly pushing ahead and gradually maturing.

We are all working on your Soviet preaching mission here in Australia, trying to help free devotees in the U.S.S.R. so they can continue your mission. Even their being in jail has created so much preaching in the West. When you went to the Soviet Union, you preached to one boy, Ananta-śanti dāsa (Anotoli Pinyayev), and in turn he preached throughout the Soviet Union. But actually, he has preached all over the world. Even his imprisonment has inspired devotees to push on your mission.

It is therefore impossible to fully glorify you, because your glories are unlimited. You have planted the seeds of Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, and now, from the Soviet Union, that one seed you planted had inspired and helped to unite us all to spread the message of Lord Caitanya.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*From the Traveling Temple (Australia),*

## Barcelona

*ohe!*  
*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura, doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse korunā kori'*  
*diyā pada-chāyā, śodho he āmāya,*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, O Vaiṣṇava Thākura, O ocean of mercy, be merciful to us, your servants, and purify us by the shade of your lotus feet. Your feet we humbly hold.

We hold your lotus feet on our heads, and we carry your pure instructions in our hearts. Whenever we do that, we feel relieved from the pangs of material existence.

We are insignificant, and our hearts are ironlike, but by the remembrance of your wonderful qualities and transcendental character, we are enlivened, and our hearts melt.

Service unto you is our life and soul. Without that service, our life is like death. Our desire is to serve you by being your instruments in your preaching mission. Please, bless us and empower us so that we are able to maintain and expand your movement unlimitedly.

## Baroda

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your feet.

It is now more than ten years since you ended your manifest presence in this world. The dark clouds of Kali-yuga seem to be expanding daily. Ignorance of the real purpose of human life is now a most common commodity, even here in Bhārata-bhūmi.

As it is stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.20.17):

*nṛ-deham ādyaṁ su-labhaṁ su-durlabhaṁ*  
*plavaṁ su-kalpaṁ guru-karṇadhāram*  
*mayānukūlena nabhasvateritaṁ*  
*pumān bhavābdhiṁ na taret sa ātma-hā*

**“The human body, which can award all benefit in life, is automatically obtained by the laws of nature, although it is a very rare achievement. This human body can be compared to a perfectly constructed boat, having the spiritual master as the captain and the instructions of the Personality of Godhead as favorable winds impelling it on its course. Considering all these advantages, a human being who does not utilize his human life to cross the ocean of material existence must be considered the killer of his own soul.”**

In our fight against Māyā we see that increasingly, material self-interest is coming in the way of success. The influence of Māyā is so powerful that even may of our Godbrothers and co-devotees have fallen victim to her power. We pray on this holy Vyāsa-pūjā day that by your mercy we may get the intelligence to always remain untied under your captaincy and not be swayed to the illusion of so-called “independence” ( which really means to submit ourselves to the control of the material energy), by which we think we will be happy. This material consciousness, which is symptomized by the desire of *lābha, pūjam pratiṣṭhā,* and false ego, will only drag us to repeated birth and death. We pray that we may always follow the instructions of the above-mentioned verse of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and thus free ourselves from Māyā’s clutches. We pray that all the devotees of ISKCON will unite under your divine instructions, giving up all material desires, and deliver the world from its present diseased condition.

As the poem says, *Śrī-guru-karuṇā-sindhu, adhāma-janāra bandhu.* Kindly bless us with a drop of your mercy by granting us the intelligence to avoid the traps of Māyā and somehow or other push on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

*Your worthless servants,*

Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda

Basu Ghoṣa dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kaviraja Gosvami has stated:

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*  
*Ādi 1.45*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi*** **1.45).**

You have commented in this regard that the relationship with the Supreme Lord. Without knowing you, we would have had no choice of understanding that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Without hearing your presentation of Kṛṣṇa’s instructions in *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam ,* we would never have understood that we are not this body but are individual spirit souls, eternally the servants of Kṛṣṇa.

Without your guidelines, we could never have fathomed the depths of ecstasy of pure love of Kṛṣṇa.

Without your guidelines, we could never have imagined how to perform devotional service and be pleasing to Kṛṣṇa.

Without your personal example, we could never have appreciated the qualities and behavior of a pure devotee.

Without your mercy, we could not apply ourselves to the process of *bhakti.*

And without your recommendation to Kṛṣṇa, there would be no chance of our ever returning to the spiritual world.

As servants struggling to please you by following your instructions and trying to expand Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we are faced with a serious attack on our preaching here at Bhaktivedanta Manor. We are pitted against formidable Kali-yuga demons intent on evicting the Deities and devotees from a place of your pastimes.

The dispute had been smoldering since Kṛṣṇa arrived in Letchmore Heath, with a major skirmish, about seven years ago. However, the last two years have been all-out war. It is said that war brings people together in a mood of cooperation and sacrifice. Indeed, it had been heartwarming to see so many people from all walks of life come to the aid of the temple. Demons have sided with the demons, but those with some piety and sincerity are making a stand for Kṛṣṇa. It is amazing to watch how they have become influenced and purified through their involvement in the campaign to save the temple.

The campaign has also inspired our congregation, who have been highly active demonstrating their concern through marches, lobbying the government, donating to the cause, and becoming more sincere in their devotional service. We are amazed at their depth of commitment to your temple and appreciation of your mission.

The war continues. It is such a complex and fast-moving battle of strategy that we have to admit we are bewildered by it all. What is Kṛṣṇa’s plan? What are we supposed to do?

How we long to be able to go into your room, pay our obeisances, describe the situation to you, and await your analysis. We would have such confidence. Without that opportunity, we are constantly praying to you and the Deities for guidance. We are researching your instructions in books and letters. We are trying to keep ourselves free from material influence. Without direct access to your personal presence, it is no easy matter to divine the proper path of action.

We have still no clear idea of what will be the outcome of the present dispute. Sometimes we have high hopes and sometimes we have deep fears. Sometimes we think we can see Kṛṣṇa’s plan, and sometimes we are in confusion.

All we can offer you on your Vyāsa-pūjā is our prayer that you remove any of our shortcomings which are obstacles to the progress of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We suspect that Lord Kṛṣṇa has a big plan in mind, initiated by your desire, but which is far beyond our limited aspirations. However, we would humbly like to offer ourselves to you as fully-surrendered servants ready to undertake whatever sacrifices, austerities, and tribulations are required to manifest your plan.

All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*Your worthless servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Bolivia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Actually, no one can glorify a transcendental personality like yourself without knowing him and serving him purely with loving affection. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kaviraja Gosvami wrote wonderfully the teachings and glories of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, just having the qualification of having heard about Him from bona fide associates and the notes written by them. We don’t have either Kṛṣṇadāsa’s purity or qualifications as devotees, neither were we so fortunate as to meet Your Divine Grace personally. So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, drag us into the ocean of the understanding of your books, and give us the strength to serve and follow nicely your genuine servitors who are bathing us in the nectar of your holy pastimes and teachings. Please bless us to be inspired for lifelong enthusiastic and bold preaching, not caring for difficulties, but following the unique example you set for the ten thousand years to come. You are the founder-*ācārya* of the glorious main branch of Lord Caitanya’s tree: your beloved ISKCON society.

Although we are proudly passing as your followers, we are actually poorly representing your sacred mission due to our unfortunate attachment to material enjoyment. We are embarrassed, feeling that we are slowing the flow of your mercy due to personal illusions and ambitions. Please, be compassionate towards we foolish so-called followers, and never kick us out of your beautiful society, the house in which the whole world can live.

We’ve heard how once some devotees questioned you in amazement seeing you suddenly laughing. You told them that you had just seen Narada Muni laughing like anything seeing the *yavanas* chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in one of the temples in America. We can’t help but also laugh at the idea of being so happily dragged into the ocean of bliss of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya by your kind, inconceivable mercy. Please deliver these sinful rascals who are obscuring your light out of lack of purity.

We pray that your glories be heard and sung throughout the three worlds!

All glories to Oṁ Vishnupada Paramahāṁsa Aṣtottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, our beloved spiritual grandfather!

*Your servants, ISKCON Bolivia,*

## Bologna

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this auspicious event I try to collect thoughts to glorify your glorious personality. You came from the spiritual world to reclaim the fallen souls, and you have transformed lowborn humans into merciful soldiers for spreading the mission of Lord Caitanya. Incessantly by your example you gave us the understanding that we are the servant of God. I always forget this fact. Bound up by unlimited desires, the determination to surrender to you becomes very difficult. This is the occasion to ask Your Divine Grace to cut this network of illusion so that I can really concentrate on offering you some valuable service, that I may be of some use to this great ISKCON movement. By observing your dear disciples, I can see that they are engaged in working hard for Kṛṣṇa, and by your blessings wonderful achievements have been made by spreading Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. Now after some years of ISKCON training I would like to increase my service attitude, knowing well what are my propensities that can be engaged in hard labor to please you more and more. My only hope is to pray to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa for more strength to utilize in becoming honest in serving you, without interruption. I don’t desire that type of theoretical lip service, but some concrete result that others could be benefited by. You have created hundreds of ISKCON centers, where millions of people can take spiritual benefit forever. In order to please you we must develop love of Kṛṣṇa, which means serving you submissively, without becoming proud, recognizing that it’s only by your causeless mercy that anything can be achieved. You have given us so much. Without self-interest, you preach this wonderful message of *Kṛṣṇa-kathā.* You give us this wonderful ISKCON. You gave us wonderful Kṛṣṇa devotees. You gave us *Bhagavad-gītā.* You gave us Kṛṣṇa’s *prasadam.* You gave us Ekādaśī. You gave us Tulasī-devī. You gave us Vaiṣṇava etiquette. You gave us Lord Caitanya. What have you not given to us? I should at least preach this movement, your glories, to show gratitude, pure gratitude. You are so humble, Śrīla Prabhupāda, never caring for any other interest outside Kṛṣṇa’s interest, ISKCON’s interest. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I prostrate myself at your lotus feet, aspiring to please you by hard labor and surrender.

(Written by Durgama dāsa)

*Bologna Temple,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Bombay

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances.

Unalloyed devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa is the eternal occupation of all loving entities. Yet, as Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself states in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* this is the supreme secret—*rahasyam hy etad uttamam.* The Lord revealed this secret when He spoke the *Bhagavad-gītā.* Yet the message remained misunderstood. He then came as Lord Caitanya Himself and established once again the holy intent of the Vedic literatures. Still the message was not completely understood. It was when you so devotedly took up the preaching mission of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that the supreme secret became revealed and accessible to even the lowest of mankind—the *narādhāmas* like us. Thus the example you have set in taking up the preaching mission as the *ācārya* is unrivaled and unexcelled in the past, present, and future.

Here in Bombay, we can see the full manifestation of your preaching vision and machinery in the form of the glittering complex you have lovingly constructed for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārījī. To follow in your footsteps and to maintain the preaching tempo, we see the need to rise fully to the platform of *vairāgya.* As you have yourself explained, the basis for success in preaching is *viṣaya chāḍiyā se rase majjiyā.* It is only by completely disassociating ourselves from sense gratification that we become recognized as devotees by Śrī Caitanya. Bombay is the biggest commercial center, yet you have declared that you go to Bombay for purification. By this you have revealed that the oneness of *sādhana* and *sādhya* in *bhakti* finds its highest expression in the *saṅkīrtana-yajna,* where the process for purification and the platform of purified action are one and the same—namely, preaching. We, the devotees at Bombay, therefore desire to rededicate, on this most auspicious day of your appearance, all our energies, intents, and desires, nay our full existence, to your preaching mission.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this year we are embarking on so many fronts in the cause of your service. The *Guru*kula school is being opened and will serve its role in respiritualizing the modern society. We are also increasing our college and scientific preaching at eh Bhaktivedanta Institute, with the building of a proposed academic complex. We are also pushing to increase book and *prasada* distribution. You have emphasized that these four activities in particular will enhance the prestige and spiritual influence of ISKCON in Bombay.

O master of our souls! All glories, all glories to you! Even as we clumsily flutter our wings to fly, you freely traverse the unlimited sky of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore, you are our eternal guide. We are without intelligence and one-pointed dedication. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we surrender at your lotus feet. Kindly bless us so that we can serve you better, Just as one offers Ganges water back to the Ganges, we ask for mercy so that we can offer it back in the form of service to you.

(Written by Rasarāja dāsa)

*Your aspiring servants from Hare Kṛṣṇa Land, Juhu, Bombay,*

## Boston

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We offer our most humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet, which are worth taking shelter of because you are the most intimate and beloved associate of Lord Gaurāṅga. No one in history has ever distributed Lord Gaurāṅga’s mercy as you have.

It is only by the grace of Kṛṣṇa that one can get the opportunity to associate with a *Mahā-bhagavata.* Indeed, this fact is stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.22.6), where Manu speaks to Kardama Muni:

*diṣṭyā me bhagavān dṛṣṭo*  
*durdarśo yo 'kṛtātmanām*  
*diṣṭyā pāda-rajaḥ spṛṣṭaṁ*  
*śīrṣṇā me bhavataḥ śivam*

**“It is my good fortune that I have been able to see you, for you cannot easily be seen by persons who have not subdued the mind or controlled the senses. I am all the more fortunate to have touched with my head the blessed dust of your feet.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your causeless mercy you have made yourself available to everyone in the form of your books. You once stated that you would never die, but would live forever in your instructions. Therefore, allow us to remember them, especially on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Your appearance is a manifestation of the Lord’s unlimited mercy, and you gave yourself unlimitedly to the entire world. Let this realization remain with us eternally, so that we may extend that mercy to all conditioned souls. Please remember us to renounce the spirit of independence and work cooperatively, thereby showing our love for you.

(Written by Ānandamāyā dāsa )

*Your humble servants at Boston ISKCON*,

## Brisbane

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories, all glories, all glories unto you. Śrīla Prabhupāda! You have saved us all form a hell, this material world. Without you we would not have had any chance of being freed from this fearful prison.

You have dissipated the darkness of night. Your precious gift is so magnanimous that we cannot possibly repay you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can only pray that somehow, despite our many disqualifications, we can try and please you by becoming instrumental in your vitally important mission. We have such little appreciation, such little devotion, such little realization or compassion. We can only fall down in the dust of your lotus feet and beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please allow us to always remain your humble servants.

## Cairns

Tamo guna means darkness—  
Its victim can’t see  
That without service to Kṛṣṇa  
This rare human life  
Is simply wasted.

Rajo-guna means passion—  
Its victim determine that  
There is lasting pleasure within  
Short-lived sense indulgence.

These predominant base modes  
Artfully impose  
A godless state of ignorance and  
Artificial bliss upon the whole world.

How brave you were, Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
To come all alone  
As you did!

O rose among thorns!  
O light in the night!  
You are an oasis in the desert,  
Bubbling forth the elixir of life!

First you force open our eyes  
With revolutionary news—  
“Fleeting pleasure will bind and confuse,  
Denying perception of God’s presence…”

And then, you describe  
(with your inimitable calm)  
the form and pastimes of God!  
O patita-pāvana!

And you kindly arrange  
For that bleak time when you’ve gone  
That the truth be book-bound  
And available to everyone.

How can we thank you?  
What chance have our attempts,  
When, by nature, devotional service  
Can only deepen our indebtedness!  
How can we thank you?

*From your granddisciples in Cairns Yātrā, Australia,*
