## Guadalajara

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Allow us to prostrate ourselves at your lotus feet to offer our humble homage on your appearance day. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote in your book *Easy Journey to Other Planets* (p. 23). “The serious candidate should accept a reliable spiritual master for the purpose of being properly trained. Because the senses are material, it is not possible to understand the transcendental platform through them alone. Therefore, the senses have to be spiritualized by the prescribed method under the direction of the spiritual master.”

On this day, we beg you that we be able to carry out your desires, taking shelter of your dear disciples who are faithfully trying to follow and carry out your instructions. Therefore Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu has advised us, *gopi-bhartuḥ pada-kamalayor dāsa-dāsānudāsaḥ:* “One must be the servant of the servant of the servant.” We have faith in your ISKCON society and in your words and instructions, which you have left for eternity, written in your books, letters, and recorded lectures on tapes. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya:* We know that this is the only means of understanding your mission, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. We beg to develop complete faith in your instructions and to be able to reestablish our loving relationship with Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, through the blessings of Śrīmati Rādhārani, who is the personification of *bhakti.*

*yasya deva parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*  
*(Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.23)*

**“To those souls who have implicit faith in the spiritual master and in the Lord, all the imports of the** ***Vedas*** **are automatically revealed.”**

Please allow us to become Your Divine Grace’s eternal servants, so that we may have some hope of getting out of this dense forest of illusion in which we’ve been for millions of lifetimes. We ask that you allow us to use the words that you dedicated to your spiritual master on his appearance day, and in this way conclude our humble offering, prostrating ourselves at your lotus feet.

“Personally, I have no hope whatsoever of executing any service directly for millions of births to come during my sojourn in this world, but I have faith that one day or other I will be liberated from this mire of illusion in which I find myself so profoundly submerged. Therefore, let me pray with all sincerity at the lotus feet of my divine spiritual master, that he let me suffer what I am destined according to my past sinful activities, but may I remember the following: I am nothing more than a minute servant of the absolute and all-powerful Lord, which I’ve understood through the great mercy of my divine master. Please allow me, then, to prostrate myself at his lotus feet with all the humility that I possess.”

His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda-kī Jaya!

*New Nīlacala Mandira, Gaudalajara, Mexico,*

Sanat-kumāra dāsa / Gadādhara dāsa / Kṛṣṇadayā dāsa / Murāri Hari dāsa / Caitanya-candra dāsa / Jagannātha-tīrtha dāsa / Tejasvit dāsa / Premānanda dāsa / Bhakta Cesar / Bhakta Francisco / Bhakta Amadeus / Nṛsiṁha-tīrtha dāsa / Adhokṣaja dāsa / Lakṣmī-Nārāyaṇa dāsa / Mahānīla dāsa / Devakī-devī dāsī / Śyāmā-devī dāsī / Rasāsu-devī dāsī / Gandhinī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Estela / Gaurasundara / Nityānanda / Vaṁsī / Bihāri / Nārada / Śrīnivāsa

## Gujerat

Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Our only beloved in this world.  
It was you only  
Who cared to come and pick us up  
Out of our most condemned, disgusting,  
Shelterless and so very hellish existence.  
We are drowning in the fathomless whirlpool of pain,  
Content on coming and going to and from the house of Yamarajā.  
Then we met you!  
Who can ever understand our inconceivable fortune?  
Because who is more merciful than you?  
Who has such compassion and love for us as you?  
O para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,  
When can we see you again?  
We used to see you everyday in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class.  
Who can forget your golden, smiling face  
Emanating pure Kṛṣṇa-prema in all directions?  
There would be tumultuous kīrtana—  
All the devotees leaping, crying,  
“Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!  
Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!”  
Unwillingly, somehow the ecstatic kirtana would end.

Then you would sing “Jaya Rādhā Madhava” like no one else in the world.  
Just to hear you everyday showing Kṛṣṇa everywhere.  
Your words—the essence of all nectar.  
Just to see you everyday, effulgent like gold.  
Just to see your kind smile, encouraging us all.  
To see you laugh, your compassion, your love for us…  
Just to see you, Śrīla Prabhupāda…  
But we can’t see you anymore…  
“Being unable to obtain the association of Lord Gaurāṅga and His associates Narottama dāsa simply weeps.  
I will smash my head against the rock  
And enter into fire.  
Where will I find the reservoir of all loving qualities?”  
Who has love for Kṛṣṇa as you do, Śrīla Prabhupāda?  
Who has compassion and love for us as you?  
Who is humble, kind, gentle, pure, unmotivated, generous, as you?  
Who has anything compared to you?  
All the good qualities of this world multiplied millions of times  
cannot equal even one millionth of your fathomless transcendental qualities.  
You are more loving and dear than millions of Bengali mothers,  
More renounced than billions of sannyāsīs,  
More generous than trillions of desire trees,  
More humble and tolerant than millions of blades of grass,  
And more powerful a preacher than millions of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees.  
You are not of this cheating material world.  
Once on saṅkīrtana, a man saw your photo.  
He was an expert face reader. He said,  
“Your spiritual master cries very much—day and night—  
But his crying is not of this world.  
He is from ‘the other world,’  
A siddha-purusa.”

You were constantly crying in ecstatic separation from Kṛṣṇa,  
But still you were so humble in front of us  
You played as an ordinary *guru*, asking advice on different matters.  
Who can know our inconceivable good fortune?  
We have met you and now we have hope.  
Just like Amoga. He was a helpless blasphemer of Lord Caitanya.  
But because he was related to Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭśārya,  
Lord Caitanya embraced him and awarded him ecstatic Kṛṣṇa-bhakti.  
Because we are related to you, Lord Caitanya’s dear devotee,  
So also we will receive Lord Caitanya’s special favor.  
You are our eternal transcendental father,  
And we are your eternal fallen sons and daughters.  
You have risked everything for us.  
You told us once  
“I neither saw left or right, but walked through hell for you.”  
For us …

Narayana Mahārāja said once that it was a very, very cold winter in Vṛndāvana,  
And you were very old.  
He asked you why you didn’t have any bedding, and you said  
“I need every paisa for printing books.”  
You suffered everything for us.  
Then you gave us everything.  
Kṛṣṇa’s name. Kṛṣṇa’s sevā. Vaiṣṇava sevā.  
Prasāda. ISKCON. Association of real saintly persons.  
Your books. Your very self?  
Who in this world was so kind to us?  
And yet who in this world was so kind to us?  
And yet who in this world is more ungrateful than us?  
You revealed the topmost love of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa to us, cow-eaters.  
You gave the highest to the lowest.

“The splendid path of pure Kṛṣṇa-bhakti, the pure love of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, obtained by the constant chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, is the topmost secret which merciful Kṛṣṇa never revealed to His closest friend; which bewildered great sages of the past. Which is not attainable by pious activities, austerities, meditating or yoga; which even Sukadeva Gosvami didn’t know.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*)

You have given us the best of everything.  
But have we disappointed you?  
Please don’t kick us away, thinking us too fallen.  
“Glorious is that master who does not abandon his servant.  
Even if the master wants separation from the servant.  
The true servant will never give up the shelter of his master’s feet.”  
So even if you want separation from us  
We will never, ever go away from your lotus feet.  
Besides you, who is there for us in this whole world?  
You are our only opulence, our life and soul, our only shelter.  
But why should you care about us?  
Who could care about us?  
We were demons, no doubt,  
Born in families of Rākṣasas, vampires.  
My father’s favorite dish—bread soaked in cow’s blood—  
“The redder, the better.”  
Who could imagine the creatures you chose to shower your mercy on!  
Drug addicts, cow-murderers, homosexuals, lusty demons.  
How could the seed of pure Kṛṣṇa-bhakti sprout  
In our condemned, wretched hearts?  
All our filthy habits would surely devour it.  
You delivered Kṛṣṇa-bhakti for us to take up sincerely,  
But, just as we were grasping a little hold of your lotus feet,  
Lord Caitanya, tears flowing from His eyes, feeling very morose  
Due to long separation from you, must have called you back,  
Leaving us stranded here in this horrifying world  
Full of more demons.

As Śrīmati Rādhārani says,

“My dearest Lord, kindly hear My true submission. My mind is Vṛndāvana and since You like Vṛndāvana, when will You kindly place Your lotus feet there again?

If You don’t very soon, then it will not be possible to maintain My life. You keep telling us that You have not left, that You are still here, but My mind does not accept this. I can no longer see the sad faces of Mother Yaśodā and all the inhabitants of Vraja. Why are You keeping us half alive in such a state of suffering? What about You r cows, all Your friends, Your mother, father, the gopis?”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what about all of us,  
Your children and grandchildren?  
Some of us were hardly four-five years old  
When you left us.  
What about your dynamic preaching programs,  
Doubling book distribution, prasada distribution, *guru*kula?

“My dearest Rādhārani,” Kṛṣṇa replied, “Please hear Me, for I speak the truth! I cry day and night simply remembering all of you. No one knows how unhappy it makes Me. I know in My absence You cannot live even for a moment. So just to keep You living I come daily to see You, though You cannot see Me. Thus You can always feel my presence there in Vṛndāvana. I hope very soon I will be visible to You again.”

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we also feel your presence, but  
We hope very soon you will be visible to us again.  
We also cannot live without you,  
Feeling all vacant in this world, in the absence of You.  
When will we see your golden lotus feet again?  
Will we ever see your smiling, loving, moonlike face again?  
Will that sweet time ever come again?  
When you were here,  
We were all so humble, hard-working and happy.  
We did anything for you—move mountains,  
Twelve-hour book distribution, two book bags on our back,  
Three chapattis and a thermos of dahl, rain or snow.  
But now we are puffed up, lazy, useless, rotten,  
Fallen flat on our faces, without you.  
Like Arjuna, when Kṛṣṇa left the planet,  
He was easily defeated by some simple cowherdsmen.  
You could have taken us with you.  
Like Rama, when He left, He took all Ayodhya.  
When Lord Caitanya and Kṛṣṇa left, They took all the living entities with Them.  
There are millions of souls here. Kṛṣṇa wouldn’t have minded.  
“Kṛṣṇa doubtless grants all the desires of His pure devotees” like you.  
So it must be your desire for us to stay here,  
And somehow do something here “to repay you.”  
In 1973, on Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta’s appearance day,  
We saw you on the Vyāsāsana,  
Crying in separation from your beloved *Guru* Mahārāja…

So also, today, on your appearance day, we also cry in separation from you.

Your aspiring servants,  
ISKCON Bamanbore (Gujerat, India)

## Guyana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances again and again in the dust of your lotus feet, which are the only means of shelter for our wretched selves and indeed for all the fallen, conditioned souls, who are foolishly thinking and making futile arrangements for achieving permanent happiness in this material world, certified by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself as *duḥkhālayam aśāsvatam,* temporary and full of miseries.

Although you are no longer physically present with us, we can still have your association every moment of our lives by sincerely endeavoring to hear the transcendental instructions you have left us, and by struggling to take these instructions as our very life and soul. Because of our fallen condition, we are always prone to forget our inestimable good fortune and your causeless mercy in allowing us to participate in your ISKCON movement. You are nevertheless so kind and forbearing that you never become disgusted with us; with great patience you keep on ever encouraging us not to waste this invaluable opportunity by falling away from your family of devotees in ISKCON, which is like an oasis within this desertlike age of Kali.

We sometimes become deterred by obstacles we have to face in our attempts to fulfill your orders to distribute your books, the holy name, and *prasadam.* We pray that you please help us to remember always how you yourself constantly struggled to fulfill the desires of your beloved *Guru* Mahārāja, even up to your last breath. That is the perfection of life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are longing for the day to come when we can really serve and glorify Your Divine Grace in a manner befitting your exalted position as Kṛṣṇa’s most confidential representative. On the most auspicious event of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we take this opportunity to beg for your blessings so that we can surrender fully our minds, bodies, and everything at our disposal in your divine service, and thus give up our stubborn attachment to this illusory material existence.

Begging to remain always situated as your most humble servitors.

*Devotees of New Godruma Dhāma, New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, New Navadvīpa Dhāma, New Kulīnagrāma Dhāma*

## Hartford

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Once in Brooklyn, N.Y., at your Henry St. temple, I had the opportunity to see you and to hear from you. What a fortunate day in my life! You were sitting on the *vyāsāsana* in the small temple room, and the temple room was packed with devotees. I was standing outside the door with my eyes watching you and my ears hearing you. A devotee then handed me your slippers and instructed me to put them on your lotus feet when you came out of the temple room. What a fortunate day in my life!

Having your slippers in my hands, I became proud, thinking of how I tell the devotees of the personal service I rendered. I then began to pray to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvami Mahārāja Prabhupāda to help me put on your slippers without making any mistakes. I understood my false pride could cause something to go wrong. I prayed to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta because you taught us that the grandfather is more lenient than the father. What a fortunate day in my life!

Class ended, you walked out the door, I was nervous and knelt down with your slippers in my hands a few inches from the floor. What a fortunate day in my life! I was waiting for you to lift your feet one at a time so I could put your slippers on. You waited for me to put your slippers down on the floor so you could slip your feet into them. Finally I realized what a fool I was, and put your slippers on the floor. You put your feet into them and walked away. I offered my obeisances and never wanted to get up again. I felt this was the most unfortunate day in my life.

I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I may someday become qualified to put your slippers on your lotus feet without becoming puffed-up.

(Written by Pyārī-mohana dāsa )

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Hawaii

*eka bhāgavata baḍa—bhāgavata-śāstra*  
*āra bhāgavata—bhakta bhakti-rasa-pātra*  
*(Cc Ādi-līlā 1.99)*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

There are two *bhāgavatas*—the great scripture *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and the pure devotee absorbed in the mellows of devotion. Your *līlās* are not bound by time and space; however, some remain more firmly fixed in my unsteady mind, and they exemplify your pure *mahā-bhāgavata* vision.

Sometime in 1974, Śrīla Prabhupāda was residing in Māyāpur, where his quarters still remain intact. I was running up the steps, two at a time, one evening, heading toward the *brahmacārī* floor, but noticed a commotion at the far end of the floor. Walking a few steps I saw his form, resplendent in its silken cloth. I bowed.

Śrīlea Prabhupada had seen a cobra in his bathroom, someone said, as several devotees brandishing flashlights peered here and there behind pipes, under sinks, etc. “There may even be two,” said someone else. Śrīlea Prabhupada leaned casually, meanwhile, as fixed as the Himalayas, just gravely observing. Radioman was particularly anxious, “Where should I sleep—on a shelf, maybe the roof?” After a few more minutes of vain searching, Śrīlea Prabhupada said wryly, “So many men for one snake?” and immediately the fruitless search was finished, and everyone went to take rest.

The next day after *jape* several of us waited as Śrīlea Prabhupada came out of his room, toting his cane, ready for his morning walk. He paused on his way toward the steps and, turning slightly, he said, “ Anyone was bit by the snake?” Walking toward the steps, he turned, just about to descend, then he stopped again and, looking back, he said, “Pradyumna had fled?” He had a transcendental glint of humor in his eye. One could only imagine Pradyumna and how difficult it would be for him to flee with so many trunks of obscure books.

After his morning walk and *guru-puja,* during the *Bhāgavatam* class, Śrīla Prabhupāda spoke on Kuntī-devī’s prayers. He was emphasizing our dangerous position in the material world with his ever-fresh transcendental genius. Suddenly he said, with his wonderful spontaneity, “We think we are safe in our four-story marble building, but the snake is there, and what is the snake? The snake is death.”

Often the snake is given as a representative of time and death—*kala-sarpa.* It may strike at any time, just as our snakelike senses may also strike at any object and thus mislead us. Beyond this a multitude of envious snakelike persons surround us in this “dungeon of demons.”

Let us not take shelter of the false ego or the external energy. *Durāśayā ye bahir-artha-māninas….kāmam āśritya duṣpūram:* We shall fill our thirsty ears with your amazing unique songs (*govinda-gaṇāmṛtaiḥ)* and thus transcend this horrible ocean of material desire and forever dive and swim in the ecstasy of your pastimes (*premāmṛtambo-nidhī).*

*Your servants of the Hawaiian Islands*

## Heidelberg

Time is passing, almost frightening,  
It cannot be an hopless race.  
The soldiers of nescience, never tired of marching,  
Please help us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Your Divine Grace,  
Victory—for sure come,  
Otherwise, the greatest blunder.  
The soul’s defeat, no higher affliction,  
You carry the mace to make us surrender.  
Blinded by material illusion,  
Proud of our tiny independence.  
You have to force us, there is no other solution,  
To experience the joy of devotional ecstasy.  
Abandoning society, love, and friendship,  
At your divine master’s command.  
Traversing the path of loving hardship,  
Serving your mission, our only demand.  
Time is passing, almost frightening,  
Soon another life we have to face.  
Constantly hearing your divine voice challenging,  
Please help us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Your Divine Grace.  
“Intelligent boys, rich man’s sons,  
Do something big, be happy and jolly.  
Big, big monkey, big, big belly,  
Ceylon jumping, melancholy.”  
Let us be forever your servant,  
Nothing I call to be mine.  
No exchange , like a foolish merchant,  
When, my lord and master, when will that day be mine?  
Time is passing, almost frightening,  
We are determined to win the race.  
Courage like soldiers, ready for fighting,  
Please help us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Your Divine Grace.

*(Written by Avināśa-candra dāsa )*

## Hong Kong

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

It is stated in the scripture that amongst the four sages—Satya, Tretā, Dvāpara and Kali—Kali-yuga is actually the best because in this age Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu very mercifully gives love of God by His *saṅkīrtana* movement. It is also said:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye 'nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*  
*(Bhāg. 2.4.18)*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we are living in a very degraded time and are coming from a most condemned race, still, by your establishment of ISKCON and your kind instructions on how to perform *saṅkīrtana,* you are relieving us from the embarrassing conditions of material life and bestowing upon us our human right—to realize our eternal relationship with you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. You once said that the Khasa race will be the last to become devotees, but they will be the best. We may not know how and when, but one thing we know is that within this statement you have given us all encouragement and blessings. Just like the youngest child in the family knows only to depend on the father’s kindness, we simply know to take shelter of your lotus feet, which are the reservoir of all benedictions. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are *patita-pāvana,* savior of even the Khasa race! We worship your lotus feet, our eternal object of meditation.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us all bow down at your lotus feet and beg for the opportunity to engage in your worldwide movement of *saṅkīrtana.* Otherwise, what is our hope?

*Your eternal servants in Khasadeśa,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Girihari Swami / Jitārāti dāsa / Durāśaya dāsa / Rāmaśarana dāsa

## Houston

Dear Śrīla Prahupada,

You built a house of bhakti  
Under the roof of the holy name.  
Let us live in cooperation;  
Without each other, we are lame.  
Let us not want to have control,  
Let us instead serve the devotees, as we should,  
Which is the pure nature of the soul.  
Let us glorify the house you built  
For the world to reside in peace,  
A servant’s quarters for Kṛṣṇa’s devotees  
Within which the effects of Māyā’s cease.  
Let us leave our pride and jealousy  
Outside that sacred place,  
Entering with sincere humility,  
Inviting with us the human race.

*(*Written by Gopāla *Ācārya* dāsa)

*Begging to remain your servants,*  
*The servants of ISKCON of Houston, Texas*

## Johannesburg

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, which are the only shelter in this age of Kali. On this most auspicious occasion we humbly submit ourselves as aspiring disciples of Your Divine Grace. We beg for your mercy that we may become disciples in the true sense of the word. The essence of spiritual life is to please the spiritual master. The order of the spiritual master is the life and soul of the disciple, and the disciple takes great pleasure in executing that order, no matter how difficult it may be.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we may become more pleasing to Your Divine Grace by taking up the banner of Lord Caitanya and traveling all over the land to distribute the nectar of the holy name. You have given us the formula for success—the holy name, *prasadam,* and book distribution. But still we do not listen, and we try to concoct new ideas. Kindly invest us with the strength to give up all desires for sense gratification, even the most deeply rooted, and to simply execute your order, leaving aside all other considerations.

The means to execute your desire and will is contained within your books. You have said that everything you wanted to say in in your books. Kṛṣṇa consciousness means “all questions answered and all problems solved.” All the answers are there in your books, but we do not hear.

Your spiritual master remarked once about you: “He likes to hear. He does not go away. I have marked him.” Spiritual life begins with hearing. The more we hear and listen to you, the more we will learn how to please you. You have said that our love for you will be shown by our spirit of cooperation. May we learn how to truly cooperate with one another by giving up the tendency for fault-finding, blaspheming, etc., and to appreciate the services of our Godbrothers and Godsisters by seeing how they are endeavoring to push forward your movement.

The debt to the spiritual master can never be repaid, but by working cooperatively, distributing your books and attempting to preach as you did, then we can at least bring you some pleasure instead of only causing you disturbance.

So, on this auspicious day we pray and rededicate ourselves to becoming more determined in hearing your instructions and executing them so that one day, by the miraculous potency of your mercy, we may become adorned with all the qualities of the Vaiṣṇava.

*Begging for your mercy, your fallen servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## KHQN

Dear Śrīla Prahupada,

Please accept our useless obeisances. All glories to you and your exemplary service to Lord Kṛṣṇa and your spiritual master.

In this material world there is great confusion due to our determined desire not to accept God and His supreme authority. Consequently we suffer insurmountable frustration in our search for immortality. However, by coming in contact with you, the willing gift of the Supreme, the material world can be understood as secondary. And as that association continues, one begins to acknowledge the primary status of spirit. Then, through further association, one comes to engage in service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, the eternally satisfying condition of the spirit soul, according to your desires. Thus one is given the opportunity to go back home, back to Godhead.

Even in material thought we have the concept of “opportunity knocking,” meaning that somehow or other one is presented with opportunity and the question becomes, “What will be done with it?” None of us asked you for the opportunity, but you came knocking, and knocking, and knocking and knocking. So we pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that we will awaken and hear the knocking, then that we will have the strength to turn from material life and move to the door, and lastly that we can muster the intelligence to take full advantage of the opportunity you so relentlessly present to anyone and everyone.

*Your eternal servants at KHQN (Radio Kṛṣṇa), Govinda’s Buffet, and the Festival of India.*

Cāru dāsa / Vaibhavī-devī dāsī / Vatsala dāsa / Śaśi-kalā-devī dāsī / Lokanātha dāsa / Balarāma dāsa / Śrīnāma dāsa / Madhuhā dāsa / Bhagavatī-devī dāsī / Haridāsa / Nityanātha dāsa / Jaya Gopala dāsa / Nṛsiṁha-deva dāsa / Cakravartī dāsa / Āditya-devī dāsī / Bhakta John / Bhakta Garth / Dharmarāja dāsa / Prītīlakṣaṇa-devī dāsī / Sūrāṅgī-devī dāsī / Mirābaī-devī dāsī

## Kitwe, Zambia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this occasion of the celebrated appearance of our most dear and revered spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I would like to feel that not only is he with us in thought and word, but perhaps by his great mercy and the will of the Supreme, all-knowing Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, this year will prove especially significant. Why particularly this year? Well, friends and devotees, have we not seen how Śrīla Prabhupāda eventfully performed great actions during the double-figure years of 1922, 1933, 1944, 1955, 1966, and 1977? And can we not expect something significant by His Divine Grace in 1988?

In 1922 Śrīla Prabhupāda first met his spiritual master, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati, who immediately impressed upon Acharya Caraṇa the necessity of preaching in the English language to the Western world. Then in 1933 Śrīla Prabhupāda was formally initiated by his spiritual master. Now, we come to 1944, which was the eventful year in which Śrīla Prabhupāda began publishing *Back to Godhead,* which was and is, in his own words, the backbone of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Just about ten years later Śrīla Prabhupāda left his wife and family to take up the order of *vānaprastha* and in 1966 he rented the storefront called “Matchless Gifts” on Manhattan’s Lower East Side, from where his preaching first began to take root and grow. The same year he registered the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, based on the same principle which Śrīla Prabhupāda first inculcated in the League of Devotees in India. In 1977 was, of course, the year of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s departure from the material world to join the cowherd boy Kṛṣṇa in His eternal pastimes in the spiritual world.

Now, O devotees, can we perhaps from this pattern of significant events not see something forthcoming for the world in 1988 directly from Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite his seeming physical absence? If he is in his books as he always said he was, I would have to suspect that the *vāṇī,* or words of His Divine Grace, may perform some wonderful work, since so many living beings on the planet have read and are reading the literatures of Śrīla Prabhupāda. What that event is specifically I cannot say, but surely we can expect His Divine Grace to unfold another pastime for us who have remained faithful to his order. And to be faithful to his order we must unitedly cooperate as his disciples to loudly broadcast the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and simultaneously practice all the precepts which he so kindly and mercifully laid down.

O Prabhupāda, may this be the year in which the fallen conditioned souls realize your great gift to the world and, giving up their false ego, false pride and false strength, surrender wholeheartedly to your lotus feet, which can carry one and all back to home, back to Godhead.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Upananda dāsa

## Korea

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**And a Saint Walked Amongst Us**

From the holy land of Vṛndāvana you came  
To walk amidst us, the hardened streets of New York.  
As the touchstone turns iron into gold,  
You came and touched our hearts.

In the short time you were with us  
You gave so much, yet took so little.  
Renouncing family life and your own personal comforts,  
You traveled all over the world spreading the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

You established temples wherever you went  
And taught us the process of worshipping the Deity.  
You gave us Kṛṣṇa-prasadam and the holy name.  
And most significantly you gave us transcendental literatures,  
Which will become the lawbooks for all mankind.

In essence you have given of yourself to the world,  
And all you asked in return was that we assist you in  
Whatever we are able.  
How can we not surrender, having seen your example?  
Let us not hesitate and, like Arjuna, prepare to do battle.

*Your granddisciples,*  
Jaya Baladeva dāsa / Jaimini dāsa

## Laguna Beach

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances.

You once said, “ This service which I have given you is all that you know of love.” Prabhupāda, there is no light in this world except for your pure teachings. We want to understand them completely and give them to the world. Only then will our lives have meaning.

We see how easily even important instructions are misunderstood or forgotten, how *mleccha* habits tend to replace Vedic culture and Vaiṣṇava etiquette.

You give us a complete way of life—a spiritual culture. You give us music, art, philosophy, cuisine, deity worship, and brahminical *dharma.* You give us Lord Caitanya, Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, and the association of saintly persons, past and present. You give us all instructions regarding these things, and we want to see that nothing is lost. We want to see that every aspect of the spiritual culture that you taught us is alive in our lives. We want to establish a solid framework so that this way of life can be transmitted completely intact for generations to come. If we can play some small part in giving this gift of yours to the world, our lives will be successful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as our service to you in Laguna Beach, we want to make this temple a showcase of Vedic culture. We want to establish a pure and simple standard of Deity worship. We are learning to prepare *prasadam* as you like it. We are gradually raising the level of cleanliness to the standard you wanted. We are trying to adopt the personal habits and behavior that you so perfectly exhibited. By practically trying to apply your instructions, we are feeling your presence more and more in our lives. Our vision is becoming clearer, and we are enthused to improve more. As we feel the internal purity that comes from your association, we are encouraged that perhaps one day we will be your true representatives.

Our only unhappiness is that we do not fully represent you. By meeting us, people do not understand what a true devotee is. They do not become inspired with sufficient faith to give up material life and blissfully surrender to you. We wish that it was you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, giving the classes, leading the chanting and preaching, so that the world could see your perfect example. In your absence we are forced to try to represent you, but how can one with an unclean heart represent one whose heart is pure?

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we do not want an empty show of spiritual life, a thin veneer exhibited for so-called preaching. We want to fully understand and embrace you within our hearts. Please let us always think of you and pray to you. Please inspire us with pure vision so that we can always serve you and please you. Please accept us as your servants, and let us play some small part in your mission in this world.

*Your aspiring servants of Laguna Beach.*

## Lansing, Michigan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

“In this material world, when one accepts some particular type of service, he is required to possess some particular type of qualification also. Without this, one is unfit for such service. But in the devotional service of the Lord, the only qualification required is surrender.” (*Bhāg.* 2.7.46purport) Although we are unqualified, you engage us in varieties of devotional service. You see the pure spirit soul beyond the outer material coverings and disregard that we may be *mlecchas* and *yavanas.* It does not matter whether we be American or Indian; white or black; educated or ignorant; man, woman, or child; rich or poor; old or young; beautiful or ugly; *sannyāsī* or householder, *śūdra brāhmaṇa.* Indeed, even aborigines and cannibals are not deterred from the Lord’s service. These designations do not affect our chance to revive our genuine nature of *sat, cit,* and *ānanda.* The only stipulation is that we surrender to you. First, by hearing from you, our dormant constitutional position of Kṛṣṇa consciousness awakens.

“Śrī Sūta Gosvami said: O God, although we are born in a mixed caste, we are still promoted in birthright simply by serving and following the great who are advanced in knowledge. Even by conversing with such great souls, one can without delay cleanse oneself of all disqualifications resulting from lower births.” (*Bhāg.* 1.18.18)

“Surrendered souls, even from groups leading sinful lives, such as women, the labor class, the mountaineers and the Siberians, or even the birds and beasts, can also know about the science of Godhead and become liberated from pure devotees of the Lord and by following in their footsteps in devotional service.” (*Bhāg.* 2.7.46)

In a fast flowing river, the small fish take shelter of the river and thus are able to easily swim upstream, against the swift-moving current. The hardy elephant, taking shelter of his own prowess, is quickly swept downstream. And so, rather than be discouraged by our lack of qualification, we are praying to always remain tightly under the shelter of your lotus feet. We don’t know how we are continuing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, except that you are maintaining us. You are carrying what we lack by habituating us to always remain steadfast in the regulative principles of *sādhana-bhakti.* And although we are greatly lacking in so many ways, and not increasing our service to you as we know we should, we are dependent on your mercy. You are fanning the small spark of devotion in our steel-framed hearts, and gradually our affection for you is increasing. You are teaching us to transfer our attachments to Lord Kṛṣṇa rather than negate them. “The Americans who have joined the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement were materially intoxicated before they became devotees, but now that their intoxication is over, their material assets have become spiritual assets that may be helpful in furthering the service of Kṛṣṇa… Everything can be used in the service of Kṛṣṇa. If one remains intoxicated and does not use one’s material assets for the service of Kṛṣṇa, they are not very valuable, but if one can use them for the service of Kṛṣṇa, they become extremely valuable.” (*TQK,* p. 59)

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being more kind and easier to approach than the Lord Himself. We are eternally indebted to you.

*yeṣāṁ tv anta-gataṁ pāpaṁ*  
*janānāṁ puṇya-karmaṇām*  
*te dvandva-moha-nirmuktā*  
*bhajante māṁ dṛḍha-vratāḥ*

**“Persons who have acted piously in previous lives and in this life, whose sinful actions are completely eradicated and who are freed from the duality of delusion, engage themselves in My service and determination.” (Bg. 7.28)**

Coming to you with no qualifications, you make us pious, eradicate our sinful actions, deliver us from delusion, and engage us in the Lord’s unlimited service. Now we are begging for the determination to give up our petty weaknesses and be of real service to you. Then, with purified hearts, we will be able to induce others to approach you. Only then will our lives be of any significance.

*Your eternal servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Apūrva dāsa / Kāmalinī-devī dāsī

Pañca-tattva / Amalā-purāṇa

## Leicester, U.K.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Helpless, foolish, undeserving, we prostrate ourselves in the sacred dust of your lotus feet, the only shelter within the three worlds.

We feel ashamed in approaching Your Divine Grace. You are so noble, so pure, so perfect. The clear and refreshing brightness of your glance, your words, your smile, like the first rays of the morning sun, is painful to the drunken fool who lives in hiding beneath the veil of night.

Like the drunken fool, we who are intoxicated with myriad desirers, steeped in illusion, pray for your causeless mercy. We want to be near you, to follow you and to serve you, and yet we depend on your mercy to invoke the seeds of surrender, which lie deep within the darkness of our clouded hearts, to emerge, grow, and finally bear fruit. The fruits will be yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to pluck and offer to Kṛṣṇa at the time and in the manner that you see fit.

In our small Leicester branch of the ISKCON British Yatra tree, we are feeling secure and hopeful in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have not been exempt from the tensions, mistrust, and confusion which have beset your sacred movement in recent years, but by your grace we have beset your sacred movement in recent years, but by your grace we have learned some valuable lessons. We know more clearly than ever that the shelter of your instructions is the only safe place within this material world, and we know that the slightest departure from that shelter will, if not corrected, cause the reawakening of limitless material desires and the descent into a life of illusion and suffering.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day we, your children, have gathered together in your house to remember and glorify your wonderful qualities, your uncompromising service to your divine spiritual master, your amazing accomplishments, and your compassionate teachings and instructions.

On this auspicious day we offer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our loyalty to your ISKCON movement, our commitment to remain steady and responsible in our devotional service, our pledge to carry your message to whomever we meet, and our promise to follow your instructions as they are in a spirit of cooperation with all the members of your transcendental ISKCON family.

*Begging to remain eternally your humble servants, the devotees of Leicester,*

## Lima

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

In the *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa,* we find the following *śloka* about the real spiritual master, and we see that such a definition is perfectly applicable to Your Divine Grace.

*kṛpa-sindhuḥ su-sampūrṇaḥ sarva-sattvopakārakaḥ*  
*nispṛhaḥ sarvataḥ siddhaḥ sarva-vidyā-viśāradaḥ*  
*sarva-saṁśaya-saṁchettā ’nalaso gurur āhṛtaḥ*

**“One who is an ocean of mercy, who is fulfilled in all respects, who is possessed of all good qualities, who always works for the benefit of all souls, who is free from lust, who is perfect in all respects, who is well-versed in the scriptures, and who knows the science of Kṛṣṇa, who can remove all the doubts of his disciples, and who is always alert in the service of Kṛṣṇa is to be known as a** ***guru. (Hari-bhakti-vilāsa*** **1.45.46, quoted from** ***Viṣṇu-smṛti-vacana)***

So let us remember in each act of our lives, and not only on some particular occasion, that you have given us everything necessary to end this material existence, that even though you are not physically present, you keep on guiding us towards Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have shown that we are still your inexperienced children, sometimes even trying to imitate the father instead of following his instructions strictly. Even so, we know that you are so merciful that you always protect us and give us a new opportunity to rectify our mistakes and to take the correct path.

There is no doubt that you are the *mahā-bhāgavata* who can take all of us back to the spiritual world. Śrīla Prabhupāda-kī-jaya!

*Your servants of Lima Mandir,*

## London

To His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, on the holy occasion of his Vyāsa-pūjā day, Śrī Nandotsava 1988.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*vāñcā-kalpatharubhyaś ca*  
*kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo*  
*vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namah*

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On such an auspicious day as this, your transcendental qualities become even more manifest, and on this particular occasion your quality of *kṛpā-sindhu,* the ocean of mercy, seems to encompass all the others, just as the ocean encompasses so many things.

The ocean symbolizes infinity, depth, and power, yet can present the most picturesque panorama of beauty on a moonlit night. In the same way, despite your depth of learning and power, your picturesque beauty always shines through in the most charming manner. The ocean is the source of many sought-after precious jewels like pearls and coral, and in the same way you are the source of the jewels of Vedic knowledge, which learned scholars pursue with great endeavor. The ocean is the natural habitat for millions of living entities of myriad shapes and sizes. In the same way, one astrologer has noted that here is a person who can build a house in which the whole world can live. This you have shown by creating ISKCON, which is giving shelter to all kinds of living entities. The ocean is never disturbed by the countless rivers and streams that constantly pour into it, and we have seen you similarly remain completely undisturbed in the face of impending calamites. On the other hand, just as the ocean sometimes transgresses the natural boundary of the shoreline and wreaks untold havoc on the principles of Kali-yuga. Your ISKCON is sustaining so many farms, temples, *āśramas,* restaurants and book publishers, just as the ocean sustains many big continents and islands. *Yogīs* very highly appreciate the qualities of salt water for cleansing the digestive system, and your admirers similarly are thrilled by your salty comments on material life, which usually startle the ordinary man. The seaside atmosphere is considered amongst the best for healthy purposes, and in the same way, the very atmosphere you have created by your teachings is enlightening all those who come into contact with it. Just as the sun takes water from the ocean and redistributes it over the planet in the form of rain, so your teachings are being absorbed and redistributed in all the languages of the world. You are guiding countless millions of souls from the material to the spiritual world, just as the ocean facilitates traveling from one place to another. The sound of the ocean, even from far away, is found to be very soothing, and the sound of your recorded *bhajanas* and lectures always soothes away the material anxieties. Learned scholars of this age have acknowledged your unique presentation of *bhakti-yoga,* just as the sun is seen to rise out of the ocean; therefore there is no doubt that you are certainly a veritable ocean of mercy, and we see that even people like Mrs. Sally Agarwal, with whose family you first stayed in America, cannot help emphatically describing you are oceanic.

Here in London we are trying to serve you as best as we can, and in this special day, we would like to offer our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet and pray to be eternally engaged as your menial servants. All glories to the ocean of mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara Mandira,*

## Long Island

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are bringing mercy to all conditional souls from the ocean of mercy, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You have left us your mission—for your servants to continue distributing the mercy of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi in the form of the International Society of Krishna Consciousness. Our link to you is by serving your transcendental order to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in every town and village. The true association between the disciple and spiritual master is through the instruction and transcendental order of the spiritual preceptor. As it is said in *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (6.23), “Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” Our faith in your order to cooperate together under the banner of ISKCON is the test of our love for you. Please give us the strength to fully surrender to your transcendental order and act as your instruments in service to Lord Caitanya. Your mercy is all that we are made of. We are begging for the shade of your lotus feet.

*Your servants at ISKCON Long Island,*

## Longdenville, Trinidad

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. To please your spiritual master you kindly come to the Western countries to carry out the mission of Lord Caitanya—to engage everyone in this *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement so that they can end this miserable, temporary existence in this material world, go home, back to Godhead, and enjoy eternal life, full of knowledge and bliss.

In carrying out the order of your *Guru* Mahārāja, you have been so kind to us. You have given us the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, to chant. You have left us many wonderful books, explaining this supreme science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, so that we can become fixed in our understanding and for others also to become convinced of the authenticity of this great movement. You have established many beautiful temples, spiritual palaces, where devotees can come together, glorify, and worship the Deity of Kṛṣṇa. You have established the *Guru*kula system of education to train our children to become devotees and so not be slaughtered in the modern “educational” institutions.

You have established scientific institutes, so that the “learned scholars” of this planet can become attracted, convinced that Kṛṣṇa is God, and surrender at His lotus feet. You have taught us to hold many wonderful festivals to attract the general mass of people, so that they not only hear the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, but also partake of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy in the form of delicious *prasadam.* And ever so important, you have given us this society of wonderful devotees, without each other’s association, no one can become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are very merciful. There is so much you have done for us. We have scarcely touched upon what you have achieved—your superexcellent qualities, your unlimitedly wonderful patience. It is impossible to fully describe these, what to speak of repaying you. However, we beg at your lotus feet to be blessed with this understanding and to be blessed to continue to appreciate this more and more—that the only way we can truly love you, really show appreciation for what you have done for us, is for devotees in this wonderful ISKCON movement to cooperate and work together more and more, in an atmosphere of friendship, love and trust, as you yourself have explained in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* 4.30.8. Otherwise none of your matchless gifts can be maintained, what to speak of expanding upon the solid foundation you have given us.

*We beg to become your surrendered servants at ISKCON Longdenville,*

## Los Angeles

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet, which have given shelter to all living entities, just as the lotus feet of Lord Vāmanadeva gave shelter to Bali Mahārāja and all the inhabitants of the universe.

Crowded and packed to capacity, the room was full of devotees from temples all around the world. You recited the *mantras* in obeisance to the disciplic succession after a rousing *kirtana.* Suddenly a slender, elderly Indian man shot to his feet, arms thrust into the air, exuberantly breaking the momentary pause of silence—“Whole world sunshine, Prabhupāda!” he shouted as a wave of approving ecstasy swept through the crowd. (Māyāpur, 1975-76)

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have certainly brought sunshine to the whole world, which would otherwise be immersed in the ignorance of repeated birth and death. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is as brilliant as the sun, which has arisen just to dispel the darkness of this age. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the person *Bhāgavata,* the personification of all Vedic knowledge, the bestower of perfection. You continue illuminating the three worlds even in your physical absence (in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you have written that this *saṅkīrtana* movement will spread throughout the universe.)

“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of Providence, just so that we may accept you as the captain of the ship for those who desire to cross over the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.” (*Bhāg.* 1.1.22)

You explain that anyone wishing to cross this dangerous ocean “…must follow the footsteps of the sages of Naimiṣāraṇya and accept Śrī Suta Gosvami or his bona fide representative as the captain of the ship. The ship is the message of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the shape of *Bhagavad-gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.”*

Certainly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had you not sacrificed just for our benefit, we would never have heard the wonderful sound of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* nor would we have ever been able to strive to give away this means of enlightenment to others. Now, under the shelter of your lotus feet, we feel inspired to endeavor to inundate the entire world in your message, which is that very same message of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

“Who is there, desiring the deliverance from the vices of the age of quarrel, who is not willing to hear the virtuous glories of the Lord?” (*Bhāg.* 1.1.16)

Yes Śrīla Prabhupāda, whatever small desire we may have to hear about the glories of the Supreme Lord has been created by you. More than once you yourself told us, “I have created your good fortune.” We get a hint of your true position from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* itself:

“My dear Lord, You are glorified by the selected verses uttered by great personalities. Such glorification on Your lotus feet is just like saffron particles. When the transcendental vibration from the mouths of great devotees carries the aroma of the saffron dust of Your lotus feet, the forgetful living entity gradually remembers his eternal relationship with You.” (*Bhāg.* 4.20.25, purport)

Fortunately, we are still hearing your sweet voice carrying those saffron mercy particles, and even if that were not possible, you live in your instructions and in your writings. Yes, you are that captain of the ship, and you are still guiding us by your causeless mercy and compassion, even though you have left behind your physical form. Whatever success we may experience in endeavoring to push on this movement is only to your credit, for we are indeed blind. Somehow or other we need not only continue hearing your message and clinging to your order for the next ten thousand years.

(Written by Makhanlāl dāsa)

*Respectfully submitted,*

*Your servants, the citizens of New Dvārakā,*

## Madison

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me. Unfortunately I’m not running behind you shouting “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!” as the song says. Instead, I’m always asking you for so many other things and, merciful as you are, you give them to me in a way that they don’t stop my devotional service.

I really, really feel as though you are my father. You’ve given me everything. I’m completely satisfied with the diet you’ve given of *bhagavat-prasadam.* You’ve given me a very satisfying and fulfilling job in carrying on our family tradition of preaching; a nice residence in your temple; you taught me how to play *mṛdaṅga* on your instruction tape; you’ve given me a nice, faithful wife; some cooking talents; the best literature and philosophy; as well as practical instructions on how to organize my life and priorities.

This all surpasses what my material father had given me.

Someday, I know, you’ll give me Kṛṣṇa. Logically I can see you’ve given me everything else I’ve wanted. Now it’s up to me to want Him only.

Thank you very much. Please give me some more service to help me purify my desires.

*Your insignificant servants at Madison, Wisconsin,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Mirtasena dāsa / Mahārha-devī dāsī / Bhakta John / Bhakta Joe / Bhaktin Lori / Mukunda-Mādhava

## Malaysia

*oṁ ajñana-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlītaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Eternal, unlimited glories to thee,  
The greatest devotee of the Lord,  
Setting your lotus feet on this fallen planet.

Your smile, like the powerful sun,  
Dissipates the darkness of ignorance.  
Your words are more powerful than unlimited brahmāstras  
Killing demons and doubts far and near.

Your hands use śāstra as Arjuna used śastras  
To even more inconceivable effect.  
Your legs travel for distance, far more than the Gaṅgā,  
To purify and transform.

Your lotus feet are the shelter, and will be for all time.  
For the fortunate souls who in any way receive  
A grain of dust from them.

Your heart is the reservoir from where we get life.  
Your instructions, our intelligence and our guide.

Response to your movements puts Cleopatra to shame,  
Your mind the reins, your followers drive,  
A medium for Kṛṣṇa to pour merciful rains,  
Extinguishing the suffering and the pains,  
Your each action wants more,  
All historic plays combined.

Your glance ignites the spiritual spark  
As Kṛṣṇa injects the jīva in the dark.  
Your kindness and mercy no limits can find.

You are always present, but we humbly pray,  
Please give us your mercy on this special day,  
Despite all temptations may we never stray.  
In absence of vapuḥ, your vāṇī we serve,  
The ISKCON you gave please may we preserve.

Unlimited, eternal glories to Your Divine Grace,  
The greatest of Vaiṣṇava who came to this place.

*Your humble servants in ISKCON Malaysia.*

## Manchester

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, another year has passed, and we approach you once again empty-handed. Although without any spiritual assets of our own, we are filled with great hope because of having contacted you. Certainly the rarity of such a fortunate meeting cannot be excessively emphasized, and this is confirmed repeatedly in the infallible *Vedas.*

In a puny attempt to repay our unlimited debt to you, we are trying to represent your pure message to the people here, and even in spite of our presence we see how wonderful its effect is. Your potency is truly mystical, for just by hearing about you, we see so many people becoming devotees. We also desire to become a devotee, and thus we have come before you today to beg that you pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa for us. Please once more overlook our stupidity; one day we will become your servants, and this will only be due to your being endlessly compassionate.

*Your fallen servants,*

Kṛṣṇa-dharma dāsa / Abhaya dāsa / *Guru*-dakṣina dāsa / Dāruka dāsa / Bhakta Richard / Bhakta Captain / Cintāmaṇi-dhāma-devī dāsī / Vraja-prāṇa-devī dāsī / Gaurāṅga-līlā-devī dāsī and congregation

## Manipur

Most merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*Kaṭha Upaniṣad* (1.3.14) emphasizes the importance of the human form of life and the spiritual path as:

*uttiṣṭhata jāgrata*  
*prāpya varān nibodhata*  
*kṣurasya dhārā niśitā duratyayā*  
*durgaṁ pathas tat kavayo vadanti*

**“Please wake up and try to understand the boon which you have now in this human form of life. The path of spiritual realization is very difficult; it is sharp like a razor’s edge. This is the opinion of learned transcendental scholars.”**

Everything is difficult without your sanction. We need your guidance at every moment and in every step to have a glimpse of spiritual life. So our humble plea to you on this most auspicious day is: Please do not forget your insignificant servants in this distant land of this globe.

You planned to bless Manipur and its inhabitants by your visit in 1976 and 1977, along with your disciples from the West. However, because of the delay in getting the innerline permit and other factors, that plan remains unfulfilled. So the Vaiṣṇava in Manipur are serving Your Divine Grace in separation. There are prominent, visible signs that you have given blessings to this land and its residents. As the years pass by, attachment to your divine mission is increasing at a greater pace, and many Vaiṣṇava are taking more and more responsibility in serving that divine mission. This is your glory, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The Vaiṣṇava in Manipur—this small, historic land of Babhrūvahana, the brave son of Arjuna—are praying for the blessings of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda that during your centennial appearance celebration, Your Divine Grace will visit Manipur with all your divine children from every corner of this planet. Preparations are underway to receive

Your Divine Grace at that time. By the touch of your divine mercy many sincere Vaiṣṇava from various sections of the society have come forward to join in these preparations:

Śrīmān Pārtha-sarathi dāsa, Deputy Director of Education, Government of Manipur—helping in translation of your books from English into Manipuri and overall preaching activities.

Śrīmān Rādhā-mohana dāsa, Additional Chief Engineer, I.F.C.D. Division, Government of Manipur—looking after construction of the temple and other works.  
Śrī Nīlamaṇi and Śrī Bakula, retired Inspectors of Schools—helping in running the Bhaktivedanta Mission of School and organizing cultural programs.  
Śrī R.K. Shitaljit Singh, one of the most respected Vedic scholars in Manipur—helping in translation work.  
Śrī Jagadish Sharma, a well-known *paṇḍita* and prolific preacher of *bhāgavata-dharma—*helping in many aspects of the mission.  
Śrī Nityānanda, Īśvara R.K. Thambousana, Yaima Singh, and many others—helping in organizing and coordinating various programs.  
Śrī Shyam Sharma—helping in making films of the history and background of *Bhagavata* culture in Manipur.  
Śrī O.P. Agarwal, L.K.Singh, Ibochouba, Dusari Gopal, and others—helping in raising funds for these projects.

*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* substantiates,

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi*** **1.45).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by our blessings we are firmly convinced that all these projects will be manifested in due course of time.

*Your unworthy servants, the devotees of ISKCON Manipur.*

## Melbourne

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We offer our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

In the *Bhagavad-gītā,* Tenth Chapter, verse 9, Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself describes the nature and characteristics of His pure devotee. “The thoughts of My pure devotee dwell in Me, their lives are surrendered to Me, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss enlightening one another and conversing about Me.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always surrounded either by a few intimate servants to lovingly tend to your every need, or by hundreds of chanting, dancing disciples eagerly tasting every drop of transcendental nectar emanating from your lotus mouth. Whether it was your ecstatic chanting of *kirtana, japa* or lectures, every word was for the glorification of Kṛṣṇa. This is only possible for the *paramahāṁsa* devotee of Kṛṣṇa, who is constantly absorbed in thoughts of Him.

It is said that a fool reveals himself as he speaks; so you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, surely revealed yourself as being the foremost scholar of *śāstra* and exponent of devotion to Kṛṣṇa this age has known. In this connection Śrīpada Madhv*ācārya* has quoted a verse from the *Varāha Purāṇa:* “Some devotees of the Lord exhibit external symptoms acting like madmen, others are introspective devotees, and still others partake of both natures. It is by one’s steadfastness and by the merciful vibrations emanating from one’s mouth that one’s devotion can be judged, not otherwise.”

You were always completely surrendered to the will of Kṛṣṇa and naturally saw everything—even adversity—as His mercy. Whether it was the long struggle to break the shackles of the Indian subcontinent, to travel West on the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, fighting to gain ground in Māyā’s hostile New York City, or overseeing the development of ISKCON and solving a million and one problems around the world, your complete faith in and surrender to Kṛṣṇa never deviated one inch. You always took every opportunity to preach Kṛṣṇa’s glories and enlighten the devotees about the path of devotional service.

You have taught us everything—cleanliness and correct moral standards, Deity worship, Ratha-yātrā festivals, preparing *prasadam,* preaching, management, book publication, *japa,* and *kirtana.* The list goes on endlessly. Anything that is of value has come from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we beg to reaffirm our faith in your instructions and seek your special mercy that we may fully surrender to you, for we have no other shelter in this Kali-yuga.

*Your insignificant servants at Melbourne Mahāprabhu Mandira,*

## Mexico City

Allow us to offer our humble and respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupada A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. On this day of his divine advent, we are trying to choose appropriate words in his glorification.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*paramānanda-mādhavam*  
*Madhya 17.80*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

On this sacred day we are glorifying His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupada A.C. Bhaktivedanta Gosvami Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda, who factually demonstrated that he was on the *paramahāṁsa* level, which is the ultimate stage of perfection in the renounced order of life.

There wouldn’t be sufficient time, paper, or words to describe all of his achievements in serving the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, nor adequate intelligence to understand all of his transcendental acts in devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

In this regard, it says in the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta:*

*yāṅra citte kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*  
*Madhya 23.39*

**“Even the most learned man cannot understand the words, activities and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead.”**

During his stay among us, he clearly demonstrated the most elevated principle of renunciation enunciated by Śrīla Rūpa Goswami, which is,

*anāsaktasya viṣayān*  
*yathārham upayuñjataḥ*  
*nirbandhaḥ kṛṣṇa-sambandhe*  
*yuktaṁ vairāgyam ucyate*

*prāpañcikatayā buddhyā*  
*hari-sambandhi-vastunaḥ*  
*mumukṣubhiḥ parityāgo*  
*vairāgyaṁ phalgu kathyate*  
*(Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.2.255-256)*

**“One is said to be situated in the fully renounced order of life if he lives in accordance with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He should be without attachment for sense gratification and should accept only what is necessary for the upkeep of the body. On the other hand, one who renounces things which could be used in the service of Kṛṣṇa, under the pretext that such things are material, does not practice complete renunciation.”**

One who can control his senses in spite of temptation is called a *gosvāmī.* Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya says in His *Śikṣāṣṭaka* prayers, *na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīṁ…: “*nor do I want any number of followers….”

Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in one letter: “My main purpose is to propagate the teachings of Lord Caitanya, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I am not after recruiting some disciples; but for preaching work we want some assistance, and if someone voluntarily offers his service, it is welcome.”

He had thousands of followers, who collected thousands of dollars, thousands of attractive female disciples, and so many people who wanted to become his disciples, but he engaged them in all the service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in his translation of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, dhanam:* “The spiritual master is not actually happy if the disciple brings him money, but when he sees that a disciple is following the regulative principles and advancing in spiritual life, he is very glad and feels obliged to such an advanced disciple.” (Cc. *Adi* 7.91)

In his final instructions he says : “Our money is only spent for Kṛṣṇa—Kṛṣṇa’s palace, Kṛṣṇaṣ temple, Kṛṣṇa’s worship, Kṛṣṇa’s glorification. It is not for our personal enjoyment. This is human life.”

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

*His respectful servants at Mexico City Mandira,*

Hari-kanthā dāsa / Prahlāda dāsa / Eka-bhoktā dāsa / Saṅkīrtana dāsa / Maitreya dāsa / Giri-Govardhana dāsa / Akarma dāsa / Padyāvalī-devī dāsī / Rāma-śraddhā-devī dāsī / Viṣṇupriya-devī dāsī

## Miami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this most auspicious anniversary of your appearance, I would like to share some remembrances of how you were always showing us the spiritual nature of our relationship with you—that you are our eternal spiritual father.

I remember the first time you were going to leave the tiny storefront at 26 Second Avenue, your first American temple. It was January 1967, and you were going to San Francisco. We had had your association for only a few months, but you were already everything to us. One devotee asked you, “I hear you’re going to be leaving in a couple of days, Swamiji.” And you answered, concerned, “Do you think I would ever leave you? Don’t think like that. I am always with you, and you are always with me.”

Just before you left, you walked into the temple room and offered your *dandavats* to the pictures of your *Guru* Mahārāja and Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thākura. Then you turned to us and said, “The grandfather and great-grandfather take care of the children better than the father.” This showed us that through yourself you were giving us a relationship with the greatest family, the disciplic succession, and that it was absolute, The *ācāryas* were even present in their picture.

Then you let us accompany you to the airport. Just before you boarded the 747, you called over your sons and daughters. You embraced each of your sons and patted each of your daughters on the head.

After returning from San Francisco, you stayed with us for a few more months, and then you were going to India. We were already feeling separation. Just before you left, you looked over at a new painting of the Rādhā-Madanamohana temple, which was just across the way from where you’d be staying in Vṛndāvana. You had been expressing some hesitation about going, because you knew we would not be able to hear it. But after seeing the painting, you said, “Now I can go to India. When you look at this picture, we will all be together.” Again you showed us that our relationship with you is unbreakable and eternal—spiritual and absolute.

While you were still in India, we continued our *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* discussions on what you taught us. We were talking about the five eternal relationships between the good and perfect.” You wrote back, “You should think of Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master simultaneously. The spiritual master is your spiritual father, and Kṛṣṇa is your spiritual husband. A girl cannot forget her father or her husband at any time.”

As the best father, you were always taking care of us. You would always write to us and say things like, “Please try to become more strengthful in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I am always praying to Kṛṣṇa for your advancement.”

While you were still in India, we continued our *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* discussions on what you taught us. We were talking about the five eternal relationships between the living entity and Kṛṣṇa when someone brought up that we also have an eternal relationship with you—that you are our eternal father. The others were amazed. Later we sent a transcript of that *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* to you. You sent it back with the comment that the devotee was right: you are our eternal father.

When you returned from India, we saw you again in New York. You were always trying to encourage us that our loving relationship was real, that we were your real family. You had been explaining how people in the material world like a thing not so much for its quality but for who made it. One devotee replied, “Well, we say that too. We say, ‘Oh, Prabhupāda said that? Then it is all right.” Exhibiting your intimacy with us and your limitless humility, you responded, “That is love; that is a different thing. Just like a mother has a child who is blind and out of love calls the child ‘lotus-eyed.’ ”

Later you came to Boston, where we had a temple on Glenville Avenue. In one of your lectures you said that although in your householder life you had five children, now you had five hundred children, who loved you more than your five children ever could. At the end of the lecture one devotee called out, “And we are growing children.” “Yes,” you answered, “when the children are growing there is less anxiety for the father.”

In another lecture you again indicated our relationship to you as your loving children. You were explaining to the people how God can be everything and still be a person: “Just like ISKCON they say A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami is everything. But still I am a person.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the greatest person and our eternal father, birth after birth. Please give us strength to preach your message everywhere and increase your family of loving devotees unlimitedly.

*Your servants at Miami Beach ISKCON,*

## Mombasa / Nairobi

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet, which are the only shelter for your glorious ISKCON movement.

The spiritual master is considered to be directly as good as the Supreme Lord because he gives transcendental knowledge for enlightenment. *Yasya sāksād bhagavati jñāna-dīpa-pade gurau.* The *ācārya* is considered to be as good as the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

If we are devoted and faithful to the order of Śrīla Prabhupāda we can conquer the influence of *sattva-guṇa, rajo-guṇa* and *tamo-guṇa. Yogīs* and *jñānīs* practice in many ways to conquer the senses, but the devotee immediately attains the mercy of Kṛṣṇa through the mercy of the spiritual master. *Yasya prasādad bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprāsādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi.* If the *guru* is pleased, certainly the Supreme Personality of Godhead is also pleased. “Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.23) Simply by rendering service to the spiritual master one crosses the ocean of nescience and returns home, back to Godhead.

The living entities are wandering in this material universe through different species of lives from time immemorial, but only due to the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master does the conditioned soul get the seed of devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are your servants, and we beg your causeless mercy on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā so we can advance on the path of pure *bhakti.* We pray at your lotus feet to bestow your kindness upon us and enable us to carry out Lord Caitanya’s message to the different corners of the globe. We are still struggling to gain a foothold in this part of Africa and to bring the message clearly, so the people of these lands can take advantage of the mercy you so kindly brought West. Certainly here in Africa it is a mission of mercy, but no more so than it was to New York in 1966. It is simply a question of us developing the purity so we in turn can be clear via media for this most sublime message which you have given us on behalf of Lord Caitanya. Please give us the courage and the vision to carry on and not to allow the forces of Māyā to attack and weaken our resolve.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept these souls as your aspiring servants,*

**Nairobi:**
