## Monterrey

*Ohe! Śrīla Prabhupāda*  
*Bahu grantha lekhe barnada gauranger sarva-lokjana jurite*  
*Bitaram ejnana kori, dukhi jane mora pran sampurna haite.*

**“O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are writing many books, describing the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Gaurāṅga, and you are giving soothing relief to the entire human society. Let me simply distribute this knowledge to those who are suffering and unhappy. Then my life will be perfect.”**

Our dear Param *Guru* and founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please listen to our request. We have the grand fortune to be your *upaśiṣyas.*You have given us the highest process to obtain *prema-bhakti,* as you affirm “through the ISKCON society.” So protect us from those who do not recognize your sacred Vaiṣṇava-saṅga(ISKCON), and allow us to remain here underneath your wings.

O Prabhupāda, you were the single grand *ācārya* who sacrificed everything for the sake of Lord Caitanya’s mission and your *Guru* Mahārāja and the previous *ācāryas.* You have come to the West to bestow Kṛṣṇa’s love through your books. What would it have been for us if you had never come? None of your Godbrothers can be compared to you, as you said: “There are many disciples of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Thākura, but to judge who is actually his disciple in executing the will of spiritual master …. One can very easily understand who is a genuine world-preacher and who is useless (*āsara*).” However, some envious so-called disciples have left you and propagate that those who you judged to be *kaniṣṭha-adhikārīs* are now grand *ācāryas,* and they can take your position. You considered this to be “an insult” to Your Divine Grace. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, protect us from such agents of Kali-yuga.

When Lord Caitanya left the earth, there appeared false followers, pseudo-Vaiṣṇava. So also, now that your *vāpu* is not present, some of your followers have gone bad. Consequently, we beg you, “Do not let us also make the same error. Allow us to be always your genuine followers, chanting sixteen rounds, following the four principles, attending the morning and evening programs, reading your books, preaching, distributing your books, going on *harināma,* living in the association of your devotees without long hair and moustaches of *māyā,* and serving your sacred Vaiṣṇava-saṅga ISKCON.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, ocean of mercy, we beg you to listen to our prayers. You state, “If a spiritual master can convert even one soul into a perfect pure devotee, his mission in life is fulfilled.” So who may doubt that you did not fulfill your mission in life? Therefore we ask you to protect us from those who are found to break the faith of your pure devotees.

O Prabhupāda, whole-heartedly we beg you, please bestow your mercy upon us so that we will never commit sin, nor deviate from your sacred instructions, and thus remain forever in your holy Vaiṣṇava-saṅga ISKCON, which means going back to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

*The servants of your servants, granddisciples of Monterrey, Gaura-Nitāi Mandira,*  
Hare Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Gaurāṅga Śakti dāsa / Bhakta Medardo / Bhakta Martin / Madhusūdana-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Carolina / Bhaktin Marta

## Nava Jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra, West Germany

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Begging for the ability to praise you,  
Praying for a spark of intelligence  
To understand your greatness, and hoping  
To one day be able to serve you perfectly,  
We, the insignificant residents of Nṛsiḥna-kṣetra,  
Bown down to your lotus feet  
With our utmost awe and reverence.

Mundane means prove to be totally insufficient to fully describe,  
Reward, and preserve your powerful endeavor.  
Your fame is not subject to the past, present, and future of this karmic world,  
Where everyone is sinking in the mire of illusion.  
You descended from the spiritual realm,  
Fulfilled your mission, fully situated in the spiritual realm, and you disappeared  
From our external sight into that spiritual realm.

Just as Kṛṣṇa carries the lotus of Goloka with Him wherever He appears,  
So you too, His mighty servant, never leave this divine lotus  
Of transcendental pastimes.

Therefore, taking shelter in you is the only protection  
From the dangerous mirage of this world.  
By your own sublime example you laid down the law of devotion.  
Showing us how to be pure,  
You spared us from the punishment awaiting forgetful souls.  
Without your protection we cannot attain the Supreme Lord’s protection.  
Without your appearance, the Supreme Lord does not appear.

In this age Kṛṣṇa comes in His name.  
But you are the one who carried the names!

In this age Kṛṣṇa is found in the Bhāgavatam.  
But you are the one who revealed it to us!

No one can see Kṛṣṇa in this world.  
But you established His arcā-vigraha!

No one sees light for this world.  
But your vision illuminates our future!

In this world everyone seems forlorn.  
But you gave us faith and the best friend!

In this world everyone is sent to an unknown place,  
But you directed us to the final abode!

Although you came to this world  
And soon again left,  
We fall at your merciful feet  
And beg you aloud:  
Please stay here  
And do not leave,  
For without you  
This world cannot move  
On to the Golden Age  
Of effulgent Mahāprabhu.  
Please grant us the mercy  
To meet you in our hearts.  
Let us be near you forever  
When this lonesome soul departs!

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## New Delhi

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvati Gosvami. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya-deva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

O Prabhupāda, wherefrom did you come?  
At the sinking of the sun,  
When twilight cast it’s gloom,  
You entered, great mystic,  
Saved us from our doom.

Your sunshine-heart spread its rays  
Into bleak Kali’s temples.  
Your Vaikuṇṭha smile revived those weary hearts;  
Kṛṣṇa’s soldiers ensemble!!  
The messiah is here!!

O Prabhupāda, wherefrom have you come,  
Today in the attire of a saffron mendicant?  
You are the moon whose silver rays  
Streak across the darkening sky of Kali.  
Your cooling flames relieve our burning hearts.

You only could capture the hearts of the ‘hippie-akiñcanas.”  
Your firm steps made their way across America’s byways,  
Your ancient cane soundings tidings  
Of the forgotten wisdom: “Veda.”

“Come, young boys and girls!” you beckoned,  
Your sweet bhajanas and kīrtana, and sweeter still your mystic pen  
Drenched our hearts in Kṛṣṇa-prema.  
Somehow the jaṁsū fruit of your mercy seemed to drown us  
In the ocean of Śrī Caitanya’s saṅkīrtana movement.

O Prabhupāda, who could imagine, you the gardener  
Harvested so many golden, wheatlike crops of Kṛṣṇa-bhaktas?  
With your scythe vijñāna  
You cut away all the weeds of doubt and misgivings.

O raincloud, your showers of Bhaktivedanta purports and letters  
Soothed our desert-parched minds.  
As the raincloud,, you are the true friend  
Of all moving and nonmoving living entities.

Today / caught a glimpse of your mighty power,  
But then again / was flung back into the storm of darkness.  
Your instructions will no doubt one day guide us out of this cyclone of ignorance,  
And being free, we will free others to join Caitanya’s dance.

O great musician, your orchestrated symphony of temples, farms, Gurukula, B.B.T., B.I.  
Filled our ears and changed the hearts of many.  
Bhumi’s hairs stood on end as Harināma spread her wings across unknown  
Horizons.  
All creatures dance in ecstasy as Prabhupāda plays on,  
And Mother Bhūmi feels, for the first time, relief since the time of Kurukṣetra.

Lord and master, my distressful tears of separation  
Are today heaped at your divine lotus feet,  
And the haunting question arises: Will we meet again?  
Will I feel your fathomless glance,  
Whose curtains of yogamāyā veil the eternal nature of Śyāmasundara?

O ocean of mercy, O Gurudeva,  
The earth lies vacant without your lotus-feet touch.  
And let this song be thrown to the wind  
If they say: “We shall not meet again!”  
My dear, eternal master and protector,  
On this day I bow down at your lotus feet,  
Begging to remain your sold-out kukkura.

*From your servants at Hastināpura-dhāma, Delhi*

## New Goloka (North Carolina)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

Faith is described by Kṛṣṇadāsa Kaviraja Gosvami in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya-līlā,* 22.62*):*

*śraddhā-śabde—visvāśa kahe sudṛḍha niścaya*  
*kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*

**“By rendering transcendental loving service to Kṛṣṇa, one automatically performs all subsidiary activities. This confident, firm faith, favorable to the discharge of devotional service, is called** ***śraddhā.”***

Faith in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, can be described in much the same way. It is true that simply by following your instructions, everything desirable is accomplished. Faith is different form the simple belief that you are a pure devotee and eternal associate of the Lord, although these are facts. To have faith in you means that one has taken refuge in you, in both your *vapuḥ* and *vāṇī* forms. You have demonstrated to us during the last few years that if we trust your basic instructions for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness—books are the basis, preaching is the essence, utility is the principle, and purity is the force—we will surely be successful. The rapid expansion of the temple here is proof of this fact.

We pray that we will never forget the necessity of simple dependence upon you and your words. Please let us remain forever at your lotus feet.

*Your servants at New Goloka Mandira,*

## New Govardhana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Eternal father, guide, well-wisher, and best friend. Where else can such a loving and merciful personality as yourself be found? You are the embodiment of *gaura-śakti,* the supremely clement preaching potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By your unlimited kindness millions of lost souls are reviving their service to Kṛṣṇa.

When we remember your instructions and try sincerely to please you insuperable predicaments seem insignificant. We then feel content in any circumstance aboard the unsinkable ship of your *vāṇī* with you at the helm.

By executing your will to spread the *yuga-dharma—*by which all activities become purified and perfect—we appreciate your mercy and begin to taste the nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. “This Organization is not run with by-laws and meetings or vouchers, but by following the instructions of the spiritual master with sincerity. Whatever success I have had is due to that. To one with faith and sincerity, by-laws are useless.”

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālam*  
*pañguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*Śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master a lame man can cross mountains, a blind man can see the stars in the sky, and a dumb man can recite eloquent poetry.”**

When we are eager to please you, *impossible* becomes a word in the fool’s dictionary. On the other hand, when out of illusion or pride we forget your orders, the most trifling task appears laborious, complex, and burdensome.

The Vaikuṇṭha breezes of *sādhana-bhakti* refreshingly clear away the foul airs of self-interest from our hearts, reviving our eternal service mood only due to your grace, leaving no desire but to serve you.

You have said that chanting sixteen rounds daily is the most important service. Fools who are no longer following these most simple instructions and can therefore no longer experience your association are saying that we no longer have adequate guidance and must run to others for shelter, like frightened children. By pleasing you we receive guidance, enlightenment, and pleasure beyond expectation or measurement, because you are the unlimited *sad-guru* representing the infinite Godhead. Your storehouse of spiritual treasures will never be insufficient for the sincere soul.

*Sei mora mantra-japa, sei mora dharama karama*

For us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your lotus feet are the conclusion of the religion of the *Vedas* and are the goal of all our vows, austerities, and the chanting of our *mantra.* They are the purpose of all our religious activities. Let us remember constantly that our unifying link, as brothers and sisters, is to serve you. Forgetting this, we will think that we are the most capable, experienced devotees, and from this pretense our “esteemed” opinions will only create enmity, that he is tolerant and sees the good in others.

As you have said, “Our movement can only be destroyed from within”; “Maintain what I have given you”; “Do not fight.” If in a selfish mood of noncooperation we lose what you have won with great endeavor and thus make you like Alexander the Great, will we still cry “It was his fault!” as we go our separate ways?

Let us forget petty differences, forming a united army to “do the needful” and thus accomplish “mission impossible,” being showered by your mercy. You have given these pathetically flapping corpses life and hope, “a life full of grace, fresh like the morning dew.”

We beg humbly to be able to remember your instructions and try to act on them sincerely in all humility, and so remain eternally connected to you. We see this as life’s cherished goal.

*From your servants at New Govardhana, Australia,*

## New Orleans

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, by whose mercy the whole world is now awakening to the realization that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have changed the course of history. The historians have recorded history for the past five hundred years. Throughout the great events of so many civilizations we find that the devotees are conspicuous by their absence. During all of the convulsions of humankind for the past five thousand years, we find the devotees and the knowledge of Lord Kṛṣṇa locked within the boundaries of India.

Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have changed from dark to night. Already they are giving you credit for this. And this is just the beginning of the rising of the sun and moon, Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, on the Western countries.

Now you have centers all over the world, in every major city. From these centers the message of Godhead is broadcast in so many ways to the people in that area. In this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are still spanning the globe on your incredible preaching tour. *Bṛhat-mṛdaṅga-*book distribution.

The conditioned souls all over the planet are reading the millions of books you have distributed and now have facility to surrender to Kṛṣṇa according to their capacities.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness has been firmly planted by you in the major cities of the world, and now we are seeing so many fruits and flowers. Even the greatest tragedies fail to uproot your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. They can bring big, big fire brigades, but it is too late. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness fire has spread too much to be extinguished. Everyone will have to be purified by the all-encompassing sacrificial fire you have ignited. Śrīla Prabhupāda-ki jaya!

## New Talavan, (Carriere, Mississippi)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvati Gosvami. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanya-deva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You are graceful, golden, and swanlike, and you are standing in the bow of the huge golden ship of your ISKCON society.

Out of your causeless mercy, you have appeared on the surface of our sea of dreams. You and your ship seem solid, still, and strong—like the eye of a storm—while all about, the waters of our dream sea are rising high and crashing down with enough force to destroy the three worlds. Into those treacherous waters you put your golden hand, and in a voice strong and deep, like thunder, you speak of a world where there are no tears of sorrow. And though your voice is strong, it is simultaneously gentle and comforting…and as the sound enters our ears, it removes all fear from our hearts. You grasp our weak, trembling hands with the certainty and strength of an angel of God and pull us out of the grips of the waters of death. Speaking of the transcendental, spiritual kingdom of our eternal father and Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you tell us of an eternal, blissful life of service unto the sweet lotus feet of the Supreme Lord and all His wonderful devotees. Comforting us with soft nectar-filled words, you encourage us, “You come home now, my children. It’s time for you to come home!”

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of your efforts, the pathway of our hearts, which was previously overgrown with the sprouts, bushes, and trees of material desire, is now imprinted with Kṛṣṇa’s footprints.

Because of your efforts, our minds, which were previously preoccupied with engaging the senses in nonsense activities, are now concerned with personally keeping Vaiṣṇava principles and preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world for the benefit of others. Though we are weak in ability and wavering in determination, we are trying to please you by our cooperation in pushing forward Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement.

Please forgive our childish immaturities and transgressions, and bless us that we may serve your feet eternally.

*Your humble servants in Talavana,*

## New Vrajamaṇḍala

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

In the *Bhūmi-gita* section of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*Twelfth Canto, we find descriptions of the present age, symptoms we are seeing in some countries, such as:

“Cities will be dominated by thieves, the *Vedas* will be contaminated by speculative interpretations of atheists, political leaders will virtually consume the citizens, and the so-called priests and intellectuals will be devotees of their bellies and genitals. The *brahmacāris* will fail to execute their vows and will become generally unclean, the householders will become beggars, the *vānaprashthas* will live in the villages, and the *sannyāsīs* will become greedy for wealth…. In Kali-yuga men will be wretched and controlled by women. Uncultured men will accept charity on behalf of the Lord and will earn their livelihood by making a show of austerity and wearing a mendicant dress. Those who know nothing about religion will mount a high seat and presume to speak on religious principles. In the age of Kali people’s minds will always be agitated… and will gradually come to appear like ghostly haunted creatures…..”

But then Śukadeva Gosvami mentions one outstanding feature of this age:

*Kaler doṣa-nidhe rājann*  
*Asti hy eko Mahāṇ guṇaḥ*  
*kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya*  
*kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya*  
*mukta-saṅgaḥ paraṁ vrajet*

**“My dear King, although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, there is still one good quality about this age: simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra,*** **one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom.”** ***(Bhāg.*** **12.3.51)**

In the thick darkness of ignorance of these days, you act like a very powerful lighthouse, reflecting the effulgence from the spiritual realm, by which only one flashlight can save so many people without any hope at all.

It seems that by your creating this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, this *śloka* has become true in a so-merciful way. It seems Śukadeva Gosvami could have also said, “My dear King, although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, still there is one good quality about this age: that Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda will give the ISKCON society.”

You did everything for us, even up to caring about the smallest of details. But out of a deep humility you gave all credit to your *Guru* Mahārāja. This genuine quality we see reflected in the following letter, answering your disciple Jāhnavā dāsī, who wrote glorifying you:

“You wrote so many nice things glorifying me but I think that my *Guru* Mahārāja is big. I am not big, he is big. Sometimes in the association of the big one we seem big. Like the sun is light and heat, and by reflecting the greatness of the sunlight, the moon in the middle of the night also looks big. But actually the moon is by nature cold and dark, but in the association of the sun is seen as big; this is the real position. So I am so grateful you appreciate my *Guru* Mahārāja, who wanted to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, he is so big….”

We only pray to be fixed in your service, even if we have to take another birth here. We only desire some pure loving devotional service at your lotus feet, and to receive some of your urge to preach.

Just as we hear that Daśaratha was famous by this name because he was such an expert fighter he could fight on ten chariots at one time, we also saw and heard you preaching in the ten directions, all over the world, against all kinds of bogus philosophies, against impersonalism and nihilism.

*Namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*Nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This leader flashlight many young people are looking for, it is to be found in your personality. Everybody is busy trying to solve some type of problem, but as you said, the real problem is to stop death. In an issue of *Back to Godhead* from 1944 you wrote: “The leaders of nations have ….opened many factories for manufacturing weapons for the art of killing, but none had opened a factory to manufacture weapons protecting man from the cruel hands of death, although our wishful desire is always not to die.” You taught us how to use these powerful weapons such as *japa-mālās, karatālas* and *mṛdaṅgas* to protect us against death and take us back to Godhead.

We find you also exhibited the *kṣatriya* spirit of generosity. We read from a letter to Jadurāṇī dāsī: “The spiritual master accepts the sinful activities of his disciples from the first initiation. Perhaps I gave initiation very easily, but what can I do? I am ready to go to hell for the service of Lord Caitanya…”

Where in the whole world is to be found such a magnanimous personality as Śrīla Prabhupāda?

We beg to remain even in the smallest service until death, and we hope also to see your fame widespread all over the world in our lifetime.

All glories to the all-merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants at New Vrajamaṇḍala, Spain,*

## New York

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Children, alone, blind and wandering aimlessly in the dark cave of illusion.  
Suddenly, peaceful, luminous rays of divine light pervade the atmosphere.  
They give divine vision, thereby relieving the miseries caused by the blazing fire of  
Material existence and illuminating the path that leads to freedom.  
Eternal shelter,  
The blissful chanting and dancing party of the devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu,  
The mercy of moon of Navadvīpa, birth after birth.

Benediction moon’s rays reach far and wide,  
They judge not who’s fit or unqualified,  
Emanate from the holy names of Kṛṣṇa and Hari,  
Sent to the West by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Thākura.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda chants and others become pure.  
Millions of stars can’t equal one moon,  
Who distributes his rays to free our hearts from their gloom.

He brought fruits from the garden of Śrī Gaurahari—  
An empowered ācārya giving mercy so free,  
His message shall free millions of jīvas, no doubt.  
He wrote volumes of books to help lessen the count  
Of Devī-dhāma’s prisoners, distraught and worn out.  
Your instructions are the captain always guiding the ship’s helm  
Across the ocean of nescience to the transcendental realm.

His Divine Grace brought Kṛṣṇa to many a land,  
From New York to L.A., London, Japan,  
Much sweeter than mangoes on a hot afternoon,  
Preaching in Moscow, free the comrades quite soon,  
Turn crows into swans with the dust from his feet,  
No gambling, no sex, no intoxication, no meat.  
His knowledge is so bright it shines like torchlight,  
It dissipates illusion and delivers us from fright.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda is so kind he gave us his time  
To purify our hearts and make us sublime.

He taught us to love Kṛṣṇa, with a leaf, a flower, water, and fruit.  
If we give this message to others, then we water the root  
Of the mercy tree of Gaurāṅga, giving shelter in every land.  
By pleasing guru, we please Kṛṣṇa; that’s how ISKCON expands,  
Shining rays of causeless mercy on every town and village,  
Benefiting every man.  
While lotus of good fortune spreads from the waxing moon;  
It blossoms in the hearts of men for their eternal boon.

Please shelter our souls under your lotus feet;  
They have a cooling effect that gives us relief  
From the blazing fire in which so long we do burn,  
Until this chanting and dancing is all that we yearn.  
Your instructions are clear: we must simply adhere;  
The preacher is most dear to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we hear.  
Books are the basis to guide the whole, wide human race;  
Purity is the force that makes Māyā hide her face;  
Preaching is the essence—it wakes their sleeping ears with blessed grace;  
Utility is the principle that facilitates serving guru,  
Anytime and any place.  
Fulfilling your desires is our goal, birth after birth.

O master at whose feet all other masters sit,  
Our humble thanks and daṇḍavats for all your matchless gifts.  
Returning home to Vṛndāvana, nitya-līlā with Kṛṣṇa for sure,  
He gave us his disciples the legacy and responsibility to be pure.  
Mutual cooperation will show our love for our master divine,  
Spread the rays of the benediction moon, chant the holy name and you’ll find,  
By obeying Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions, then our lives become sublime.

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

With straw between our teeth we offer our most respectful obeisances on this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance day and Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

Because of your sincere service rendered to the lotus feet of your spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktivedanta Sarasvati Thākura, the fulfillment of the desires of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the predecessor *ācārya* are being accomplished.

The benediction moon of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is benefiting the entire world. By illuminating Kali-yuga’s dark horizons with the congregational chanting of the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, your worldwide *saṅkīrtana* mission shines the only light that enables us to see clearly the path back to Godhead.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is stated:

*Keha yadī tāṅrā mukhe śune Kṛṣṇa-nāma*  
*tāṅrā mukhe āna śune tāṅrā mukhe āna*  
*sabe Kṛṣṇa hari bali nāce kānde hāse*  
*paramparāya Vaiṣṇava ha-ila sarva deśe*  
*(Madhya 17.48-49)*

**“When someone heard the chanting of the holy name from the mouth of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and someone else heard this chanting from that second person, and someone again heard this chanting from the third person, everyone in all countries become a Vaiṣṇava through such disciplic succession. Thus everyone chanted the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and Hari, and they danced, cried, and smiled.”**

In 1966, under a tree in Tompkins Square Park, Your Divine Grace chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa *Mahā-mantra.* Today, more than twenty years later, the world continues to receive

the benefit form your pure chanting. Your Divine Grace also performed *saṅkīrtana* in almost every major city in the world. Then your disciples, purified by hearing your transcendental vibration, continued spreading the mercy of the Pañca-tattva, with the expectation of eventually reaching to every town and village in the world.

Now, due to the benefit of your pure chanting of the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, hundreds of temples have been opened, millions upon millions of transcendental literatures have been distributed, and multitudes of conditioned souls are being liberated form the blazing fire of material existence. The reason for this is so nicely expressed in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:* “The true *ācārya* presents Kṛṣṇa to everyone by preaching the holy name of the Lord throughout the world. Thus the conditioned souls, purified by chanting the holy name, are liberated from the blazing fire of material existence. In this way, spiritual benefit grows increasingly full, like the waxing moon in the sky.” (*Anya* 7.12, purport)

Through your books we are given the mercy of your purifying association. This is your kindness upon us, and although you have returned to join the eternal chanting and dancing party of Lord Gaurāṅga, simultaneously we are given your association through the teachings in your books, purifying and guiding us straight on the path back to Godhead. With sweet words we can express our respect, gratitude, and glorification on your divine appearance day. But our real offering of homage will be revealed in how sincerely we obey all of your instructions and work together to become your humble instruments, favorably assisting in your mission of fulfilling the desires of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Now please hear our prayer, O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, O most venerable Vaiṣṇava Thākura. Please be kind upon us. Please shine the merciful rays of Lord Gaurāṅga’s benediction moon upon us. Thus we may engage in your pure devotional service, birth after birth.

(Written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

*Begging to remain your servants forever,*

*Your fallen, insignificant children from the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira,*

## Nigeria

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We offer our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. The whole world outside of India was completely covered by great illusion. All the living entities of the world, having lost realization of themselves and being enchanted by *māyā,* were very busily engaged in all activities of sense gratification. Thus they were subjected to the happiness and distress of this world—both being miserable. But none of them could understand their condition. Only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, could see with your transcendental vision the suffering of all humanity which is occasioned by a lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In spite of their advanced technology, material wealth, mundane scholarship, etc., you knew that the people was miserable. Therefore you ventured outside of India to save the world.

Undoubtedly there are many devotees in India who are very advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They must have seen the suffering of humanity also. But none of them came outside of India to help. Only you did, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and at such great risk to yourself. Therefore you have surprised everyone else in carrying out the orders of the spiritual master, in sacrificing everything to Kṛṣṇa, and in having compassion on the conditioned souls.

You brought Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the most fallen people, but you did not change anything. You created devotees in the outside world who were as good as those in India. You generated so much love in young hearts. Everyone was attracted to you and eager to please you. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, how did you bring about such a miracle? You have answered this question already: it is because you have presented Kṛṣṇa consciousness exactly as it has come down in the disciplic succession. Thus you have clearly demonstrated the power of purity. And today, though you have returned back to Kṛṣṇa, that power of yours still lives on in this world in the shape of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, which is being continued by your disciples and granddisciples.

There have been many *ācāryas,* and they have done great things in the world. But what you have done, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is unique. You stand out amongst the *ācāryas.* Your transcendental position cannot be easily understood by us. Therefore, we beg to remain the menial servants of your servants, and we offer our most respectful obeisances unto you.

*Your servants in Nigeria Yatra,*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Bhakti Tirtha Swami

## North Sidney

**A day in the life of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda**

(excerpted from ‘A TRANSCENDENTAL DIARY—the life and times of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda’)  
(abridged from Volume One, November 25, 1975—January 8, 1976 by Hari-śauri dāsa )

**December 5th1975**

Vṛndāvana

It is hard to say when Prabhupāda’s day begins and when it ends, because he never seems to actually terminate his activities in the way that we do. He only rests for a few hours per day, and even that is intermittent. He maintains a very regular general schedule.

6:00 A.M. wash, brush teeth, etc. Take medicine of *yogendra-dāsa,* honey and *baḍa* cardamom seed mixed by mortar and pestle.  
6:30 - 7:30 A.M. morning walk  
7:30 - 8:30 A.M. greet the Deities, *guru-puja,* then *Śrīmad-Bhāgvatam* lecture on Prahlada Mahārāja from Seventh Canto  
9:00 - 9:30 A.M. breakfast of fruits and *cīrā,* etc.  
9.45 - 11:15 A.M. rest on roof for an hour and then meet people (usually by appointment)  
11:15 - 1:15 P.M. massage with oil  
1:15 - 1:45 P.M. bathe  
1:45 - 2:30 P.M. lunch *prasadam*  
2:30 - 3:00 P.M. sit in room and chant *japa*  
3:00 - 4:00 P.M. rest  
4:00 - 5:00 P.M. meeting with specific people or devotees, or chant  
5:00 - 6:30 P.M. give *darśana* for people in general  
6:30 - 9:30 P.M. meet people, or senior devotees; GBC business or just chant  
9:30 P.M. take hot milk and rest; massage  
12 - 1:00 A.M. rise and translate  
5:00 A.M. light rest or chant *japa*

Today was a typical example. After his all-night translation work, he stopped at about *maṅgala ārati* time or just after. He lay back against the bolsters with his feet up and lightly snoozed for a short while; otherwise he sometimes calls in one of the temple managers for discussion.

At six o’clock he went into the bathroom to wash, brush his teeth, and freshen up. Then he came back and sat for a few minutes to put tilaka on and take his medicine. This is a mixture of honey, a small reddish pellet of an Āyurvedic medicine called *yogendra-rasa,* and one large, roasted cardamom seed, all crushed and mixed together in a small oval-shaped mortar. After adding a little water he drank the mix straight form the mortar, scraping up the residue with the pestle and depositing it on his tongue with an elegant twist of his fingers.

Then he got ready for his walk. This is something of an art for the servants. As he got up from his desk he stood patiently while I helped him on with his heavy saffron-colored coat and woolen hat, and hung his bead bag around his neck. He was, and generally is, already in conversation with Haṁsadūta, Akṣayānanda Swami, and Gopala Kṛṣṇa while this was going on. This completed, he moved out, and I rushed over to the door, placed his cane directly into his hand, positioned his shoes so that he stepped into them and out of his slippers in one easy movement, and at the same time half the door open; all without delaying or interrupting his steady progress outside. The devotees were all waiting and shouted “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!” as he appeared, offering obeisances and a garland, and he returned the greeting with “Jaya! Hare Kṛṣṇa!” The privileged few for the day’s walk gathered close as he made his way up the side of the temple and out the front gate onto Chattikara Road.

The walk itself was energetic: Prabhupāda shows surprising strength, and by the end of it we were the ones struggling to keep up. He walked for exactly an hour, heading west, out beyond the boundary of Vṛndāvana village into the countryside for half an hour, and then back.

At precisely 7:30 a.m., he entered the temple from the side door, slipping out of his shoes and waiting patiently as the *pūjārīs* strained to swing back the very large, thick wooden doors on each of the three alters. The conchshells trumpted their call to the faithful, announcing the imminent appearance of the deities, and as soon as the curtains drew back, the Govinda prayers boomed over the loud-speaker. Śrīla Prabhupāda, followed by all the devotees, offered his prostrated obeisances: first to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and his *Guru* Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati; finally to the brilliant forms of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Cyāmasundara.

After taking a little *caraṇāmṛta,* the Deities’ bathing water, Prabhupāda traversed back across the black and white chequered marble floor and mounted the steps to his carved marble *vyāsāsana.* As he sat, flanked on either side by two lions, the devotees offered him *guru-puja,* chanting and prayers. “*Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma…,”* each one coming forward to offer a flower at his lotus feet and prostrating themselves before him. Everyone enjoys the *guru-puja* and the chance to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda especially in person. It is a daily act of humble submission, an affirmation of our full commitment to his service, and a constant reminder to our flickering minds that without this, we are nothing.

As the *kirtana* ended, Harikeśa moved forward to swing the microphone round in front of his mouth, and Prabhupāda’s voice rang out over the loudspeakers, *“Jaya oṁ viṣṇupāda-paraMahāṁsa-parivrājakācārya aṣṭottatra-śata śrī-śrīmad-bhaktisiddhanta-sarasvatī-gosvāmī-mahārāja-Prabhupāda-kī jaya!”* and the devotees bowed their heads to the ground in obeisance to the disciplic succession, the Pañca-tattva, the holy *dhāmas,* the Vaiṣṇava, and the assembled devotees. Then Harikeśa passed Prabhupāda his *karatālas,* and we sit down to listen and respond to Prabhupāda’s sweet and melodious voice as he glorified Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava:

*(jaya) Rādhā-madhava (jaya) kunja-bihari*  
*(jaya) gopi-jana-vallabha (jaya) giri-vara-dhari*  
*(jaya) jasoda-nandana, (jaye) braja-jana-ranjana,*  
*(jaya) jamuna-tira-vana-cari*

Whenever he sings this *bhajana,* he closes his eyes in concentration, his face showing the intensity of his meditation on the objects of his love and worship in the groves of Vṛndāvana on the banks of the Yamuna River. His *karatālas* rang out, quickening the pace, and the devotees voices swelled in response. His voice injects new meaning and freshness every time he sings it, although he does it every day before class. Just as it came to a heart-filling crescendo, the *karatālas* gave their final three rings,*dung-dung-dung,*and everyone knelt with their heads to the floor as Prabhupāda recited the *prema-dhvani* again.

Once more Harikeśa jumped up, removed the *karatālas,* handed Śrīla Prabhupāda the *Bhāgavatam* already opened at the correct page, and quickly hung another small microphone around his neck, the other end of which he plugged into a large reel-to-reel Uher tape recorder that he had been lugging around since the beginning of the morning walk. The *Bhāgavatam* was not a BBT publication but an Indian edition, containing the commentaries of different *ācāryas* which Prabhupāda uses for his translation work. Finally Harikeśa carefully slipped Prabhupāda’s spectacles onto him. Then he sat down to lead the chanting of the Sanskrit verse, loudly reciting the translation before Śrīla Prabhupāda himself began his lecture.

Harikeśa: “Translation: Prahlada Mahārāja continued to speak: ‘My dear friends born of demoniac families, the happiness which is perceived with reference to the senses can be obtained in any form of life according to one’s past fruitive activities. Such happiness is automatically obtained as sometimes we obtain distresses without any endeavor.’”

Harikeśa was also wearing a neck microphone, and both his and Prabhupāda’s were plugged into a small box with a two-way switch. He recorded himself and then threw the switch.

Prabhupāda: *sukham aindriyakaṁ daityā deha-yogena dehinaṁ / sarvatra labhyate daivād yathā duḥkham ayantnataḥ.*

Sometimes speaking with his eyes closed in complete concentration, and sometimes with them open, surveying his audience, he propounded the ancient philosophy of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the modern context. Although he quoted other Sanskrit verses profusely, cross-referencing each point with other works, such as the *Bhagavad-gītā* and the *Purāṇas* or the *Upaniṣads,* his meaning was clear and direct. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s art and potency as a speaker and preacher is clear and direct. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s art and potency as a speaker and preacher is his ability to present the most esoteric subject matter in a way which is practical and easy to understand and apply, yet without sacrificing the depth and sublimity of it. He has grasped the very essence of life, its meaning and purpose, and he is able to present it for the understanding of both the common man and the intellectual. He has previously said that if one is patient, then there must be effect. He is so potent he has spawned tens of thousands of children in every country of the world within the womb of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and he is more than adequately maintaining them.

After half an hour, class was over, and the devotees shouted, “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Śrīla Prabhupāda-ki jaya!” and once again Harikeśa sprang into action, deftly removing Prabhupāda’s spectacles, the *Bhāgavatam,* and the microphone from his neck, and handing him his cane; all while Prabhupāda stepped down from the *vyāsāsana* to go out the door. At the top of the steps leading out onto the path I waited with his shoes and, slipping into them, he walked the hundred yards back to his rooms while the devotees followed, dancing and chanting, “Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda!”

As soon as he got inside, he propped his cane in the corner next to the door in his sitting room, slipped out of his outdoor shoes and into his slippers (Prabhupāda never walks barefoot, even inside), allowed the removal of his coat and hat, and sat down for a few minutes until his breakfast was served. Whatever flower garlands he had received, he had us hang onto the various paintings and photos which adorn the walls. These will be left until they dry out, and then they will be removed (he has several times complained that the cleaners unnecessarily remove them when they are fresh).

His breakfast was served upon the low, marble-topped *chowki* in the *prasadam* room. It was prepared by Kiśorī dāsī and other ladies and consisted of cut fruits such as seedless grapes, guava, banana, orange, pomegranate, and whatever else was fresh at the market. Along with this he had a small bowl of fried *cīrā* with peas, another of fried cashew nuts, and he topped off the meal with a small piece of *sandeśa.* He began with a little ginger soaked in lemon juice in order to stimulate his digestion. If this is missing he won’t start until it is brought. He ate very little, and very slowly. It was an act of devotion, *prasāda-sevā,* rather than tongue-*sevā.*

On his *chowkī* he also had a small hand-bell in case he wanted anything else, a silver tumbler full of water, and a packet of tooth picks. I was surprised to see, while clearing his plate and wiping the table down, that his teeth actually move apart when he inserts the wooden pick, but Prabhupāda just laughed about it.

When he finished he took a little *bhāsker-lavaṇ,* an Ayurvedic digestive powder, holding out his open palm for me to tip it into. Tilting his head back aristocratically with his mouth open, he threw in the powder and, maintaining the posture, poured in some water from the silver tumbler touching to his lips. After washing his mouth out in the bathroom, he took rest upstairs for about an hour, on a mattress in the sun.

Sometimes, before resting, he sits in the *darśana* room and chats with us for a while, commenting on the state of the world in the present day. These moments are especially nectarine: to be able to sit, with Prabhupāda relaxed and causal, bathing in the warmth of his intimate association. This morning was particularly memorable.

The sun was up and shining brightly through the tall, narrow windows, casting dazzling patches of light on the clean white sheets on the floor. He walked over and sat comfortably down in the middle, legs crossed, right ankle resting on the left knee, fingers loosely intertwined, his eyes closed for a short while, enjoying the warm rays of the sun as they danced upon his golden-hued form. Seizing the opportunity, Haṁsadūta and I came and sat around, on either side, just happy to sit and be with him in this quiet moment.

He began to speak a little, reflecting on the unfortunate state of the world’s inhabitants. Because they have no knowledge of the Supreme Lord, he said, people are suffering. Under the false impression of being independent, they commit all kinds of sinful acts, not knowing and not caring for the results, foolishly thinking they are free to do as they like. And when the volume of sinful life gets too great, they have to suffer the consequences in the form of pestilence or war. They think that by politics and meetings they can avoid such things, but it is not possible. They are helpless to prevent it. This punishment is *abhibautic,* sufferings caused by other living beings. At the right time, nature brings the demons together and engages them to fight, and to illustrate the point he gave an amusing but striking example of how Māyā is working

“In my young days, we had one teacher. Whenever there was any misbehavior between the boys, the teacher would stop them and bring them out to the front of the class. He would make them stand face to face and each take hold of the ears of the other and on his order he would make them pull. So the one, he is pulling, and the other, he is hurting so he pulls back even harder, and each one is pulling and crying, but they cannot let go because the teacher is ordering, ‘No, you cannot stop. You must go on pulling!’ Similarly, Māyā brings together one Churchill and one Hitler, ‘Now rascal-pull!’ and neither can stop, and the foolish people glorify them.”

The thought of the comical scene so humored him that even before he finished he was already laughing, and he sat, shoulders and belly shaking, teeth flashing brilliant and white like pearls, resplendent in the sunlight. When Prabhupāda smiles it seems to light up the whole room, or even the whole universe. It is a Vaikuṇṭha smile, spreading a splendid spiritual effulgence everywhere around him. He was so open and congenial that is seemed, just for a moment, as if we were sitting in on a picnic with Kṛṣṇa and His cowherd boyfriends, joking and laughing in the forests of Goloka. Śrīla Prabhupāda must have many such friends with whom he can eternally enjoy happy and carefree days, yet he is simultaneously here with all of us. It was such a captivating moment that Haṁsaduṭa and I just looked at each other, laughing along but with a wonder at who this personality, Śrīla Prabhupāda, really is; and feeling very fortunate to share in a special intimacy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is extraordinary in that, exalted as he clearly is, and foolish and neophyte as we were, he seems to like nothing better than to be with his disciples. He gives the impression that there is no-one in the world he would rather be with, nor is there anything he would rather be doing than sharing whatever he has with us, although we have nothing to give in return that could possibly be of interest to him. It is a very one-sided relationship, but Prabhupāda doesn’t mind. He is not looking for anything for himself. He is only looking to see what he can give us. And just by giving himself we have gotten more than any of us could ever have hoped for.

From ten till eleven-fifteen, any special guests may come, and if there are none, although there is almost always someone, he may do his mail, or with the senior managers go over the running of the temple. There was plenty to discuss and many letters to write, and he dealt with a wide variety of topics and personalities. He is almost daily meeting with various gentlemen, negotiating the offer of a *gośālā* near Mathurā, the opening of a post office in our future *gurukula* building, and the establishment of a bank branch in a guesthouse. All these mean better facilities for devotees and guests, which will result in the temple becoming a greater focus of local community activity, and when more people come, and more preaching can go on, Kṛṣṇa consciousness will further increase, and more souls will be saved from the clutches of material existence.

By his letter-writing, which he rarely misses, he can be orchestrating the world wide production and distribution of his books through the efforts of his enthusiastic followers; solving the personal problems of a disciple struggling with *māyā;* or encouraging the newly interested, from a university teacher in Copenhagen to a distressed young man in Australia. All of them receive his close personal guidance and attention.

No matter what other business is to be done, Śrīla Prabhupāda never neglects his own schedule. He is completely regulated and steady. At eleven-thirty he had his massage, followed by a bath and lunch and again about an hour’s rest. I’ve never seen anyone sleep so little as Śrīla Prabhupāda. His resting hours total about three at the most, but he never shows any sign of fatigue at all.

When he got up around four o’clock, Kiśorī had a garland to place around his neck, some freshly ground sandalwood paste to dab on his forehead and temples, and some fresh fruit juice to stimulate his palate, as he sat at his desk to receive more visitors.

At five the doors opened for general *darśana,* and a steady flow of people entered—at least fifty or sixty of the curious and respectful continually packed his room to either sit and watch or ask questions. He talked sometimes specifically with a particular visitor, not minding if the other fifty listened in, and at other times generally to all of them. I was posted at the door to give out *perā,* a local *prasādam,* to all his visitors. Prabhupāda is particularly insistent on this. All visitors must at least receive some *Kṛṣṇa-prasadam,* because that is something tangible for their spiritual advancement. A discussion of philosophy they may go away from and forget, but the *prasadam* will act to purify. Apart from this, it is the Vedic etiquette that any guest be offered a place to sit and a little refreshment, no matter who they are, and Śrīla Prabhupāda is the perfect host.

At six-thirty the sound of the conchshells and the ringing of bells announced evening *ārati,* so *darśana* finished, and he sent the devotees and guests over to the temple to chant and see the Deities. He relaxed for a while, spending the rest of the evening discussing philosophy and matters of practical management, giving advice, and sometimes just sitting quietly chanting or reading.

Bhagatjī came at about nine to make his hot milk and for light discussion on the temple management, *gurukula,* and other things. Prabhupāda has a glass of hot milk every evening just before taking rest, sometimes supplementing it with a savoury like *kachaurī, parātha,*or fried *cīra,* carefully instructing the cooks on exactly how to make them. His milk has to be exactly the right temperature for drinking. Calling for another bowl, he poured the milk from a height of about six inches from the glass into the bowl and then back again. By doing this a few times it aerated the milk and reduced the temperature, and when it was just right, he drank it.

Evening massage is generally taking about half an hour now, or even longer, so I did not rest until about eleven. It is quite cold throughout the night until the sun comes up, so Prabhupāda’s circulation and joints need more attention.

Finally, as we all slept, he got up around eleven-thirty to twelve midnight to begin his most important work of the day, the translation of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and the writing of his transcendental purports. He chanted his *japa* for an hour or so, in complete concentration on the holy names, praying to Kṛṣṇa for the ability to serve Him nicely and to present the eternal words of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in a manner just suitable for the understanding of the entire world, conscious that his work will form the basis for law and order for the next ten thousand years. Putting aside his *japa-mala,* he donned his spectacles and clicked on the desk light, opening up the *Bhāgavatams* at the book marks, the green Varanasi edition with the Sanskrit commentaries of previous *ācāryas* and the red Bengali one. He leaned forward and studied them intently. Then, keeping the microphone close to his mouth, his thumb flicked the tape into life, and he began his dictation: “*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Seventh Canto, Seventh Chapter, verse twenty five, purport continued….”

*Humbly submitted for the pleasure of the devotees on behalf of ISKCON North Sydney and the Sydney farm,*

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Bhojadeva dāsa / Dāneśvara dāsa / Gurūttama dāsa / Hemāṅga dāsa / Mahāprabhu dāsa / Maṇḍana dāsa / Priyavrata dāsa / Sthira dāsa / Vegavān dāsa / Vimuktātmā dāsa / Vyāpī dāsa / Yakṣa dāsa / Devakī-devī dāsī / Gopamāta-devī dāsī / Guṇātītā-devī dāsī / Hemāṅga dāsa / Mahāprabhu dāsa / Maṇḍana dāsa / Priyavrata dāsa / Sthira dāsa / Vegavān dāsa / Vimuktātmā dāsa / Vyāpī dāsa / Yakṣa dāsa / Devakī-devī dāsī / Gopamātā-devī dāsī / Guṇātītā-devī dāsī / Jāhnavāmātā-devī dāsī / Jitā-devī dāsī / Kāruṇikādevī dāsī / Kriyā-devī dāsī / Peśalā-devī dāsī / Puṇyā-devī dāsī / Rāsa-maṇḍala-devī dāsī / Satyā-devī dāsī / Siddhi-devī dāsī / Śrimatī-devī dāsī / Yogeśa-devī dāsī / Bhakta Dave / Bhakta Jason / Bhakta Ludovic / Bhakta Mathew / Bhakta Peter / Bhakta Phillipe / Bhakta Rowland / Bhakta Steven / Bhakta Glenyce / Bhaktin Julie / Bhaktin Kellie / Bhaktin Sal

## Patna

*oṁ ajñana-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlītaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this, the ninety-third anniversary of your all-auspicious appearance in this mortal world.

Twelve years ago I saw you at the Yoga Pitha birthplace of Lord Caitanya in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur, then, after, I met you in Calcutta, Māyāpur, and different places. In you I found all pure Vaiṣṇava qualities: your way of wearing *sannyāsī* clothes; Vaiṣṇava style of simple appearance; and your manner of talking and preaching. These qualities are really available only in one single *guru* like you.

*Kṛṣṇa-bhakta—niṣkāma attaeva ‘śānta*  
*Bhukti-mukti-siddhi-kāmī-sakali aśānta*

**“Because a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa is desireless, he is peaceful. Fruitive workers desire material enjoyment,** ***jñānīs*** **desire liberation, and** ***yogis*** **want** ***yoga-siddhis,*** **the** ***yoga*** **powers. All these people are demanding various things like sense gratification, liberation, and mystic powers. Only the** ***Kṛṣṇa-bhakta*** **like you makes no demands. The devotee says, ‘Dear Lord, I am your eternal servitor. I surrender unto you. Now do whatever you like with me.’”**

By following you as a bona fide spiritual master (Śanta-Vaiṣṇava) and well-wisher of all living entities, we can also become ś*anta,* peaceful.

Now, by your mercy, we are here in Bihar, the state in which Lord Caitanya was first inspired to dance and chant at Gaya by his spiritual master Śrī Īśvara Puri, and where Kṛṣṇa helped Bhīma to kill Jarāsandha at Rajgir. We have a good field for preaching here, and our sincere desire to make everyone in this Bihar land Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Thank you once again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We can’t even imagine the great mercy of having such a great father as you.

(Written by Dāmodara Svarūpa dāsa )

*Your fallen servants at Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi Mandira,*

## Perth

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

All glories to your ISKCON,  
The intense desire of one becomes the  
Shelter of so many.

Your desire is for devotees,  
Sparkling, invigorated, happy souls, dedicated  
To a preaching mission.

Your desire is for books,  
Thousands and millions and billions of beats  
On the bṛhat-mṛdaṅga drum.

Your desire is for prasādam,  
The filthy hearts and rumbling “tums” of  
One and all sumptuously inundated.

Your desire is for a Kṛṣṇa conscious world,  
Impersonal atheism eradicated at  
The very roots.

Within our little sphere, we endeavor to fulfill.  
With sincere commitment we will always toil on,  
Though results are often humble.

Still let us not forget,  
Your desire is for ISKCON,  
We are ISKCON,  
All glories to your ISKCON!

Your constant love and affection is our  
Sure and steady shelter.  
*(Written by Śrī Rama dāsa )*

*Your servants in Perth, Australia,*

## Philadelphia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

There is really no other purpose to the disciple’s life other than to honor the spiritual master. If I can properly glorify you with these humble words, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then perhaps the Vaiṣṇava will bless me. With the blessings of the Vaiṣṇava, there is hope to show you *genuine* honor and *real* glorification, by actually following your instructions: make my life Kṛṣṇa conscious, preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and help others to do the same.

Therefore I pray to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śarat-vihārī, Jagannātha, Balarāma, and Subhadrā for the inspiration to say something suitable in praise of you. I pray to my Godbrothers and Godsisters not to think me impertinent to speak up amongst them, who are all more qualified to speak on your glories. And I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the perfect representative of the entire disciplic succession, to always shelter me at your lotus feet.

(*Darśana* with you, in the early 1970’s New York. Present: A teacher from Columbia University, some reporters, and a few of your disciples.)

A reporter asked you if you come to be the savior of the youth of America. You replied (more or less), “It is not future. It is already done. Formerly they were known as hippies; now they are all bright faced.” Then you gestured toward us. “Just see my students. See their bright faces.” We sat up straight and lit up brightly for you. We were proud that you were proud of us. You had made us into Vaiṣṇava!

Looking back now, how embarrassing it is! We thought we were Vaiṣṇava. But being a Vaiṣṇava is not so cheap.

Sometimes proud, arrogant, full of conceit, angry, harsh, and ignorant, we masquerade as devotees of the Lord.

Sometimes we hardly know what is to be done or not done. Neither clean nor knowing either proper behavior or truth, and sometimes falling victim to sense gratification, we attempt to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Sometimes by our desires we have become absorbed in lust and anger. Sometimes we receive money by illegal means. We are self-complacent and impudent, deluded by wealth and false prestige; we’ve proudly performed sacrifice in name only, without following any rules or regulations.

Bewildered by false ego, strength, pride, lust and anger, we have become envious of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

And yet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you saw us—your fallen disciples—as pure devotees of the Lord.

*Api cet su-durācāro*  
*Bhajate mām ananya-bhāk*  
*sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ*  
*samyag vyavaśito hi saḥ*  
*(Bg. 9.30)*

We were chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. You had sown the seeds of *prema-bhakti:*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare.

You gave us the holy name, temples, *prasadam,* deities, association of devotees, Tulasī-devī, and your books. In time we would become fully Kṛṣṇa conscious. Then we would fulfill our true destiny to be Vaiṣṇava.

No one else would have believed it possible; no other saffron-robed saint from India. Even today, do we think it is possible? But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, know it will happen—know it had already been done—because you sowed the seeds of *prema-bhakti.* We are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa is remarkable. Your faith is full and firm. You see Kṛṣṇa dancing dapperly on our tongues. You see Kṛṣṇa sweetly, sweetly smiling on our tongues. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you smell the fragrance of the sandalwood and *tulasīs* on the lotus feet of the holy name of the Lord.

Time for us—your humble sons and daughters and grandchildren—to “live and let live” in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Time for us to “forgive and forget” in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Time for us to move toward the perfect harmony of surrender to the pure chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. (The elusive *cooperation!*) Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa without offense.

It is done. You have kindly come and liberated the entire world.

Thank you, Jagat-*guru*, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your humble servant, on behalf of ISKCON Philadelphia, (written by Saudāmanī-devī dāsī)*

## Phillippine Islands

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. We offer our prostrated *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet, fully aware of our insignificant and completely in gratitude for your mercy, which you have continuously been bestowing upon us, your most unworthy servants in the Phillippine Islands, and to everybody else all over the world.

We take this opportunity, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this occasion more than at any other time, to express our most sincere appreciation for your continued instructions as we find them from your books; for your disciples, who have untiringly been pushing forward your goals to reach more and more fallen souls such as us; for ISKCON, which is your growing institution that will really make this world of united nations; for you, who have unconditionally worked so hard to give us Kṛṣṇa; and for Kṛṣṇa, who has given you to us.

On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā, we are feeling at a loss to narrate your glories, which are ever increasing. Our inadequacy does not, however, belittle your stature as the Lord’s manifestation of His *patita-pāvana* nature. Śrīla Prabhupāda, if it is within our ability to make an offering for your pleasure, let it be our wish to always be surrendered to you and your representatives, that we can be used always to assist you in your continuing mission of serving Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Please accept us to become your servants today, on your Vyāsa-pūjā, and always.

*Your insignificant servants in the Philippine Islands,*

## Pisa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Śrī *Guru* and Gaurāṅga!

This year, while reading your books, listening to your recorded classes, discussing along with your devotees, so many times I deeply appreciated Your Divine Grace for the immense treasure you bestowed upon the human race and for how much you benefited me personally by your personal life’s example.

I am taking this auspicious occasion, on this holy day, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, to meditate upon you with reverence and gratefulness.

I would thank you for myself, for my family, for the devotees, and for all mankind, Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are so great, you are still inspiring us, even being physically far away, in the spiritual world.

In our small family temple, Your Divine Grace and Śrīmati Tulasi Devī are both tangible realities living with us. Your staunch, powerful, and holy association makes us spiritually strong and able to overcome this material world by worshiping the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord.

Every day, during *brāhma-muhūrta* time, by performing *maṅgalāratrikā,* chanting, reading your books, and worshiping you, we are simply trying to practice part of what you patiently and constantly taught us. We are just trying to repeat diligently, humbly, without speculations, your precious teachings, or at least, the portion we realized of them. By your mercy, even a few drops of such teachings are so powerful to keep us ever fresh and spiritually alive.

Only after having met you was my life deeply transformed. Just after having listened to you, having accepted and followed your instructions, I suddenly felt fully satisfied and well-situated for the present and the future.

Although I am still living in my home, along with my family, working as a professional designer, these twelve years since I first met Your Divine Grace have been filled with nectarean spiritual experiences. I thank you for the fortunate association of your wonderful devotees, for by your mercy only could I be accepted by them, who are so nicely and faithfully serving you. Although I am unworthy, insignificant, and selfish-minded, nevertheless I received immeasurable spiritual gifts just because I simply tried to execute your orders.

I feel deep gratitude for Your Divine Grace. I know I am living a portion of this life in not serving you as much as I would like to do, but I hope the time will come when I shall serve you as Your Divine Grace deserves.

I intensely pray to you, please, to continue to protect me, inspiring me in the future as you always did in the past in how to serve the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa through Your Divine Grace and your devotees.

Thanking you again and again, I pray you to accept my incomplete and insignificant service unto your lotus feet.

*Your servant,*  
Matsyāvatāra dāsa

## Poland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

You revealed the most simple and most wonderful truth to the people lost in false speculation. You convinced them that what is most simple-serving Kṛṣṇa with heart and soul-is the most wonderful thing.

It is due to your great desire and spiritual strength that people can achieve understanding. Without your help, it would not be possible. The task you have accomplished is the most wonderful gift for mankind. Thanks to you we can experience what it means to be happy and give happiness to others. When we see how these morose and unhappy people change when they chant and dance with us in ecstasy, we become very thankful to you.

We are grateful for your wonderful books. They are so beautiful that sometimes people who have never seen such beautiful books ask if these are really books! Sometimes they want your books so much, but, not having money, they offer in exchange other things that they possess.

More and more your teachings become the only hope for sincere young people. We experience more and more the truth of your prediction that the country we are living in is the most important place for speaking Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this part of the world. It is only because we have so many of your instructions that we are successful.

From day to day your glories spread among the people of our country. People like to take your beautifully framed picture form us and hang it in their rooms with great respect.

It is wonderful how, although you have departed physically from this planet, your words still spread.

We all like to better appreciate your greatness, and then we would be able to more powerfully spread your glories.

*Your worthless granddisciples from the Polish Yatra,*

## Port Harcourt, Nigeria

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Kindly accept these few drops of worthless offerings at your lotus feet. Barely a decade has passed since you left the planet, and the movement you single-handedly and painstakingly founded is dāsīntegrating. The wide firelike manner by which the movement was spreading when you were present has been greatly checked. For the success of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said that four basic pillars have to be observed as follows:

Books— which are the basis  
Preaching— the essence  
Utility— the principle  
Purity— the force.

It is therefore an open secret to every one that the movement is no more evolving on these four pillars. Book distribution has fallen down by more than fifty percent, and the spirit by which devotees used to preach relentlessly has been given up. Purity is no more the basic pursuit of the movement, as we decided to put forward the interest of our Godbrothers before the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The question of utility does not come in, as book distribution has fallen and preaching has greatly suffered a setback. Purity being reduced, fewer people are being convinced to understand *yukta-vairāgya ucyate.* Proper renunciation is to use everything one has in the service of Kṛṣṇa.

Our deviation could not have but displeased you; hence, so many difficulties in our preaching and spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, can we accept the basic fact that most of us were neophyte before you left the planet, but were trying to render a superficial service just to please you, and it is now given up because you are not physically present and there is not sufficient faith to realize that you are still much around? And now we are still neophyte because we are deviating from the four basic pillars that firmly sustained the movement. As you are all-pervading, we need your assistance in this regard so that we can carry out your mission, which is nondifferent from Lord Caitanya’s mission. The Lord says:

*pṛthvite āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe more nāma.*

**“In every town and village the chanting of My name will be heard.”**

*The most fallen servant of your servants,*  
Mahāmanda dāsa

## Prabhupāda Saṅkīrtana Center of New York

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You were so merciful to introduce *prasadam* to the Western world: *karma-*free food for the soul. Not only is it nutritious and delicious, but it satisfies my senses and protects me from *māyā;* and when I taste it I become enthused to spread the glories of Govinda. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our ever well-wisher.

*Your eternal servants at the Prabhupāda Saṅkīrtana Center of New York,*

Kapīndra dāsa / Caitanya-priya dāsa / Bhakta Rashid / Bhakta Chris / Bhaktin Lisa / Bhaktin Nisa

## Puerto Rico

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our fallen obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

When we thought about preparing this offering, we offensively considered it as another “task” that we had to fit into our busy day. We were thinking about how we could get it done by the deadline, so that Puerto Rico could be somehow represented in your Vyāsa-pūjā book. We are sorry to admit to such an offensive mentality. Nevertheless, now, as we meditate on your most merciful presence, we feel your association. Our distraction is gone, and we feel blessed. As the Christians like to say, “Knock and the door shall open.” So just by approaching you we sense that you are certainly here, ready to reciprocate by listening to our stunned words of devotion to you.

By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa, one finds a genuine spiritual master, and by the mercy of *guru,* one gets Kṛṣṇa. Thus the seeming contradiction is resolved of how such degraded persons as ourselves can have anything to do with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the Almighty, the supreme pure, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You are so exalted that Kṛṣṇa Himself always remains interested in you and attracted to sport with you in loving pastimes. And yet you are so infinitely kind as to tolerate our presence, to take charge of us, to give us shelter, and thus act as *patita-pāvana,* the savior of the most fallen. You are the perfect personality, the transparent via medium who bridges the gap between Kṛṣṇa and His grossly covered expanded entities who simply wallow in Kali’s muck. You give us the great chance to emerge.

Sometimes we make vows to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just to accelerate the course of hearing and chanting, just to promote the *bhakti* cure. If we could just become truly serious, if we could just give up our cheating, then perhaps the lusty filth in our hearts would be eradicated once and for all. This mind is still so ornery. Please impel us to take shelter of you and Him and in that way cross forever beyond the ocean of nescience. Let us dedicate our thoughts to serving your lotus feet. Let us harness this bodily machine to serve your supremely merciful mission.

Here, in this land of practically uncontrollable *tulasī* growth, here, on top of this mountain from where Kṛṣṇa’s gorgeous “picture” is beheld with every casual glance, here, under the merciful presence of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa Balarama, here, within the superb sweetness of fragrant Night Queen and between transcendental cries of Śrī Peacock, here, in natural wonder, we nevertheless forget the person who has clearly pointed out:

*Atha vā bahunaitena*  
*Kiṁ jñātena tavārjuna*  
*Viṣṭabhyāham idaṁ kṛtsnam*  
*Ekāṁśena sthito jagat*

**But what need is there, O Arjuna, for all this detailed knowledge? With but a single fragment of Myself, I pervade and support this entire universe.”**

But forgetting the glories of the Omnipresent, all-pervading Lord, we become caught up in flickering desires and hates. We remain inept in inspiring people because, unlike Your Divine Grace, we are far from transparent. Thus our *anārthas* impede the divine vision, both for ourselves as well as for others.

But what can we do now? We must depend on you, represent you, and somehow show others the way to your lotus feet. It is your very great mercy on us that you have allowed us to remain in your movement. All we want is your transcendental and godly association—Kṛṣṇa is already there with you. If only we become pure and empowered, then our presently faltering stance would be rectified and strengthened, our service increased and purified. Yes, “hard times” may have befallen us. But let us become transcendental and remain fixed on your order. Beyond the required sixteen rounds a day and four regulations, we beg you to let us offer you *increased* uttering of the *mahā-mantra, increased* hearing from you through your books, *increased* preaching, and *increased* devotion in every little thing we do in the fleeting hours, days, weeks, months, and few remaining years of this lifetime.

We want to live in anticipation of going back to home, back to Godhead. We want to go there, for although every square inch of every sphere is Kṛṣṇa’s *dhāma,* still His own home is the topmost planet, *param-dhāma.* But the most compelling reason for returning to the eternal abode is that we will see you there, face to face. We will again get your personal *darśana,* and you will engage us in serving your lotus feet. And Kṛṣṇa will already be there, bestowing His cooling glance and smiling mercifully.

Please forgive the fault in this offering of our begging so many things from you. This is the glorious day of your divine appearance in this world, and we should thus be making presentations to you. And even now, we will make one more request: Kindly help us to just finish up all material business, purify us, and drag us to your lotus feet, back to Kṛṣṇa. And then our very breath, our every word, our every act will all be nothing other than offerings to our eternal spiritual master, our father, our grandfather, Your Divine Grace.

Please accept our inadequate homage. Chastise us as you see fit, but don’t kick us away from your lotus feet.

*Your eternal servants at the Puerto Rico Yatra,*
